## Snapshot
World's largest mobile-SoC vendor by units and a fast-emerging custom-ASIC house. FY25 revenue TWD 596bn (+12.3% y/y, >USD 19bn record), GM 47.5%, OpM 17.4%, EPS TWD 66.16 (Q4 FY25, 2026-02-04). Three reporting segments: **Mobile Phone** (Dimensity SoCs; 49% of Q1 FY26 rev), **Smart Edge Platforms** (connectivity/Wi-Fi/5G modem, compute, auto, edge-AI + the data-center ASIC business; 46%), and **Power IC** (5%) (Q1 FY26, 2026-04-30). The structural story is the pivot from a cyclical handset chipmaker to a data-center ASIC supplier: per JPM's Gopal Chatterjee, data center is ~30% of revenue in 2027 and could be **~60% in 2028** as TPU ramps, "essentially becoming a data center company from nothing in two to two-and-a-half years" (Gopal/Samik, 2026-06-10).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Dimensity mobile SoCs (~49% rev) · 2) Smart Edge / connectivity (Wi-Fi 7/8, 5G/NTN modem, edge-AI; ~46%) · 3) Custom data-center ASIC (Google TPU co-design, growth leg) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Smartphone OEMs (incl. China) + hyperscaler ASIC (Google TPU — "pretty much the only DC project," extreme single-customer concentration); some auto |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC foundry (N3/N2, CoWoS-L/EMIB), Arm IP (CSS royalty dispute), HBM (no margin on ASIC), substrates/packaging |

## Position in the value chain
MediaTek is fabless: it designs SoCs and ASICs, outsources all manufacturing to **TSMC** (a top-3 N3 customer; large N3/N2 allocation), and sells to smartphone OEMs, hyperscalers (Google TPU) and auto. The differentiated leg is **custom-ASIC optionality** — MediaTek is the co-design ("semi-COT") partner on Google's TPU (V8 "Zerafish"/8T today, primary candidate for V9 in 2028), which converts a commodity mobile-chip franchise into a data-center growth story (Gopal/Samik, 2026-06-10). MediaTek is one of TSMC's recognised ASIC design-service partners (alongside Broadcom, Marvell, Alchip, GUC, Socionext), positioned as a **large product company** that cross-leverages SerDes/connectivity/compute IP for full-stack design (Robert Hsu/Gokul, JPM ASIC design-services teach-in).

- 🔴 **THE CONTEXT THAT MAKES THE 20% MORE INTERESTING THAN IT LOOKS: JPM's AVGO callback discloses that Google's TPU roadmap has BIFURCATED — Google's in-house "CLT" team takes TRAINING chips while Broadcom keeps the more complex INFERENCING parts, and Broadcom's share of TOTAL TPU units runs ~66% in FY27 rising to ~75-80% in FY28** (JPM, 2026-09-03). ➤ **So there are now at least three parties inside Google's accelerator supply — Broadcom, MediaTek and Google's own CLT team — and the ~66% FY27 figure implies the non-Broadcom share is materially larger next year than the 20% MediaTek target alone would suggest.**

| **BofA** | Haas Liu | **Buy** | **5,230** (23x 2H27-1H28E PE) | ASIC sales **US$2bn '26 → US$48bn '28**; TAM expands **5-10x** on hardware/service value-add; 28x/10x '27/'28E PE on **85%/130% sales/EPS CAGR '26-28E** |
| **GS** | Evelyn Yu / James Schneider | **Buy** | *(not carried in the email as delivered)* | *"Limited near-term earnings contribution given that NVLink integration is only beginning and no revenue timeline was disclosed"* — but flags **custom HBM base-die design as "an UNDER-APPRECIATED potential new business opportunity to extend its content into the memory subsystem"** |

- 🔴 **THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT QUALIFIER, AND IT COMES FROM MANAGEMENT RATHER THAN THE BROKERS: *"Although MediaTek **did NOT change its 2027 ASIC TAM and market share guidance** given this extensive partnership and **said it would take time**"* — while *"MediaTek during the interview on August 31 indicated it **is indeed engaging with some customers**"* (MS · Charlie Chan). **GS says the same: *"limited near-term earnings contribution… no revenue timeline was disclosed."*** ➤ **Every PT above is being raised or defended on a guidance MediaTek itself declined to raise. That is the gap to hold in mind: the strategic validation is real and dated; the numbers behind it are not yet company-sanctioned.**
- **THE STANDING GUIDANCE THIS IS MEASURED AGAINST (MS desk · Tom Wigg, 2026-09-01): *"MediaTek management had previously indicated roughly **$2B of AI-chip business in 2026** and a goal of **~15% share of an $80B addressable market next year**."*** ➤ **UBS's high-20% 2027 die-unit share and JPM's *"majority unit share in TPUv8"* are both well ABOVE management's own ~15% share goal. The Street is not marking to the guide.**
- **JENSEN HUANG, ASKED THE CIRCULARITY QUESTION DIRECTLY: *"This is not circular because obviously they do their own business and we do our own business"*** (Bloomberg interview with both CEOs, 2026-08-31, via MS desk). ⚠️ **Logged because the circularity objection is now the standing bear on every NVDA-adjacent investment on this wiki; this is the company's own answer, not an analyst's.**
- 🔴 **THE POSITIONING RE-RATE, AND IT IS THE MOST SPECIFIC STRUCTURAL CLAIM MADE ALL WEEK (@mingchikuo, corpus, 2026-09-01): *"MediaTek's latest announcement… **repeatedly uses the term 'rack-scale.' This is the key to understanding the announcement**, and it directly validates the central conclusion of my research from more than two months ago: **MediaTek has upgraded the strategic positioning of its AI business from 'IC / ASIC design' to 'SYSTEM-LEVEL DESIGN.'**"*** ✅ **CORROBORATED INDEPENDENTLY BY BERNSTEIN THE SAME MORNING (Mark Li, via Tyler Seidman): the deal *"positions MediaTek as it enables customers to scale from silicon to rack and is **particularly useful for small/early ASIC customers**… it's possible to see **chassis/rack/sub-system becoming part of MediaTek's revenue**, or more likely MediaTek's revenue is still mostly from ASIC services, but **the reference design will help them win projects, and/or enjoy higher margins**."* And on the memory leg: *"**while Google will source HBM for their projects with MediaTek, MediaTek might help OTHER customers secure memories & other supplies.**"* ➤ **That last clause is the boundary of the "$14bn net cash locks up HBM/substrate" thesis: it does NOT apply to the Google programme, which sources its own HBM. The component-procurement value-add is a tool for winning the SECOND and THIRD customers, not for defending the first.**
- **WHAT THE MONEY IS ACTUALLY FOR — A THIRD, MORE SPECIFIC READING (@jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-31): *"My guess is that MediaTek may be raising this kind of funding to **make upfront payments for the SUBSTRATES used in EMIB**. Remember, **TSMC said substrates are currently the BIGGEST BOTTLENECK AFTER MEMORY**. And compared with memory, substrate capacity is relatively easier to expand."*** ⚠️ **Speculation, flagged as such (*"my guess"*) — but it is the only reading that connects the CB to this page's own 08-27 EMIB thread, and it is consistent with MS's *"ABF substrate procurement and wafer foundry capacity prepayment."***
- **NVLINK FUSION — THE SCOPE LIMIT THAT MATTERS, FROM THE ONE HOUSE WILLING TO STATE IT (JPM · Gokul Hariharan): *"While **NVLink Fusion is unlikely to be used in TPU Super Pods by GOOG**, we believe this strengthens MediaTek's hand when it comes to securing new ASIC wins, **especially newer hyperscalers or AI Labs**. We believe the NVLink Fusion partnership **remains OPTIONAL for MediaTek's ASIC clients**, enabling them to pursue a 'semi-custom' rack design that integrates their own XPU into NVLink/rack solutions."*** ➤ **So the NVLink leg is a customer-ACQUISITION tool aimed at tier-2 buyers, explicitly not a Google-programme upgrade. That materially narrows what the deal does for the 2027-28 numbers and matches management's refusal to raise the TAM guide.**
- **SIZING THE INVESTMENT FROM NVIDIA'S SIDE (JPM · Gokul): *"NVDA has been investing selectively in companies to enhance its ecosystem ([[MRVL]], [[INTC]], [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[GLW]], [[SNPS]], etc.), and this investment is **the SECOND-BIGGEST in a semiconductor company after Intel and the LARGEST OUTSIDE THE US**."*** **Dilution: *"< 2% equity dilution at the conversion price"* (JPM) / *"1.67% if the ECB is fully converted by 2031"* (BofA).**
- **THE TAPE: MediaTek limit-up on 08-31 (Jefferies Asia · Conor O'Mara: *"Mediatek limit up on the announcement with Nvidia **even though there isn't anything actually new in terms of the projects they are working on**"*), then **+10% on 09-01** (CNBC: *"Qualcomm rival MediaTek jumps 10% after $3.5 billion Nvidia AI chip deal"*). Price NT$4,315 at JPM's 09-01 note vs NT$3,925 at BofA's 08-31 note.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE DISSENTING VOICE, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT READ THE PRESS RELEASE MOST CAREFULLY (Jefferies Asia · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-31): *"Mediatek **was already one of the main participants in the NVLink Fusion strategy** of Nvidia when this idea was announced by Jensen at **Computex 2025**. Here is the **May 2025 press release from Mediatek confirming this**. So the announcement today that Mediatek will adopt NVLink Fusion **is a bit puzzling to me**… **All 3 parts of the actual announcement today are EXISTING PROJECTS the 2 companies are working on.**"* On the strategic read: *"Forbes thinks this helps Nvidia benefit even if it loses share to custom chips over time by having a stake in one of the main beneficiaries of this trend **but not at a $3.5bn stake versus its $5.3trn market cap it won't**. But this is a good endorsement of Mediatek technology from Nvidia and gives them some more funds to accelerate product development."*** ➤ **Hold this against the four bullish PTs: the most sceptical read is that the CB is the only new fact and the partnership is a re-announcement. That is testable against the May-2025 MediaTek release.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-31 — [[NVDA]] JUST PUT US$3.5bn INTO MEDIATEK, AND [[GOOG]] CAME IN BEHIND IT. THIS IS THE SINGLE LARGEST CHANGE TO THIS PAGE'S CAPITAL STRUCTURE AND COMPETITIVE FRAMING SINCE THE TPU WIN ITSELF.**
- 🔴 **THE MECHANICS: *"Mediatek completed **US$3.9bn ECB issuance**, with conversion price set at **NT$4,513.75 (15% premium to Aug 31st closing)** and **US$3.5bn subscribed by Nvidia, followed by Alphabet and institutional investors**. The shareholder **dilution is limited at 1.67%** if the ECB is fully converted by 2031."*** ➜ **NVIDIA takes ~90% of a US$3.9bn convertible in the company that is displacing [[AVGO]] generation-by-generation at Google, and Alphabet — the customer — subscribes alongside it.** (BofA · Haas Liu, Buy, PO NT$5,230, px NT$3,925, 2026-08-31 · confirmed independently by Bloomberg via MS TMT desk the same morning.)
- 🔴 **WHAT THE MONEY IS FOR, AND IT IS THE MOST OPERATIONALLY USEFUL PART: *"We estimate the ECB proceeds will boost its **net cash to ~US$14bn**, allowing it to **lock in the components where the supply is tight through long term agreements (e.g. HBM and substrate) for its ASIC customers requiring the service.**"*** ➜ **This page has carried **substrate as the primary constraint on TPUv8 shipment upside** and EMIB-T substrate yield (~60%) as its #1 risk. A US$14bn net-cash position exists to pre-buy exactly those two inputs. It converts a supply risk into a balance-sheet question — which MediaTek has now answered.**
- 🔴 **THE STRATEGIC READ, AND THE TWO HOUSES SPLIT ON MOTIVE.** **BofA (constructive):** *"Nvidia's direct investment demonstrates its confidence in Mediatek's capability in AI infrastructure **integrating the hyperscalers'/enterprises' custom XPUs to Nvidia's rack-scale system through NVLink Fusion ecosystem in chiplets, chip-to-chip interconnect and custom HBM**… the partnership allows seamless connection between chipsets and components at lower system design complexity and faster time to market for **semi-custom AI factories**."* Paired with NVIDIA's own release the same morning — *"NVIDIA and MediaTek Deepen Long-Standing Partnership to Build AI Edge to Cloud Computing Platforms"* (NVIDIA Newsroom, 2026-08-31). **MS TMT desk (transactional):** *"There's probably a little bit of **'the enemy of my enemy is my friend'** re AVGO here."* ⚠️ **Both readings retained. They are not mutually exclusive: NVLink Fusion + NVHBM give NVIDIA a route into the custom-XPU racks it does not build, and MediaTek is the integrator on the largest of those programmes.**
- **CONTEXT THAT MAKES THIS LESS OF AN OUTLIER THAN IT LOOKS: NVIDIA has recently taken stakes in **[[MRVL]], [[LITE]], [[COHR]], [[SNPS]], [[CRWV]], NOK and [[NBIS]]** (MS TMT desk, 2026-08-31), and separately holds **~$30bn of [[INTC]] and ~$21bn of [[SPCX]]** (@StockMKTNewz, corpus, 2026-08-28). ➜ **The MediaTek ECB is the largest single item in a systematic programme of taking equity across its own supply chain and its own competitors' supply chain. See [themes/custom-silicon-asic](themes/custom-silicon-asic.md).**
- ⚠️ **NOT YET ANSWERED, AND IT IS THE OBVIOUS QUESTION: whether NVIDIA's stake carries ANY governance, information or roadmap rights over the Google TPU programme MediaTek integrates. Neither the BofA note nor the NVIDIA release addresses it. Do not assume it is passive and do not assume it is not.**
- **BofA's numbers on the back of it, unchanged in structure but re-anchored: ASIC sales ramping from **US$2bn in '26 to US$48bn in '28**, *"led by TPUs supplemented by **xAI's accelerator chipset**"*, with optionality to *"expand its addressable market to components and hardware which carries **5-10x ASPs vs standalone chipsets**"* by taking the design/sourcing/system-integration role for **2nd-tier hyperscalers and enterprises**. Buy, **PO NT$5,230 on 23x 2H27-1H28E P/E**; shares at **28x/10x '27/'28E P/E** on **'26-28E sales/earnings CAGR of 85%/130%**.** ⚠️ **The "xAI accelerator chipset" attribution is BofA's; this page's standing mark on the second customer is *"xAI or SpaceX, zero disclosure and zero spec"* (Fubon 08-03). BofA naming xAI is a third-party attribution, NOT a confirmation — the second-customer row in the Sinal table is unchanged.**

- 🔴 **DIGITIMES: *"**MediaTek's EMIB solution has met Google's ORIGINAL REQUIREMENTS FOR TECHNOLOGY, YIELD, COST, AND PRODUCTION CAPACITY**."* And the tell: *"While **MediaTek still considers [[TSM]] its PRIMARY FOUNDRY PARTNER and tries to MINIMIZE DISCUSSION of alternative suppliers**, several **senior executives on its U.S.-based ASIC team are FORMER [[INTC]] EMPLOYEES**"* (via @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-27).**
- 🔴 **CORROBORATED FROM THE PACKAGING SIDE THE SAME DAY: *"**Intel EMIB advanced packaging has become a confirmed rival to TSMC with strong yields and satisfied customers**, media report, citing unnamed supply chain sources, who say **Intel's work with clients Google and MediaTek has been so smooth it indicates that costs and production yields are meeting targets.** And with TSMC CoWoS capacity still tight, **clients are likely to deepen work with Intel**"* (@dnystedt, corpus, 2026-08-27, ~84k views). ⚠️ *Both are press/supply-chain relays citing unnamed sources; neither is company-confirmed, and MediaTek is described as actively downplaying it.*

A second, yield-proven packaging source that satisfies Google's own qualification bar removes that gate — and the ex-Intel staffing explains how the option was built. **It is simultaneously a de-risking of MediaTek's ASIC roadmap and a challenge to CoWoS pricing power.** See [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [[INTC]], [[TSM]], [[GOOG]].**
- ⚠️ **THE COUNTERVAILING ITEM ON THE SAME PROGRAMME FAMILY, AND IT CUTS AT VALUE CAPTURE RATHER THAN ACCESS: *"market is focused on **GOOGL TPU likely adopting COT 'CUSTOMER-OWNED TOOLING' for v10** (Trainium 3 already starting this)… **market will not like to see hyperscalers increasing their own value-add in the chip design process**"* (MS Asia · Amerian, 2026-08-26). ➤ **Winning the packaging qualification while the customer insources the design flow is a volume win with a thinner margin. Both items are about the same customer and the same generation.**

We expect a decision in the coming months."** JPM is **OW on MediaTek, Alchip and GUC, with MediaTek and Alchip its top picks** (JPM, *Asia Tech Tour: Key takeaways*, 2026-08-25).

Jefferies adds the memory leg: *"For its MediaTek TPUs, [[GOOG]] is going to **buy the HBM directly**"* (Jefferies Asia TMT sales, 2026-08-25). ➤ **Volume up, scope down — the open question is whether the ASP uptick per chip outruns the content being pulled in-house.**

- **The trade-press framing:** *"MediaTek wins Google TPU deal on three key strengths"*, inside a piece expecting **Taiwan IC-design revenue +10% in 2026 driven mainly by companies in AI and AI-ASIC projects such as MediaTek and Global Unichip** (DIGITIMES Daily Picks, 2026-08-24). A companion piece: *"MediaTek, GUC, Alchip escalate funding war as global ASIC race demands deep capital."*

Expect Humufish tape-out in around Nov-2026 with a not too high passing rate."* The same post **maintains a positive view on MTK** alongside [[MRVL]] and [[INTC]], and separately **cut [[AVGO]]'s Sunfish volume for 2026/27 on a packaging re-design**, setting **460k CoWoS (ASIC) wafers in 2027** (@sssjeffpu, "ASIC Warp-Up", 2026-08-24 — **independent handle, no rating or PT**). ⚠️ **Two caveats carried: the "1H26" window phrasing is ambiguous on year and is reproduced as written; and the author's own "not too high passing rate" on the Humufish tape-out is a material execution qualifier, not a throwaway.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-20 — THE BIGGEST CHANGE TO THIS PAGE SINCE THE 07-31 GUIDE RAISE, AND IT IS A SHARE-OF-PROGRAMME UPGRADE RATHER THAN A VOLUME UPGRADE: FOR THE FIRST TIME MEDIATEK HAS TAKEN UP ITS VOLUME FOR THE GOOGLE TPU PROJECT *AS A WHOLE* AND PUT IT AT "MORE THAN 50%" — AGAINST A CONSENSUS THAT HAD IT WINNING ONLY *A SOCKET*. AND THE V10 ROLE IS NOW NAMED: MEDIATEK DOES THE INTEGRATION ON A "TRIPLET" ARCHITECTURE IT HAS BEEN WORKING ON WITH GOOGLE SINCE 2H LAST YEAR.** (BofA / BAML **Asia semis + components conference call, 2026-08-20** — a broker-hosted call with BofA's own analysts relaying tech-tour and earnings-season feedback; speakers: host "Robert", semis "Haas", components "Doris". ⚠️ **ASR transcript with spelling noise — "L-Chip" = Alchip, "Mediatek"/"MediaTek" both appear. Attributed to the BofA Asia tech team, 2026-08-20; no MTK rating or PT in the call.**) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_20_baml_call.html)
- 🔴 **THE QUOTE, AND BofA FLAGS IT AS THE THING THAT MOVED INVESTORS ON THE CALL:** *"this is the first time MediaTek talked about or took up their volume for Google TPU project as a whole, no matter it is training — will be more than 50%."* **BofA's own framing of why it matters: the consensus before this comment was that MediaTek had won only *a socket*.** ➜ **So the news is not "more units", it is "more of the programme" — a step from one-socket supplier to majority-of-programme supplier.** ⚠️ **TWO CAVEATS ON THE QUOTE AS PRINTED, both kept rather than resolved: (1) the clause "no matter it is training —" is ASR-TRUNCATED (the natural completion is "or inference," but the transcript does not say it, so do not assume the >50% spans both workloads); (2) the sentence does not state the BASE of the >50% — units, revenue or programmes — and the surrounding context ("took up their volume") reads as VOLUME.**
- 🔴⚠️ **BASIS DISCIPLINE, BECAUSE THIS PAGE NOW CARRIES FOUR "MEDIATEK SHARE" NUMBERS AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME MEASUREMENT. DO NOT NET, AVERAGE OR SUBSTITUTE THEM.** ① **">50%" = share of the GOOGLE TPU PROGRAMME, volume basis** (BofA Asia tech team, 2026-08-20). ② **"20-30%" = VALUE share of a $400-600bn+ five-year Google TPU-SYSTEM TAM, on cost-optimised variants** (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-19, block below). ③ **"15-20%" = share of a US$80bn 2027 ACCELERATOR-ONLY SAM (ex-HBM, ex-switch), management's own guide** (Q2'26 briefing, 2026-07-31). ④ **"~20%" = revenue share in TPU programmes** (MS · Joe Moore, via DB 2026-08-11). ➜ **The reconciliation, and it is arithmetically coherent rather than contradictory: MediaTek's franchise is explicitly the COST-OPTIMISED variant, i.e. a lower ASP per unit. A majority of the UNITS at a below-average ASP is exactly what produces a 20-30% share of VALUE. The two BofA marks one day apart are therefore not a house revision — they are the US desk (Arya) measuring value and the Asia desk measuring volume.** ⚠️ **The corollary is the one to act on: any "MediaTek share of Google" number quoted on this page without its basis (programme-volume / system-value / accelerator-SAM) is unusable.**
- 🔴 **THE VOLUME MARKS UNDERNEATH IT — AND THEY GOT A SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT CROSS-CHECK FROM A SECOND HOUSE.** BofA: **Google TPU volume ~4-4.5 MILLION UNITS THIS YEAR → 8-9 MILLION NEXT YEAR**, sourced explicitly as *"Mediatek's direct feedback from Google"* — i.e. this is MTK relaying its customer, not BofA modelling. **J.P. Morgan's Asia hardware call the SAME DAY (2026-08-20, Taiwan analyst rendered "Ivor", hosted by "Joel") independently has ~4-4.5mn on the "very upstream" measure but only ~3.5mn at the MODULE level, and for next year "the class number suggest basically double."** ➜ **Two houses, two independent channel checks, one day, agreeing on both the base and the doubling — a materially stronger read than either alone. ⚠️ BUT JPM MAKES A MEASUREMENT DISTINCTION BofA DOES NOT: upstream/TSMC-level vs module-level volumes differ by ~1mn units (4-4.5mn vs 3.5mn). Every TPU unit number on this page must carry which level of the chain it is measured at or it is not comparable — see [[GOOG]] for the full cross-broker write-up.** 🔴 **AND THE OPEN QUESTION THIS CREATES FOR MEDIATEK SPECIFICALLY, WHICH THIS PAGE CANNOT CLOSE FROM EITHER SOURCE: if MTK is ">50%" of the programme and the programme is 8-9mn units in 2027, MTK's implied 2027 TPU units are ~4-4.5mn+ — ABOVE Fubon's 3.5-4mn 2027 mark already on this page (08-03 primary). The two may not be measuring the same thing (Fubon's figure is logged in a MediaTek-ASIC context; BofA's is the whole Google programme), so the gap is flagged as a BASIS question, not scored as an upgrade or a contradiction.**
- 🔴 **TPU V10 — THE ROLE IS NOW ARCHITECTURAL AND IT IS THE MOST DEFENSIBLE VERSION OF MEDIATEK'S POSITION YET PUBLISHED: MediaTek has been working with Google SINCE 2H LAST YEAR on a "TRIPLET" architecture for the 10th-generation TPU. Within the triplet there are multiple dies/tiles — IO TILES, COMPUTING TILES and HBM — and *Google could use multiple vendors to design each tile*, BUT MEDIATEK DOES THE INTEGRATION: die-to-die stacking IPs plus laying out the 2.5D PACKAGING for the triplet.** BofA: *"things are trending pretty nicely so far."* ✅ **THIS CORROBORATES, ALMOST WORD FOR WORD, THE JPM CHECK ALREADY ON THIS PAGE — "only compute-die back-end may go to GUC/Alchip" (Gokul Hariharan, 08-02, in the Sinal table's v10 row). Two houses, independently, now describe the same structure: tiles can be multi-sourced, the INTEGRATOR seat is MediaTek's.** ➜ **Why that is the better claim to own: an integrator holding the die-to-die stacking IP and the 2.5D package layout is harder to displace tile-by-tile than a tile vendor is, and it is the seat that scales with the number of tiles rather than with any single die. It also explains how ">50% of the programme" and "compute-die back-end could go elsewhere" can both be true.**
- ⚠️ **THE COMPETITIVE DEBATE BofA IS ANSWERING, PLUS A DATE TENSION AGAINST THIS PAGE THAT IS PROBABLY NOT A CONFLICT.** The question BofA flags from the tech tour is **whether [[AMD]], [[AVGO]], Alchip or GUC penetrate the TPU supply chain "~two years out," i.e. at TPU V10** — MediaTek's integration feedback above is offered as the counter-evidence. ⚠️ **"~two years out" from Aug-2026 reads as ~2028, whereas this page carries V10 SHIPPING IN 2029 (DB · Robert Sanders, 2026-08-11: "TPU v10 family, shipping in 2029"; JPM: v10 in 2029; Fubon: v10 migrates to 400G SerDes). Most likely these are different clocks — the vendor-SELECTION / design-win window (~2028) versus the SHIPMENT year (2029) — which is consistent with 400G/448G SerDes IP being ready in 2H27. The 2029 shipment date on this page therefore STANDS UNCHANGED; the ~two-year framing is logged as the competitive-decision window, not a re-dating.** ✅ **Also note the direction of travel: DIGITIMES put [[AMD]] into the v10 pool on 08-19 (block below) and BofA now confirms AMD/AVGO/Alchip/GUC penetration at v10 is the live investor debate — so the fourth-claimant risk on this page is corroborated as a DEBATE, while the integration feedback is the first specific evidence against it.**
- **Where MediaTek sits in the design-service preference stack, and it is a relative-positioning datapoint rather than a rating:** BofA reports investors like **MediaTek, GUC and Alchip**, with **Alchip LEAST PREFERRED on its communication track record**, and BofA itself **prefers AI over non-AI (vs [[UMC]])**. ➜ **Read against the JPM ASIC pecking order already on this page (08-16, in the Alchip note): the two houses agree MediaTek is in the top tier and that Alchip's issue is credibility rather than capability.**
- 🔴 **THE NEAR-TERM TELL, AND IT IS THE CHEAPEST CHECK ON THIS ENTIRE PAGE: Taiwan companies must report monthly sales by the 10th of the following month, so MEDIATEK'S MONTHLY REVENUE IN LATE SEPTEMBER / EARLY OCTOBER IS THE VERIFICATION OF WHETHER THE TPU V8 RAMP IS ON SCHEDULE** (BofA Asia tech team, 2026-08-20). ➜ **A dated, public, un-gameable falsifier for the 2026 "more than US$2bn ASIC revenue, production starting Q4'26" guide — added to Catalysts.**
- **Macro/mix context from the same call, which is the demand backdrop for all of the above:** hyperscaler capex **still trending up with the growth rate moderating**, *despite news that [[GOOG]] and [[META]] might slow investment or cut frontier-model headcount*; and **a shift toward ASICs versus GPUs in 2H26 and 2027**, because hyperscalers are spending more cost-effectively — **memory component cost is rising while they can no longer fully fund capex out of FCF/cash**, pushing them toward external capital and toward monetising existing models rather than pouring more into frontier training. ➜ **The ASIC-over-GPU mix shift is the macro leg of MediaTek's own story, and BofA is sourcing it to hyperscaler COST pressure rather than to ASIC performance — which is the argument that favours the cost-optimised variant tier MediaTek occupies.**

**🆕 2026-08-19 — MEDIATEK'S TPU ROLE GETS SIZED INSIDE A NEW FOUR-SUPPLIER MAP: 20-30% value share of Google's TPU-system TAM on COST-OPTIMISED variants.** (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-19, written off the [[MRVL]]/[[GOOG]] warrant deal.)
- **The frame:** consensus has **[[GOOG]] capex $1.5-2.0tn+ over CY27-31**, with **~25-30% on TPU systems = a $400-600bn+ TAM**. Split: **[[AVGO]] ~55-60%+ (~$250-350bn+)** on the core compute die, HBM/packaging integration, SerDes and networking, locked by an LTA through CY31; **[[MRVL]] 10-20% (~$50-100bn+)** on XPU-attach; **MediaTek 20-30% on cost-optimised variants**.
- 🔴 **What is new here is not the role but the arithmetic: 20-30% of a $400-600bn+ TAM is a materially larger number than the ~$10bn CY27 "TPU 8t" figure this page has carried from Barclays. The two are not contradictory — one is a single node in one year, the other is a five-year value-share band — but the gap is large enough that the page should not treat them as the same claim.**
- ⚠️ **Also on 08-19, and a genuine competitive read: [[AMD]] is now reported (DIGITIMES) to be working with Google on v10 TPU technology. The DIGITIMES headline explicitly says this happens "without sidelining Broadcom, MediaTek" — but a fourth supplier in the pool is a fourth claimant on the cost-optimised tier, which is exactly where MediaTek sits.**

**🆕 2026-08-16 — A THIRD HOUSE PUTS MTK'S TPU AT THE FRONT OF THE [[INTC]] EMIB-T QUEUE — "THE FIRST HIGH-VOLUME EMIB-T CUSTOMER IN 2028E," POTENTIALLY FOR TPUv9 — AND ATTACHES A YIELD NUMBER (>97%) WHOSE MEASUREMENT LEVEL IS NOT STATED. ⚠️ ROUTER MISS: this page was not proposed for the note; recovered by hand.**
- **As printed: "EMIB-T is seeing good yields >97% and real traction, with MediaTek potentially using it for TPUv9 — MTK's TPU as the FIRST HIGH-VOLUME EMIB-T CUSTOMER IN 2028E"** (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", 2026-08-16; the note states MTK is covered by Mizuho's **Kevin Wang in HK**, so this is the **US desk's** framing of an Asia-covered name — the same split that produced the 08-09 margin fork already on this page).
- ✅ **WHAT IT CONFIRMS RATHER THAN SUPERSEDES, AND THIS PAGE IS ALREADY AHEAD OF IT ON EVERY AXIS BUT ONE.** The **2028 high-volume date** matches Nomura's "high volume production in 2028," MS's mass-production-2028 read, and management's own **HVM early 2028** for TPU v9 Humufish — **so no packaging assumption on this page is superseded.** The **TPUv9-on-EMIB-T** link matches **UBS's own check** already logged here ("MediaTek may be among potential EMIB-T customers for its v9-gen TPU chip," 07-24) and **management's on-record "Yes this is true, we are more committed to EMIB-T"** (07-31). 🆕 **The one genuinely new element is the ORDERING claim — "the FIRST high-volume EMIB-T customer" — which makes MTK the anchor tenant of Intel's external packaging ramp rather than one of several. Held as a claim, not a fact: Mizuho gives no basis for the ranking, and the same wiki carries [[AVGO]] and [[META]] as EMIB-T evaluators (DIGITIMES, 08-03).**
- 🔴⚠️ **THE >97% YIELD FIGURE CANNOT BE USED ON THIS PAGE AS PRINTED, AND THE REASON IS THE PAGE'S OWN EXISTING DATA: Mizuho does not say WHICH LEVEL it is measured at, and this page has established that EMIB-T yield is level-dependent by a factor of ~1.6x.** Already here: **JPM (08-16) "~60% at SUBSTRATE level and ~90% at CHIP-ON-SUBSTRATE level, per our checks"**; **UBS (07-26) "packaging validation yields ~90-92%," substrate fabrication "possibly ~50% vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates"**; **Unimicron's own ramp target ~50% at substrate with Intel providing commercial protection (@IntelProMUltra, 08-01)**. And on [[INTC]]: **Fubon (07-08) has [[GOOG]] requiring 95%+ before production and calls that "very challenging" by mid-2027**; **DIGITIMES (08-03) has package yield >90% with "a 50% SUBSTRATE YIELD REMAINS THE BOTTLENECK."** ➤ **So: if >97% is the chip-on-substrate/package step it is an incremental improvement on the ~90-92% cluster and says NOTHING about the substrate bottleneck that every level-specified source calls binding — which is the number that gates MTK's v9 volume. If it is the substrate step it overturns four sources and would be the single most bullish datapoint on this page. Until the basis is disclosed, >97% must NOT be read as clearing Google's 95%+ gate, and the v9 packaging risk on this page stands unchanged.** All marks retained side by side.
- 🆕 **The reticle/output ladder, which is the part that bears on MTK's 2028 ASIC revenue rather than its packaging risk: "2028E CoPoS/EMIB-T adoption unlocks 8–12x reticle size (or a 5–10x increase in ASIC outs) versus today's CoWoS-L at 5.5x."** ➤ **Relevant here because this page's 2028 ASIC line rests on a ~2.8x v9-vs-v8 ASP step at 3.0mn units — a 5–10x increase in ASIC OUTS is a supply-side unlock that would support volume without requiring the ASP step to hold, i.e. it widens the paths to the 2028 number rather than confirming the current one.** ⚠️ **Do not net it against the CoWoS-allocation-driven volume raise already on the page (Fubon 08-03: 2027 TPU volume raised to 3.5–4mn on extra CoWoS allocation) — that is 2027 CoWoS supply, this is 2028 CoPoS/EMIB-T reticle. Different years, different platforms.** See [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md) and [[INTC]].
- **No MTK rating, PT, guide or estimate changes: Mizuho HK (Kevin Wang) rates MTK Buy with no PT in this note, and the note's PT table covers US names only.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — DEUTSCHE BANK PUTS A DOLLAR NUMBER ON THE RAISED ASIC GUIDE: 15–20% of the 2027 served market implies ~$14BN OF AI DATA-CENTER ASIC REVENUE IN 2027. Buy, TP TWD5,000 (price TWD4,020) (Deutsche Bank / Robert Sanders · Melissa Weathers, "MediaTek — Googly eyes", 2026-08-11).**
- **The arithmetic, verbatim:** *"MediaTek used its Q2 results to confirm that its expected share of its **served addressable market for AI ASICs in 2027 has risen from 10-15% to 15-20%**. **At the mid-point of the revised guide, this implies $14bn of AI data center ASIC revenue in 2027.**"* ➜ **The share raise itself is already on this page from the 07-31 briefing; the $14bn conversion is net-new and is the number to carry.**
- **🔴 DB's read on *why* the share went up is two-part and the second part is a competitive claim about [[AVGO]]:** *"This points to some **supply chain relief**, but also **increased allocation of [[GOOG]]'s TPU volumes** in our view, while **confidence is likely growing on 2028 partly in the face of THERMAL CHALLENGES AT BROADCOM WITH THEIR TPUv9 PART**."* ⚠️ **Single-broker claim, unconfirmed elsewhere in today's flow — flagged as a hypothesis, not a fact. But it is the first named technical reason on this page for MediaTek taking TPU share, rather than the generic "second-source" framing.**
- **The 2029 hook: "MediaTek also confirmed that development of 448G SerDes is tracking well, with that IP ready to ship in H2-27, and with our expectation that this will feature in the TPU v10 family, shipping in 2029."** ➜ **448G SerDes readiness in H2-27 is a checkable milestone and the cleanest forward evidence that MediaTek is being designed into TPU generations beyond v8/v9.**
- **🔴 CORROBORATION FROM A SECOND HOUSE, and it matches almost exactly: "MediaTek guided that it could achieve a 15-20% share of the ASIC market in 2027, broadly in line with Joe Moore's assumption that MediaTek could secure a 20% REVENUE SHARE IN TPU PROGRAMS. That said, MediaTek believes its revenue share could INCREASE FURTHER IN 2028 alongside the ramp-up of 2nm TPU products."** Same note adds a competitor at the design-service layer: *"**GUC** indicated at its recent analyst meeting that it is interested in providing **TPU compute die back-end design services** for future TPU generations"* (Morgan Stanley Asia / Charlie Chan, via MS TMT desk, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Two independent houses now have MediaTek at ~15–20% / ~20% of 2027 TPU programme value, with 2028 upside on 2nm. That is a consensus forming, not an outlier call.**
- **Volume context for the same programme, from MS's supply-chain work: total TPU shipments forecast at 3.7M units in 2026 and MORE THAN 7M in 2027; [[GOOG]] CPU shipments 1.5M in 2026 and 3M in 2027** (MS / Charlie Chan, 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **These are MS unit forecasts, not MediaTek disclosures — do not multiply them against MediaTek's share guide without reconciling content-per-unit; the two are on different bases.**

**Q2 FY26 — PRINTED 2026-07-31 (analyst briefing held overnight). The ASIC guide was raised on BOTH axes, and that is the whole event.** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT — the prior guidance on this page is superseded; the old values are preserved in `## Changelog`.**

**The raise, precisely:**
- **2027 AI-accelerator SAM: US$80bn** (from US$70-80bn), and **MediaTek's share target: 15-20%** (from 10-15%) → **implied 2027 ASIC revenue US$12-16bn, vs US$7-12bn on the prior guide** (MediaTek Q2'26 analyst briefing, 2026-07-31; per Jefferies · Conor O'Mara call notes, GS · Evelyn Yu, JPM · Gokul Hariharan, BofA · Haas Liu, all 2026-07-31). This lands **at the top of the range the bulls were underwriting** — Fubon's 15-20% call (07-30) and MS's expectation of a raise to US$12-15bn (Chan, 07-26) both hit.
- **2026 ASIC revenue: "more than US$2bn"** (from "US$2bn"), with production starting Q4'26.
- ⚠️ **BASIS GUARDRAIL, and it is material: the US$80bn SAM is ACCELERATOR-ONLY — it excludes HBM and excludes network switch.** Management confirmed on the call that **MediaTek does not buy the HBM; Google buys it directly** — and per Jefferies' checks, **"one of the key reasons Google is moving more to MTK is to save money by buying HBM directly"** (O'Mara, 2026-07-31). So MediaTek's ASIC revenue line is *structurally lower-dollar but higher-margin-optics* than a comparably-sized AVGO figure that may carry HBM pass-through. Never compare the two gross.
- **Second-generation accelerator (TPU v9 "Humufish"): high-volume production early 2028**, tape-out and design "on track"; **yield and reliability on track**. ⚠️ **This is a SLIP against sell-side models — JPM had late 2027** (Hariharan, 2026-07-31). Management's framing turns the slip into a share argument: **"the first and second ASIC will be in production at the same year, driving significant share gains."**
- **Packaging — the EMIB-T question moved decisively.** Asked directly whether it is more committed to Intel EMIB-T "like Unimicron," management answered: **"Yes this is true, we are more committed to EMIB-T."** Also confirmed it uses **both CoWoS and EMIB-T** for large-reticle 2nm ASICs, and declined to comment on a third generation. See [[INTC]] and [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md) — this is the strongest first-party EMIB-T endorsement logged anywhere on this wiki. ⚠️ Counterweight the same day: **Unimicron said Intel's EMIB-T is unlikely to reach full mass production before 2027**, citing customers switching from CoWoS for lower cost and better performance, and expecting them to stay on EMIB-T for three product generations (@jukan05, 2026-07-31).
- **IP roadmap — the genuine surprise. 400G SerDes ready by 2H27**, called "a huge surprise" by Fubon (Jefferies-Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-07-31) and timed for **TPU v10 in 2029** (JPM). Note the internal inconsistency in the sourcing: management's prepared remarks referenced **448G** SerDes development "progressing well, best in class" while the Q&A answer said **400G ready 2H27** — both figures are reported by different desks (BofA cites 448G; Fubon/JPM cite 400G); treat the generation label as unsettled. Also: **industry-leading D2D, co-packaged copper (CPC) today with CPO via TSMC COUPE beyond it**, and explicitly **"beyond 400G SerDes will need to move to optical."** Working on a **rack design with its own SerDes + D2D + 3.5D packaging, including NVLink Fusion connectivity.**
- **Financials — a beat on operating leverage, not revenue.** Revenue **NT$152.18bn, +2% q/q / +1% y/y** — high end of guidance, **+4% vs BBG consensus** but exactly in line with JPMe. **GM 46.2%, flat q/q** (vs consensus 46.1%, midpoint of guide); **OpM 15.0% (−32bp q/q); operating profit NT$22.87bn, +3-4% above JPMe/consensus on operating leverage; net profit NT$24.6bn (+8%/+10% vs JPMe/BBG); EPS NT$15.34** (BofA cites NT$15.28). Mobile **−15% q/q / −20% y/y = 40% of sales**; **Smart Edge +19% q/q / +26% y/y = 53% of sales** (share gains in computer connectivity and auto); Power IC +14% q/q, gained share in computing.
- **Q3'26 guide ABOVE consensus: NT$152.2-159.9bn** (vs consensus ~NT$152bn), **GM 44.5-47.5% (46% midpoint), flat**. Aiming for the **high end of the full-year revenue target range.** Mobile guided flat to −MSD q/q; Smart Edge +mid-to-high single digit q/q; Power flat. **Board approved US$5bn of incremental financing.**
- **Handset market: management expects the market to decline 15% in 2026**, with customers barbelling to flagship and entry. Flagship: new **2nm SoC for agentic computation** with better cost efficiency, raising presence in 2027. **RTX Spark launches for the holidays**, bolstering computer sales.
- **Pricing is defensive, not accretive — worth being precise about:** asked why GM guidance is flat despite price hikes, management said **"pass through is for the whole supply chain… we are not increasing price to increase GM, it's to share the price pressure with customers, we are just looking to maintain GM."** So the wafer/packaging/substrate cost inflation is being passed on to hold margin flat, not expand it.
- **Sell-side reaction — uniformly positive, PTs raised or held, and the consensus-revision call is explicit:** JPM OW, PT NT$5,300 held, and says **"consensus FY27 EPS could be revised up by 5-10%"** on ASIC contribution plus operating leverage (Hariharan). **GS raised its 12m TP to NT$7,000 from NT$6,800** (25x 2H27-1H28E EPS, ~97% implied upside) — the high mark on the page after Macquarie (Yu). **BofA Buy, PO NT$5,230 held**, EPS unchanged at NT$68.61/NT$137.76/NT$347.25 for '26/'27/'28, stock at **25x/10x '27/'28E P/E vs an 8-25x historical range**, and now models cloud ASIC at **US$45.8bn in 2028E** — BofA also asserts MediaTek **"has started co-developing the AI accelerator ASIC with xAI, set for tape out in 4Q27,"** with **Meta and Toyota/Denso ADAS** as further optionality (Liu). **Fubon/Jefferies Buy, TP NT$6,000 held** (69% upside). Stock **NT$3,555** at the time of the notes.
- **Still unanswered: no confirmed second ASIC customer.** Management said only "we have several, we will provide details when we can." JPM expects confirmation **before end-2026** and believes **"it could be SpaceX"**; BofA asserts xAI as already underway. Treat as unconfirmed.

**Into the 2026-07-31 print (after the close, call 3pm) — the debate is ASIC share, not smartphones.** _(pre-print framing, retained: the 15-20% share call below was VINDICATED by the print above.)_ Fubon's Sherman Shang via Jefferies (Hubert King / Conor O'Mara, 2026-07-30) — the most aggressive TPU-supply-chain framing logged on this page:

- **"The real debate is ASIC mkt share, not sluggish smartphones. If Sherman is right on 15-20% ASIC share, MTK won't be valued as a smartphone stock much longer."**
- **AI ASIC TAM has expanded to US$70-80bn, with MTK targeting 10-15% share, implying a potential US$7-12bn ASIC revenue opportunity longer term** — and "MTK is confident in their TPU revs and **could raise its FY27 ASIC mkt share from 10-15% to 15-20%, which could imply US$10bn business impact**."
- **[[GOOG]] TPU is the variant view:** "Earlier expectations were for a relatively small side project, but Sherman has repeatedly highlighted that **Google's shipment targets have increased materially, with MTK potentially securing 1m+ TPU units and several billion dollars of future revenue**." And the 2028 roadmap: "Sherman now believes **Google's 2028 TPU roadmap is substantially larger than investors appreciate, with TPU shipments potentially reaching 12-15m units. Based on the V10 four-compute-die architecture, this could translate into 30-35m compute dies**, implying explosive growth across the TPU supply chain."
- **Numbers well above the Street:** "Sherman raised **FY27 EPS to NT$153.34, versus Bloomberg consensus of NT$128.12, a ~20% upside gap. More importantly, FY27 revenue was raised 26% versus prior forecasts** as ASIC assumptions increase."
- **Bear case is execution and CoWoS:** "Can they get enough TSMC CoWoS to monetize TPU orders" — though "Sherman's CoWoS report earlier in the week suggests Mediatek has got more CoWoS allocation," and **[[INTC]]'s EMIB-T could prove better-than-expected, which would help MTK hit their lofty targets.**
- **Second ASIC customer:** "Sherman does not expect an announcement this quarter, but still believes a **second major project may emerge later, potentially tied to xAI/[[SPCX]]**."
- **Cross-desk check:** "the recent MS report on MTK — they forecast **MTK US$15-20bn TPU rev opportunity under their FY27e US$100bn TAM case**."
- **Positioning:** "Stock price **-27% MTD**, but bouncing today"; margin loans came off from 12m shares to ~9m, "but I would rather it get closer to the 6.5m shs back in April before the rally."
- **The counterweight, from a first-party source.** An AlphaSense expert call with a **Principal Architect and CTO at a leading global cloud provider** (2026-07-30) ranks the ASIC partner stack as "**[[AVGO]] … the leading partner … with [[MRVL]] as a distant second and MediaTek focused on price competitiveness but lacking system-level capabilities**," and puts a ceiling on the whole category — custom ASICs capped at **~40-50% of training FLOPS by 2030-2035**, with scale and vertical integration as prerequisites. Worth holding against Fubon's numbers.

**Q1 FY26 (2026-04-30):** Revenue TWD 149.2bn, −0.7% q/q / −2.7% y/y, at the high end of guidance. GM 46.3% (+0.2pt q/q, −1.8pt y/y); OpM 15.3%; net income TWD 24.4bn; EPS TWD 15.17. Mobile −17% q/q / −15% y/y (49% of rev) as OEMs raised retail prices and skewed to higher-end on memory/BOM inflation; Smart Edge +23% q/q / +13% y/y (46%); Power IC 5% (Q1 FY26, 2026-04-30).

**Guidance / messages:**
- **Q2 FY26:** revenue TWD 140.2–149.2bn (flat to −6% q/q); GM **43% ±1.5pt** (step-down from 46.3% — mix/no one-off); opex ratio 31% ±2pt (Q1 FY26, 2026-04-30).
- **FY26 revenue:** mid- to high single-digit USD growth y/y; smartphone to decline significantly on memory/BOM cost, non-smartphone offsetting (Q4 FY25, 2026-02-04).
- **Data-center ASIC:** revenue to **exceed USD 1bn in FY26**, with **~USD 2bn in Q4 FY26 alone**, scaling to **multiple billions in 2027**; targeting **10–15% share** of a cloud-ASIC TAM put at **USD 70–80bn in 2027** (raised from "USD 50bn by 2028") (Q1 FY26 / Q3 FY25). A **second ASIC project** is in design, MP targeted end-2027 (Q1 FY26). As of Dec-2025 MediaTek reiterated the **USD 1bn FY26 TPU target with no push-out** and confirmed it had **passed key tape-out milestones** (shipment revenue, not NRE) (Jefferies, 2025-12-17).
- **Products:** first **2nm Dimensity flagship** SoC, phones by end-Q3 FY26; **3nm Dimensity Auto** agentic-AI cockpit; Wi-Fi 7/8, 5G + 5G satellite (NTN); USD 90m stake in Air Labs (CPO) (Q1 FY26).

## Debate / thesis

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — TWO J.P. MORGAN ANALYSTS PUBLICLY DISAGREE ABOUT WHO WINS GOOGLE'S TPU v9, IN THE SAME WEEKLY, AND THE DESK PRINTS "WE'LL HAVE TO AGREE TO DISAGREE."** (J.P. Morgan · **Gokul Hariharan** (AsiaPac TMT) vs **Harlan Sur** (US Semis), relayed in JPM Tech HW/Semis Weekly · Joshua Meyers, **2026-09-07**)
- 🔴 **Gokul's case:** MediaTek's opportunity in the **[[GOOG]] food chain "should continue to grow over the next 4~5 years, and growth is NOT BEING PRICED IN BEYOND 2028 because of concerns over competition from other ASIC vendors (namely GUC, Alchip & [[MRVL]])."** He sizes **MediaTek's TPU opportunity at an $80bn+ ANNUAL REVENUE RUN-RATE BY 2030**, and argues **MediaTek will be GOOGL's PRIMARY TPU v9 VENDOR "with a sharp jump in ASPs", as GOOGL pushes for more control over the design process.**
- 🔴 **Harlan's case, from the [[AVGO]] seat in the same weekly: "AVGO is already shipping v8i AHEAD of MediaTek's v8t."**
- ⚠️⚠️ **NEITHER ADOPTED, AND THE DISAGREEMENT IS THE POINT. These are not contradictory FACTS — v8i and v8t are different parts, and being second on v8t says nothing definitive about v9 — but they are contradictory READS of who holds the design relationship going forward. The $80bn-by-2030 run-rate is an unmodelled long-dated number with no methodology shown in the relay, and it is roughly a third of AVGO's own FY28 AI-semi framework, so it cannot be stacked on top of the AVGO numbers without double-counting the same TPU program.** ➤ **Falsifiable test recorded: the v9 award. Whichever way it lands settles a debate two analysts at the same house could not.**

**🆕 2026-09-07 — TWO MORE HOUSES LEAN THE SAME WAY ON MEDIATEK THIS WEEK, BOTH ON THE ASIC LEG.**
- **BofA (Haas Liu, SEMICON Taiwan takeaways, 2026-09-07): "we keep preference on the companies with higher exposure in CPU/ASIC/networking switches and CPO optionality and LIFTING PO AND ESTIMATES ON TSMC, ASE, All Ring, MEDIATEK."** ⚠️ The new PO and estimates are not quoted in the email summary; the full note was not retrieved.
- **BofA's ASIA 10 names MediaTek as one of two ideas to look for at its 9/21 Hong Kong Asia Pacific Conference — "MediaTek, where CUSTOM SILICON IS GOING MAINSTREAM"** (BofA TMT spec sales · Brian Fenske, 2026-09-07 — *sales commentary, not research*). The other is [[SAMSUNG]], on foundry turning profitable in 2H26.
- **MS (Shawn Kim, "Tech Bytes: Which AI Bottleneck To Invest In?", 2026-09-07, Industry View Attractive): MediaTek is named alongside GUC as MS's preferred ASIC expression** inside its AI-compute bucket.
- 🔭 **And a governance datapoint from Bernstein's most-read list this week: "MediaTek: NVIDIA & Google becoming CB HOLDERS; 'Reference design' to proliferate ASIC but co-existing with GPU?"** (Bernstein Tech spec sales · Tyler Seidman, weekly note list, 2026-09-07). ⚠️ **HEADLINE ONLY — the note was not retrieved and the convertible-bond claim is UNVERIFIED. Logged because if [[NVDA]] and [[GOOG]] are both on MediaTek's cap table via CBs, the "who does MediaTek actually work for" question changes shape.**

- **🆕🔴🔴 THE SHARPEST OPEN DISAGREEMENT ON THIS PAGE IS NOT JPM-vs-MS. IT IS MS-ASIA-vs-MS-US, AND BOTH SIDES PUBLISHED INSIDE 24 HOURS (2026-08-31).** **Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan (Taipei) — OW, TOP PICK, PT NT$5,588 (42% implied upside): *"We believe MediaTek remains the MAJOR COORDINATOR OF TPU v10 Icefish… TPU should account for 65% of 2028e revenue, and the revenue dollar may further grow to US$70bn in 2029e."*** Against **Morgan Stanley · Joseph Moore (New York), carried on this page and on [[AVGO]] from 08-30/31: *"Broadcom to retain roughly 80% of its longer term TPU opportunity"*, with MediaTek *"likely a merchant chip."*** ➜ ⚠️⚠️ **THE PAGE SHOULD STOP FILING THIS AS "JPM vs MS". The real map is GEOGRAPHIC: the two ASIA desks (JPM · Gokul Hariharan — *"pole position for TPUv10"*; MS · Charlie Chan — *"major coordinator of v10 Icefish"*) sit on one side, and the US desk covering the incumbent (MS · Joseph Moore) on the other. That is not a modelling dispute — the Asia desks are reading the Taiwan/Korea supply chain and the US desk is reading Broadcom's own disclosure, so they are looking at different evidence. Both marks stay on the page, unnetted, and the v10 award — which JPM dates *"in the next six months"* — adjudicates it.** ⚠️ **Basis guard still applies and is now doing a lot of work: MS Asia's 65%-of-2028e-revenue and US$70bn-2029e are MEDIATEK COMPANY revenue, JPM Asia's *"majority unit share"* is UNITS, and MS US's 80% is AVGO's share of TPU REVENUE. Three different denominators; do not difference them.**
- **🆕🔴 BofA PUTS THE BIGGEST 2028 ASIC NUMBER ON THIS WIKI ON THIS PAGE — US$48bn — AND ADDS A SECOND, LARGER LEG NOBODY ELSE IS MODELLING (BofA · Haas Liu, BUY, PO NT$5,230 unchanged, price NT$3,925, 2026-08-31).** *"Its ASIC sales could grow to **US$48bn in '28** led by Google/xAI chips"*, ramping *"from US$2bn in '26"* — **led by TPUs and supplemented by xAI's accelerator chipset.** ⚠️ **Put that against the models already on this page: JPM US$43.3bn 2028 (of which US$38.0bn is the v9 Humufish leg at a US$12,667 ASP), Fubon's 30-33% share ex-second-project, and the company's own US$12-16bn *2027* guide. BofA's US$48bn is the high mark and it is the FIRST house to underwrite the second customer INSIDE the base case rather than as optionality — this page has carried xAI/SpaceX as an unnamed, unquantified RFQ since 08-02.** 🔴 **AND THE NET-NEW ANALYTICAL LEG, which is in no other model here: *"The TAM could expand 5-10x on hardware service value add"* — MediaTek *"should have flexibility to expand its addressable market to COMPONENTS AND HARDWARE which carries **5-10x ASPs vs standalone chipsets** as it provides the value add in designing, sourcing and system integration for **2nd tier hyperscalers and enterprises**."* ➜ **This is the same move the page logged qualitatively on 08-01 as *"pre-validated system/rack design… one customer"* and on 08-02 as *"expanding its business model toward SUB-SYSTEM AND RACK-LEVEL design"* — BofA is the first to PRICE it, at a 5-10x ASP multiplier. It is also the direct commercial rationale for the ECB below: you cannot sell a rack you cannot source the HBM and substrate for.** **Valuation basis: PO on 23x 2H27-1H28E P/E; shares at 28x/10x '27/'28E P/E on '26-28E sales/earnings CAGRs of 85%/130%.**
- **🆕🔴 THE ECB IS A WORKING-CAPITAL WEAPON, NOT A CAPITAL RAISE — AND ALL THREE HOUSES AGREE ON WHERE THE MONEY GOES (2026-08-31).** **US$3.9bn ECB, US$3.5bn (90%) subscribed by [[NVDA]] followed by Alphabet and institutions; conversion NT$4,513.75 (15% premium to the 08-31 close); dilution 1.67% if fully converted by 2031; proceeds take net cash to ~US$14bn** (BofA · Haas Liu). **Use of proceeds: BofA — *"lock in the components where the supply is tight through LONG TERM AGREEMENTS (e.g. HBM and substrate) for its ASIC customers requiring the service"*; MS · Charlie Chan — *"key components (e.g., HBM and ABF substrate) procurement and **WAFER FOUNDRY CAPACITY PREPAYMENT**."*** ➜ 🔴 **The foundry-prepayment leg is net-new and is the more interesting half: it is MediaTek buying [[TSM]] capacity ahead of a v9/v10 ramp it has NOT yet been awarded — i.e. taking supply risk onto its own balance sheet to win the socket. Cross-check the substrate leg against the same weekend's supply data: Samsung Electro-Mechanics H1 utilisation 89% and LG Innotek 94%, both ramping AI substrate capacity, *"corroborates ABF bottleneck = Korean 2nd tier full means that Japan/Taiwan leaders are much tighter capacity-wise"* (@ParadisLabs, corpus, 2026-08-30). The scarcity BofA says the money is for is independently visible in second-tier utilisation.**
- **🆕⚠️⚠️ THE COUNTERWEIGHT NOBODY ELSE OFFERED, AND IT IS THE MOST USEFUL LINE OF THE DAY: JEFFERIES SAYS THE ANNOUNCEMENT IS MOSTLY NOT NEW (Jefferies Asia TMT · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-31).** *"MediaTek was ALREADY one of the main participants in the NVLink Fusion strategy of Nvidia when this idea was announced by Jensen at **Computex 2025**. Here is the **May 2025 press release from MediaTek confirming this**. So the announcement today that MediaTek will adopt NVLink Fusion is **a bit puzzling to me**… **All 3 parts of the actual announcement today are EXISTING PROJECTS the 2 companies are working on.**"* And on the "NVIDIA hedges its custom-silicon risk" thesis the rest of the Street ran with: *"Forbes thinks this helps Nvidia benefit even if it loses share to custom chips over time by having a stake in one of the main beneficiaries of this trend — **but not at a $3.5bn stake versus its $5.3trn market cap it wont.**"* ➜ **Two separate deflations and the page carries both: (1) the PARTNERSHIP is a re-announcement, so anything modelled off "new NVLink Fusion access" is double-counting a May-2025 fact; (2) the STAKE is ~0.066% of [[NVDA]]'s market cap — too small to function as a strategic hedge, and better read as a supplier financing a customer's working capital. What IS new is the money and the endorsement — Jefferies concedes it is *"a good endorsement of MediaTek technology from Nvidia"* — not the technical relationship.** ⚠️ **Note the source is the same desk that broke the 22.3%/7.3% CoWoS reallocation TO MediaTek; it is not a structurally bearish voice on this name.**
- **🆕 THE THREE ANNOUNCED LEGS, AND THE ONLY ONE WITH INDEPENDENT DEMAND EVIDENCE IS THE SMALLEST (NVIDIA/MediaTek joint release, 2026-08-31).** **(1) AI infrastructure — MediaTek adopts NVLink Fusion so *"customers can bring their XPU designs to MediaTek and tailor connectivity, memory, packaging, performance and power characteristics"*, focusing their own resources *"on the differentiated compute that defines their platforms while relying on NVIDIA and MediaTek for the NVLink connectivity, memory architecture, packaging, manufacturing and rack-scale technologies."* (2) Local AI computing — *"multiple generations of NVIDIA RTX Spark and DGX Spark PC chips"* integrating NVIDIA GPUs with MediaTek SoCs. (3) Automotive — software-defined vehicles.** 🔴 **Leg (2) has a hard datapoint the same weekend: *"Asustek's and MSI's initial batches of AI PCs designed around Nvidia RTX Spark processors have **completely SOLD OUT in pre-order**, and both have **already asked Nvidia for more chips**, which are made on TSMC's 3nm process"* (@dnystedt, corpus, 2026-08-30). ➜ **The leg the market is not paying for is the only one currently demand-constrained. Leg (1) — the entire ASIC thesis — remains a capability announcement with no order attached, which is exactly what MS confirms below.**
- **🆕⚠️ MANAGEMENT DID *NOT* RAISE ANYTHING ON THE BACK OF THIS, AND THAT IS THE DISCIPLINED READ: *"Although MediaTek DID NOT CHANGE its 2027 ASIC TAM and market share guidance given this extensive partnership and said IT WOULD TAKE TIME, we do think it will increase the chance for MediaTek to win a second CSP customer… MediaTek during the interview on August 31, indicated **it is indeed engaging with some customers**"* (MS · Charlie Chan, 2026-08-31).** ➜ **The US$12-16bn 2027 guide is UNCHANGED as of the deal. MS's upside case to its 2028 ASIC assumption rests on three unbanked items — *"1) higher chance to win a second or even third CSP customers; 2) higher revenue from material preparation; 3) solidified partnership with Alphabet in TPU."* ⚠️ Item (2) is BofA's 5-10x hardware-ASP leg under another name: two houses now model the same thing and neither has a contract. Score the next briefing on whether *"engaging with some customers"* becomes a name.**
- **🆕 THE MARKET'S OWN READ SPLIT INTO THREE CAMPS ON THE DAY (corpus, 2026-08-31).** **(a) Kingmaker — *"It does feel like Nvidia is playing KINGMAKER with the next generation of ASIC winners like $MRVL / MediaTek, with a second order effect of eroding $AVGO position"* (@aleabitoreddit).** **(b) Term-structure — *"while the short term impact to $AVGO is not threatened, I do think you have to seriously weigh the LONGER TERM impact on their custom XPU business. **Others are going to get more volume than originally assumed**"* (@BenBajarin, after *"a few background call backs"*).** **(c) Circularity — *"A little circular financing to start the week"* (@zerohedge).** ➜ **(b) is the most useful because it separates the two clocks, and it matches UBS's house position on [[AVGO]] the same day (*"plan of record"* near-term, F2027 TPU units CUT). Working synthesis for this page: nothing in this deal changes 2026-27 allocation; everything in it changes who is credible at v10 and beyond.**

- **🆕🔴 BEAR / CONTESTED (2026-08-18) — A HOUSE THAT COVERS [[AVGO]] SAYS THE v8i IS *NOT* MEDIATEK'S.** In its AVGO F3Q26 preview, Wells Fargo lists Broadcom's Google content as **"custom XPUs + rack components; focus on TPU v8t / v8i positioning — WE BELIEVE BOTH ARE BROADCOM, vs reports that TPU v8i is MediaTek"** (Wells Fargo · Aaron Rakers, 2026-08-18). ➤ **This is a direct denial of the generation split this page has been building on — the working map here (Jeff Pu 06-30, @jukan05 06-30, and Barclays via the AVGO page: "AVGO makes the TPU 8i, the volume node at >$60bn CY27, vs ~$10bn MediaTek 8t") assigns v8t to MediaTek and v8i to Broadcom, so Wells is contesting the MEDIATEK half rather than the Broadcom half.** ⚠️ **Note what it does and does not say: Wells asserts a belief, cites no check, and is writing from the incumbent's side of the trade. It does NOT dispute MediaTek's second-source role at Google in general — the same note calls "MediaTek's position as second-source for Google TPUs… a primary focus" and flags reports of [[AMD]] possibly involved in TPU v10.** ➤ **Kept as an open contest, not a correction: the page's part-level attribution now has one named house on each side, and the resolution is a checkable fact at the next TPU disclosure. Anyone sizing MediaTek's 2027 DC-ASIC revenue off a specific part number should treat the part attribution as an assumption, not a given.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/81b52470-2170-4d31-a7b2-2d8f34f632ce.html)
- **🆕 TWO POST-CALL MODEL REVISIONS LAND THE SAME DAY AND THEY AGREE ON THE MECHANISM (OPERATING LEVERAGE) WHILE DISAGREEING BY ~10% ON THE PRICE — the useful part is that JPM now publishes the unit-and-ASP build behind the US$12-16bn, which the page did not have.** **J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan — OW, PT NT$5,300 (Jun-27) UNCHANGED, but FY27/28 EPS RAISED +8%/+7% to NT$137.95 / NT$296.89** (from NT$128.04 / NT$277.85), on "stronger ASIC momentum and better operating leverage" (2026-08-02, price NT$3,555 as of 07-31).
  - **THE UNIT/ASP BUILD, net-new to this page: DC ASIC revenue US$2.25bn / US$15.1bn / US$43.3bn in 2026/27/28, on 0.5mn / 3.3mn / 1.3mn TPU v8 "Zebrafish" units at ASP US$4,250 / 4,500 / 4,000, plus 3.0mn TPU v9 "Humufish" units in 2028 at ASP US$12,667 (US$38.0bn).** ⚠️ **Read the ASP step: v9 is modelled at ~2.8x the v8 ASP. That single assumption is ~88% of the 2028 ASIC line and therefore ~most of the SoTP. It is the number to attack.** Other enterprise ASIC is a rounding error (US$114/135/149mn). JPM's 2028 revenue is ABOVE its own prior estimate on ASIC and BELOW on mobile.
  - **THE OPERATING-LEVERAGE CLAIM, quantified: "DC ASIC could reach ~11%/45%/70% of company revenue in 2026/27/28, driving meaningful OPM expansion to 16%/22%/26%."** JPM estimates **ASIC GM "still above 40%"** (i.e. slightly below corporate) with **"limited incremental opex post-ramp (mostly R&D… which we do not expect to fluctuate materially), while variable OPEX is likely to be small (some IP licensing and royalties, and warranty provisions)."** **This is the same shape MS stated qualitatively on 08-01 ("GM dilutive, OpM sizable and accretive") now carried through to a number — the two houses are corroborating, not competing, and the page can treat GM-dilutive/OpM-accretive as settled.** JPM's annual OPM revision: 16.3%/21.8%/26.4% for 26/27/28 vs prior 16.0%/20.7%/24.6% — the leverage upgrade is entirely in the out-years.
  - ⚠️ **THE MOBILE CUT IS SEVERE AND WAS NOT VISIBLE ON THE PAGE: JPM cut smartphone SHIPMENTS by −13%/−21%/−22% for 2026/27/28 to 299/240/230mn units** (from 345/306/294mn), 4G −17/−24/−24% and 5G −9/−18/−19%, with **mobile revenue cut −10%/−20%/−22%.** Quarterly: 3Q26E units 67mn (−19% vs prior), 4Q26E 63mn (−21%). **ASP is raised to offset partially (2026 US$26.8 → 30.4 → 32.5).** So the EPS raise is a net of a large ASIC upgrade against a large handset downgrade — **the core business is being written down toward a terminal ~NT$51-53 of core EPS on 8x, i.e. NT$416/share, only ~8% of the NT$5,300 SoTP; NT$4,879 is the ASIC leg at 30x.** ⚠️ **Anyone who thinks the smartphone franchise is a floor under this stock should read that SoTP: JPM ascribes it essentially no support.** Management framing per JPM: 3Q sequential decline "despite flagship launches," 1H pull-in on expected memory-price rises, GMs "holding up better than feared despite decreasing scale and rising cost pressures (foundry, OSAT, substrate)," margins stabilising **44-46%** — and explicitly **"we do not expect margin expansion in the core business from price hikes alone, given still-lackluster end demand."**
  - **TPU v10 "ICEFISH" — JPM's checks now put a business model on it, and it rebuts the CoT-disintermediation bear case head-on.** ⚠️ **The bear case JPM is answering is the one raised by GUC/Alchip commentary — that a fuller Customer-Owned-Tooling model at v10 strips MediaTek's dollar content. JPM: "We don't think so."** Its checks say the **v10 model stays "largely similar" to v9 — high-speed SerDes, I/O dies, D2D interconnect IP, packaging design and full-chip implementation from MediaTek, compute-die design largely in-house at Google** — with **"only variable… the Compute die BACK-END design, which may be outsourced to pure design-service vendors like Alchip / GUC for certain SKUs, although this is not yet crystallized."** JPM's conclusion: **dollar content per chip still rises modestly at v10, ASPs up not down, "contrary to some market concerns about ASP erosion," with a "high likelihood of securing the project and even potential share expansion" on a semi-CoT **TCO advantage versus Broadcom**. Project is **still at RFQ**, decision **"in the coming months" / by end-2026**, mass production **late 2029**. See [[AVGO]] and [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). ⚠️ **Note the tension with JPM's OWN GUC note published five hours later the same morning, which frames the identical CoT shift as "the black-box model from US fabless vendor… will be BROKEN DOWN SYSTEMATICALLY in favor of a disaggregated approach" — same analyst, same day. The reconciliation JPM offers is a hierarchy, not a contradiction: "the highest value capture from this approach in the next 3-4 years is likely to accrue to Mediatek," with GUC/Alchip benefiting "somewhat." Worth holding the page to that ranking when v10 is actually awarded.**
  - **EMIB-T YIELD IS NOW A NUMBER — "~60% currently at SUBSTRATE level and ~90% at CHIP-ON-SUBSTRATE level, per our checks."** This is the first quantification on this wiki of the yield question the page has been carrying qualitatively since 07-31, and JPM frames it as the resolved obstacle rather than the live one. ⚠️ **Cross-check against [[INTC]] and the Unimicron supply-chain read the same weekend, which puts the SUBSTRATE-side initial yield target at ~50% with Intel providing commercial protection to the substrate vendors during the ramp (@IntelProMUltra relaying Unimicron's Q2'26 call, 2026-08-01) — i.e. two independent sources bracket EMIB-T substrate yield at 50-60% today, and one of them says the economics only work because Intel is subsidising it.** That is consistent with commitment and with fragility at the same time.
  - **SECOND ASIC CUSTOMER — JPM names it, with a hedge: "we believe MediaTek is making solid progress on new customer RFQs, with one getting close to a potential win (WE BELIEVE SPACEX). We expect confirmation before end-2026," with meaningful revenue "likely late 2028 or beyond."** Treated as a **share-price catalyst that eases single-customer concentration** rather than a numbers event. Also: **MediaTek "is expanding its business model toward SUB-SYSTEM AND RACK-LEVEL design"** for new customers — corroborating the rack-design disclosure already on the page from the MS note, and see [[SPCX]].
  - **JPM's three named 6-month catalysts: (1) clarity on the TPUv9 Humufish ramp, (2) confirmation of the second customer (SpaceX), (3) "increasing evidence in the supply chain that MediaTek could be the PRIMARY player in the TPUv10 generation"** — plus **Google Cloud's 80%+ 2Q26 revenue growth "led by TPUs"** and possible 2H26 Gemini launches as sentiment support. See [[GOOG]].
- **🆕 NOMURA IS THE STREET HIGH AND IT IS NOT CLOSE — Aaron Jeng maintains BUY with TP NT$5,800 (63% implied upside), on 25x the 2027-28 average EPS against a stock at 15x (2026-08-02, close NT$3,555).** Jeng's framing is explicitly a rebuttal of the July de-rating: the questions he fielded since his late-June preview were **"triggered by STREET NOISE such as the delays of TPU v8 or v9 projects, rising ASIC competition, and likely negative sales growth for 3Q26F"** — and he argues the guide addressed all three. **He keeps 2026-28F EPS UNCHANGED** (in contrast to JPM's +8%/+7%), i.e. he had already underwritten the raise: **his standing US$14bn 2027 ASIC estimate sits inside the new US$12-16bn guide.** ⚠️ **One net-new modelling detail and it is a positive: the "implied" 4Q26E GPM guidance of AT LEAST 45-46% is "slightly ABOVE our model forecast of 44.7%," which Jeng reads as price increases offsetting ASIC dilution — a second-order confirmation that the price-hike pass-through is real in the numbers, not just in the commentary.** He also carries **v9 EMIB-T "progressing well… with further share gains and HIGH VOLUME PRODUCTION IN 2028"** and **"MTK is also engaging with SEVERAL OTHER ASIC customers"** (plural, unnamed — a weaker but broader claim than JPM's single SpaceX). **Spread to note: NT$5,800 (Nomura) vs NT$5,300 (JPM) vs the NT$3,555 tape — the bull case is now a ~50-60% upside call from two houses whose disagreement is about the multiple (25x blended 27-28 vs a 8x/30x SoTP), not about the ASIC dollars.**
- **🆕 The Morgan Stanley call note is the fullest transcript-level record of the 07-31 briefing on this page, and it settles the 448G/400G inconsistency, dates EMIB-T mass production, and — most usefully for the model — states the ASIC MARGIN SHAPE for the first time (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan, "MediaTek 2Q26 Earnings Call Note," distributed 2026-08-01; published view: "Raising 2027 ASIC revenue and more confident about 2028; reiterate OW," 2026-07-31).** Five items that are net-new against what this page already carries from the Jefferies/GS/JPM/BofA relays:
  - ⚠️ **ASIC MARGIN STRUCTURE — the missing piece for anyone modelling the US$12-16bn: "GM will be DILUTIVE, but OpM will be SIZABLE AND ACCRETIVE… opex ratio will come down substantially but value will increase."** So the ASIC ramp should be modelled as a **gross-margin drag with operating leverage more than offsetting it** — which is why management can guide GM to a flat 46% ±1.5pt band while adding a US$12-16bn low-margin revenue leg. **This directly reconciles the two things that looked contradictory on the 07-31 print: a defensive pricing posture ("we are not increasing price to increase GM… just looking to maintain GM") sitting alongside a large ASIC revenue raise.** Anyone applying MediaTek's ~46% corporate GM to incremental ASIC dollars is modelling it wrong in one direction, and anyone assuming the OpM stays at 15% is wrong in the other.
  - **448G, NOT 400G — the flagged inconsistency now has two of three sources on 448G.** MS's independent write-up: **"448G Serdes is progressing well, delivering best-in-class performance and power with our Co-packaged copper system solution… expected to be ready sometime in the SECOND HALF OF NEXT YEAR with tapeout on schedule."** Same timing (2H27) as the Fubon-relayed "400G", same "on schedule" tape-out — so the number, not the calendar, was the discrepancy, and **the page should carry 448G as the working figure with the 400G relay marked as a probable transcription error rather than a second product.** MS adds that **the NEXT generation is OPTICAL and "development is on schedule"**, and that MediaTek's **D2D performance and power are "industry equivalent."**
  - ⚠️ **EMIB-T now has a MASS-PRODUCTION DATE AND A BACK-UP PLAN, which nuances the "we are more committed to EMIB-T" headline from the call.** MS: **"EMIB-T technology is making good progress in terms of yield rate improvement and maturity for MASS PRODUCTION IN 2028. Will have some BACK-UP PLANS (e.g. TSMC CoWoS/CoPoS) but SWITCHING THE TECHNOLOGY MIGHT TAKE TIME."** Read against [[INTC]]: the customer commitment is real and now dated to the same year as the second accelerator's HVM (early 2028), but MediaTek is explicitly dual-tracking, and the "switching might take time" caveat is the honest statement of how binding the [[INTC]] dependency becomes if 18A-era packaging slips. See [[TSM]] and [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
  - **A RACK-LEVEL business is being built, with one named-but-undisclosed customer: "is further advancing its PRE-VALIDATED SYSTEM/RACK DESIGN. Is working with ONE CUSTOMER on this and will provide more details next time."** Combined with the end-to-end 3.5D platform and the "subsystem/chassis format" language, this is MediaTek moving up the stack from accelerator silicon toward the system — the exact capability the AlphaSense cloud-architect expert call (07-30) said it LACKED ("focused on price competitiveness but lacking system-level capabilities"). ⚠️ **Not yet a rebuttal — one customer, no details, no revenue — but it is the first evidence the gap is being addressed, and it is the thing to test at the next briefing.**
  - **HBM stays out of the SAM by CUSTOMER CHOICE, not by MediaTek's exclusion — and management expects that to persist: "CSP still requiring HBM instead of any solutions so would still be the mainstream in the near term."** This is management closing the door on HMC/alternatives near-term, and it reinforces the **accelerator-only basis guardrail** already on this page: the US$80bn SAM must not be compared gross against [[AVGO]] figures that include memory.
  - Supporting detail also captured: **2026 revenue now expected to grow HIGH single digit y/y, i.e. "the high end of the last guided up mid-high single digit"**; full-year GM reframed to the **46% ±1.5pt range** (an update from "sustain at 46%" last quarter); **2Q26 segment split Mobile 41% of revenue (−14% q/q, −20% y/y), Smart Edge 53% (+19% q/q, +26% y/y), Power IC 6% (+11% q/q, +6% y/y)** — with Smart Edge strength credited to share gains across connectivity/computing/automotive **plus "the contribution from DRAM content in TV SoC"** (i.e. part of the Smart Edge beat is memory-price pass-through, not unit or share gain, and should be quality-adjusted accordingly); **capacity "secured enough to support MediaTek's growth, and expects to see some upsides, including the demand in 2027"**; and on 2028, **"not yet ready to provide guidance… but confident it will be quite a bit bigger than 2027, as well as MediaTek's market shares… thinks will get BETTER-THAN-FEARED market share."** (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan, 2026-08-01)
- **Bull:** The data-center ASIC ramp is the re-rating engine. MediaTek is the **co-design ("semi-COT") partner on Google's TPU** — V8e "Zebrafish" (8T; 2nm, bigger than Ironwood, 4 compute dies vs Ironwood's 2) is ramping after a year's delay, and MediaTek is the **primary candidate for V9 in 2028** while Broadcom's "Pumafish" V9 (EMIB) got pushed out/cancelled, replaced by a "Sunfish Ultra" extension. JPM models MediaTek TPU revenue at **USD 10–12bn in 2027** and DC ASIC at **USD 25–30bn by 2028** — a steep ramp from ~USD 2bn (Gopal/Samik, 2026-06-10). Additional RFQs in hand from **Meta, Microsoft, Tesla**, with another customer potentially announced before end-2026; "they pretty much see every RFQ right now" (Gopal, 2026-06-10). V9 ASP rises to USD 12–15K (from ~USD 4.5K on V8) on a 4-compute-die N2 design with EMIB and more MediaTek content (Gopal, 2026-06-10). DC ASIC is **operating-margin accretive** (Q1 FY26).
- **🆕 Bull, and the most detailed 2Q26/3Q26 picture on the page — BofA (Haas Liu) reiterates Buy, PO NT$5,100 → NT$5,230, and puts the cloud-ASIC ramp at US$2.1bn / US$13.3bn / US$45.8bn for '26/'27/'28E (BofA · Haas Liu, "Solid AI and auto ASIC progress beyond the near term muted mobile demand," 2026-07-27 dateline / received 2026-07-26):** price NT$3,750, PO on the **same 23x 2H27-1H28E P/E** — so the PO raise is estimate-driven, not multiple-driven. **The print already happened and beat: 2Q26 sales +2% q/q against a guide of "-0 to -6% q/q,"** which BofA attributes to **customers pulling in chipset inventory ahead of a price hike** to pass through higher advanced-node, packaging/testing and component costs in 2H26. Mix lifts 2Q GM to the **upper half of the 44.5-47.5% guide (BofAe 46.4%)**. ⚠️ **Note the guide discrepancy against what this page carries from the Q1 call: this page has Q2 FY26 GM guided at 43% ±1.5pt (i.e. 41.5-44.5%), while BofA cites a 44.5-47.5% guide** — a ~3pt gap on the same quarter's guidance. Not resolved here; one of the two is stale or mistranscribed, and the 7/31 call is the place to settle it. **3Q26 is where BofA is deliberately street-low: sales −6% q/q vs street +4%**, China mobile order cuts offsetting healthy consumer (cost pass-through) and automotive (share gains). **The ASIC numbers are the reason to own it on BofA's math:** TPU **v8t for Google ramping meaningfully from 4Q26**, cloud ASIC **US$2.1bn ('26E) → US$13.3bn ('27E)** "with upside if more of TSMC's CoWoS capacity support is locked in"; **TPU v9t tape-out ready in 4Q26 for production from 2H27**; and — net-new to this page — **a co-developed AI-accelerator ASIC with xAI set for tape-out in 4Q27**, lifting cloud ASIC to **US$45.8bn in 2028E**, with further optionality from **Meta and Toyota/Denso ADAS ASICs**. EPS raised 1-3% to **NT$67.67 / NT$137.87 / NT$346.11** for '26/'27/'28E; shares at **27x/11x '27/'28E vs a historical 8-25x range**, on **'26-28E sales/EPS CAGRs of 85%/130%**. Call focus flagged: end-market outlook, ASIC TAM/share, chipset pricing and GMs "amid cost hike across the chain." (BofA, 2026-07-26)
- **🆕 Bull (MS · Charlie Chan reiterates OW, PT NT$5,588 held, industry Top Pick — and expects management to RAISE the 2027 TPU revenue guide to US$12-15bn from US$7-12bn, 2026-07-26):** MS's 2Q26 preview ahead of the **July 31 call**, and the first Morgan Stanley mark carried on this page. PT NT$5,588 implies **18x 2028e EPS** — "still attractive vs its 2025-28e revenue CAGR of >50%." The framing: **"3Q26 should mark the bottom of MediaTek's transition to a Cloud AI ASIC revenue cycle. We see a strong TPU revenue ramp starting in 4Q26 onward, which we expect will account for more than half of revenue by end-2027."** Near-term MS is street-low with BofA — **3Q26 revenue down mid-single-digit q/q vs Consensus +2%**, on limited 3nm TPU and less new-flagship 2nm Dimensity 9600 revenue plus China smartphone weakness; **GM held at 45-46% in both 2Q26 and 3Q26** on incremental substrate and foundry costs; **>US$2bn of TPU revenue maintained in 4Q26**. **The guidance-raise call is the actionable part, and the arithmetic behind it is worth carrying: MS puts the 2027 TPU revenue TAM at US$100bn, of which a 20% MediaTek revenue share = US$20bn — above the company's own US$7-12bn guide — so MS expects the guide to move to US$12-15bn (MS's own forecast ~US$15bn).** On the [[AVGO]] split, MS reconciles its global view explicitly: **"MediaTek's 3nm TPU volume shipment may approach that of AVGO in 2027. However, AVGO should remain the majority TPU supplier over time, with ~80% revenue share, given higher ASP on higher gross margin"** — i.e. MediaTek closes the unit gap while Broadcom keeps the revenue and margin. Five questions MS wants asked on the call, all of which are live debates on this page: **(1) progress of 2nm TPU and Intel EMIB-T production yield — and any backup plan to use TSMC CoWoS; (2) next-gen 1.4nm TPU v10 competition; (3) updates on the second CSP customer; (4) ASIC TAM and MediaTek's 2028 share "considering new Qualcomm competition in ASIC design service"; (5) AI smartphone killer apps / China-or-US LLM partnerships.** The Qualcomm-entering-ASIC-design-services item is net-new to this page as a named competitive vector. (MS · Charlie Chan / Daniel Yen / Daisy Dai / Lucas Wang, 2026-07-26)
- **🆕 Bull (UBS reiterates the TPU preference inside its Intel-vs-TSMC packaging deep-dive — UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership?", 2026-07-26/27):** in a note whose main subject is [[INTC]] EMIB-T vs [[TSM]] CoPoS, UBS states its MediaTek stance plainly: **"We like MediaTek on its TPU opportunity and see the EMIB-T execution risks as manageable."** That is a direct answer to the packaging-risk bear that Fubon raised (EMIB-T "not yet MP-ready," so V9 priority stays TSMC) and that MS is asking management about. The supporting numbers UBS supplies are on the [[INTC]]/[[TSM]] pages but bear on the MediaTek v9 path: **EMIB-T packaging validation yields ~90-92% (needing to reach HVM levels), EMIB-T "substrate" fabrication yields possibly ~50% vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates, but capacity expansion at Ibiden "could offset lower yields and support selected products like Google's TPU v9"** — i.e. UBS's own base case has the substrate constraint solved by Ibiden capacity rather than by yield, and specifically for TPU v9. EMIB-T targeted for **mass production in H2'27-28**, slightly ahead of CoPoS in 2028. (UBS, 2026-07-26)
