- **🆕 Bull (Jefferies sales · Cristina Titu, "MediaTek: The TPU Trade Nobody Owns Yet (+56%)", 2026-07-23 — marked "market commentary, not research"):** the sharpest positioning framing on the page — **"as your inbox fills up with Google earnings analysis, I would argue the more interesting opportunity sits next door."** Thesis: MediaTek is **"transitioning from a mature mobile-chip story into an AI ASIC champion. The market is focused on the noise. The money is in the signal"** — recent weakness driven by noise while fundamentals strengthen. Carries **Fubon (Sherman Shang) at NT$6,000, i.e. 56% upside** (consistent with the Fubon TP already on this page from 06-05). Supply-chain checks cited: **TPU volumes reaching 12–15mn units by 2028**, with the **next-gen TPU V10 architecture potentially driving 30–35mn compute dies through the ecosystem** — "not incremental growth; an AI capex supercycle." **Management remains confident TPU revenues begin contributing in 4Q26 despite market chatter around delays** — a direct, dated rebuttal of the delay bear. **The number Titu says investors should focus on: MediaTek's custom-ASIC market share rising from 10–15% to as much as 15–20% in FY27** as CoWoS allocations firm and packaging constraints ease. Earnings leverage: **FY27 revenue NT$1.22tn (+~90% y/y), EPS NT$153.34 — 27% above previous estimates and materially ahead of consensus**, with operating profit more than doubling. **Valuation defence:** re-rated to ~51x in May-2026, back to **~39x today**; on ~96% consensus EPS growth that is a **PEG of 1.23x** — "you are paying roughly 1x growth for a company doubling earnings annually," which she argues is far more defensible than the headline 39x. **Optionality not in forecasts: a second hyperscaler ASIC project in final design stages, with industry speculation pointing toward xAI/SpaceX** — cross-reference the "2nd US CSP project coming soon" already on this page from Macquarie (06-30) and the [[SPCX]] page. ⚠️ Note this is **sales commentary carrying a third-party (Fubon) target**, not Jefferies research, and the 12–15mn/30–35mn unit figures are supply-chain checks rather than company guidance.
- **🆕 Bull (a second independent EMIB-T corroboration — UBS · Timothy Arcuri, INTC note, 2026-07-24):** UBS's own checks **"suggest MediaTek may be among potential EMIB-T customers for its v9-gen TPU chip."** This is now the **third independent source** tying MediaTek's v9 TPU to Intel's EMIB-T packaging — after **JPM (Hariharan, 07-01)**, which modelled MediaTek v9 ("Humufish") unit upside as partly a function of **Intel's EMIB-T packaging yield**, and **UBS (Sunny Lin, 07-01)**, which flagged Intel prioritising internal products plus the Google/MediaTek v9 engagements under a 2028 EMIB-T capacity constraint. Also from the same INTC print: Intel highlighted a **growing EMIB-T backlog** and MS models **billions of EMIB-T revenue next year**. **Why this matters here rather than only on [[INTC]]:** it makes **Intel's packaging yield an input to MediaTek's own v9 unit forecast** — a supply dependency outside MediaTek's control, and one where the evidence is genuinely two-sided (Fubon, 07-08, reported Google asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to 95%+ before production and judged that "very challenging" by mid-2027, concluding Google would fall back to TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion). Read the two together: **the design intent is now corroborated by three houses; the yield gate remains unresolved.** See [[INTC]], [[GOOG]].
- **Bull (Macquarie PT NT$10,000, @dnystedt 2026-06-30):** Macquarie Securities raised its MediaTek price target to **NT$10,000** on strength in the custom-ASIC business — citing both Google TPU orders and a **2nd US Cloud Service Provider (CSP) project "coming soon."** This is now the Street-high PT and corroborates the JPM/Fubon multi-customer thesis (Meta/MS/Tesla RFQs) with a named-but-unnamed second US CSP win as the incremental catalyst. Tape context: MediaTek +9% on 2026-06-30 as the Korea-memory scare faded (R&Co TMT Daily, 2026-06-30). (@dnystedt, 2026-06-30; Macquarie via media)
- **Bull (Triggerfish thesis, Jeff Pu / @sssjeffpu 2026-06-30):** Pu argues the Street underestimates MediaTek's **"Triggerfish"** TPU part. Content/ASP: **~USD 18K vs Humufish ~USD 13K or Zebrafish ~USD 5.5K** — Triggerfish to be **~50% of the v9 mix** (incl. Humufish). Architecture: **4x companion compute cores with large SRAM (~250MB ×4), one 5nm simulator, 4x I/O cores** — the large SRAM + simulator CPU lets MediaTek **extend from training (v8t) into inferencing**. Competitive: rumored Broadcom "Bladerunner" (2nm) "hasn't progressed"; only one new TPU SKU (Whalefish/Sunfish ×2) expected in 2028, at a process-node/HBM disadvantage → limited volume. Positioning by generation: **v8** — MTK's v8t to start volume earlier than v8i; **v9** — MTK's Humufish/Triggerfish "the only 2nm project by now"; **v10** — MTK's Icefish "to be solid, while others uncertain." Note MediaTek shares underperformed recently despite sell-side TP raises (a setup/expectations point). (Jeff Pu / @sssjeffpu, 2026-06-30)
- **Bull (roadmap sequencing, @jukan05 2026-06-30):** independent read echoing Pu's generation map — **v8:** MTK's v8t to start volume earlier than v8i; **v9:** MTK's Humufish/Triggerfish is "by now the only 2nm project"; **v10:** MTK's Icefish "to be solid, while others uncertain." Jukan flags this contradicts the prior North-American sell-/buy-side narrative that MediaTek's **v8t was progressing more slowly than v8i and had been delayed** — a positive revision to the execution read. (@jukan05, 2026-06-30)
- **Bull (Nomura Anchor Report, 2026-06-30):** In "Is the cycle over?" Nomura reiterates **Buy on MediaTek with a raised TP, citing "TPU upside."** Its 2027F CoWoS-allocation work argues **Google's TPU CoWoS share rises to 26% in 2027 (from 23% in 2026)** — nearly doubling y/y — and, critically, **"the majority of [TPU] growth would be taken by MediaTek (its share in TPU could more than double to 30%+ in 2027F from ~15% in 2026)"** — a more aggressive 2027 read than Fubon's 17%. MediaTek is named the ASIC design partner of **TPU v8t (codenamed "Mad Dog")** and benefits from Google's more aggressive 2026-27F capacity bookings; 2028F "remains a mystery" pending Google/Intel **EMIB-T** execution (the most-watched project being **Google v9 TPU + MediaTek on EMIB-T, >9× reticle, ramp 2028**). Nomura raises Google's contribution to TSMC AI revenue **+200% 2026F / +116% 2027F** (~4.2mn/8.3mn TPU builds). (Nomura "Is the cycle over?" / Aaron Jeng, 2026-06-30)
- **Bull (Fubon 2027 outlook, 2026-06-05):** Fubon raises its MediaTek TP to **NT$6,000** (Buy) on higher TPU V9 involvement + the Meta Arke project. Key model: MediaTek now handles the **two N2 compute dies** on V9 (not just I/O), lifting dollar content to **US$15–16K/chip** (vs ~US$4.5K Zebrafish). TPU market share (by compute-die count) **6% in 2026 → 17% in 2027 → 30% in 2028**; **V8t "Zebrafish" volume 2.5–3M units in 2027** (~99.9k CoWoS wafers, 5.3% of TSMC CoWoS, raised from prior). On packaging, Fubon argues **Intel EMIB-T is not yet MP-ready**, so the V9 priority stays **TSMC CoWoS-L** — "risky to bet on one side." **Meta Arke** deal expected to finalize **late 3Q26** (a 2028 growth driver); also positive on the NVIDIA **RTX Spark** project though volume is only ~5–10M (limited revenue) (Fubon "Semi 2027 outlook", 2026-06-05).
- **Bull (channel corroboration, Dec-2025):** Jefferies/Sherman raises his TPU estimate to **~1m units across 2026+2027 combined, ~15% of total 2027 TPU shipments**, at ~USD 4K dollar-content/TPU → a **~USD 4bn TAM for 2026/27 combined**; total TPU volume could **double to 5–6m units in 2027** (5m Broadcom + ~1m MediaTek) at USD 23–34K system pricing. Follow-on TPU projects (more complex design) carry **sales in 2028**; MediaTek is a finalist (vs a US competitor) for **Meta**, decision "in the next few months." MediaTek raised its cloud-ASIC **TAM from USD 40bn to USD 50bn by 2028**, targeting **10–15% share (>USD 5bn in 2–3 yrs)**; its **224G SerDes IP is already approved** and it is "more flexible than others" but **will not move into lower-margin back-end** work, and is investing heavily into DC/CPO/silicon photonics/2nm/large-reticle and US headcount (Jefferies, 2025-12-17). Note: where JPM models V9 ASP at USD 12–15K, the Jefferies/MediaTek channel figure for current V8e dollar-content is ~USD 4K.
- **Bull (Bernstein, the original semi-COT thesis):** frames the engagement as **TPU V7e front+back-end design** and models **TPU revenue USD 1.9bn CY26 / USD 2.5bn CY27** at **~40% GM ex-HBM**; sizes the data-center custom-accelerator TAM for MediaTek at **USD 40–60bn in CY28**. Unlike US rivals that demand high margin, MediaTek "uniquely combines cost advantage, scale, and capabilities" — the Google+MediaTek combination could **save Google USD 3–4bn/yr** via lower MediaTek logic/CoWoS margins and direct HBM sourcing (Bernstein, 2025-05-05).
- **Bull (BofA rack-power model corroborates the MediaTek TPU ramp; BofA "Watts to Tokens", Vivek Arya, 2026-05-25, read-through):** BofA's bottoms-up per-platform GW/unit table (the source behind the raised chip forecast that also drives its "Power gap" utilities note) puts Google TPU at **~41GW cumulative deployed CY26-30**, split **Broadcom ~28GW + MediaTek ~17GW** — i.e. it independently models **MediaTek as Google's second TPU design-service partner**, with MediaTek TPU unit shipments ramping **365k (CY26) → 4,473k (CY30)** (~70 rack-equivalents by CY30) vs Broadcom's ~1.9m→5.5m. Directionally consistent with the UBS dual-track (9%→38% share) and Redburn/Nomura die-share reads already on this page; a semis-team cross-check that the MediaTek TPU volume ramp is real and material. See [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md) / [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_Watt_to_tokens.html)
- **Bull (HBM-cost advantage drives Google share gains; Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23):** Conor's supply-chain read is that the **rising HBM cost is the key driver of Google's shift to MediaTek** — MediaTek **does not bundle HBM in the BOM** (Broadcom does charge for it), so Google cuts a large chunk of the bill of materials by moving to MediaTek as long as it stays as good as Broadcom. Frames MediaTek's Google share going **6% (2026) → 30% (2028)** (Broadcom's share falling correspondingly), with the **400G switch timing (V9 vs V10)** the swing factor — Broadcom is still the only 400G supplier, so Google adopting 400G earlier (V9) could cap MediaTek's gains. No signs of MediaTek (or Qualcomm) cutting orders amid the compute shortage (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). Corroborated by StoneX/TrendForce: MediaTek (TPU V8/V9) is **increasingly handling Google's HBM procurement directly** and is in **talks with Samsung** on HBM3E/HBM4E supply — Google previously relied entirely on Broadcom for HBM (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23).
- **Bull (share-taker framing, @firstadopter 2026-06-30):** Tae Kim: "MediaTek is taking share from Broadcom and leaving Qualcomm in the dust" — a directional read consistent with the Google TPU share-shift thesis and MediaTek's DC-ASIC ramp vs QCOM's still-nascent DC push. (@firstadopter, 2026-06-30)
- **Bull (Goldman Sachs PT raised to NT$6,800, 2026-07-01):** GS (Evelyn Yu / Jim Schneider / Ryan Huang) reiterates **Buy**, PT raised to **NT$6,800** (from NT$5,000; 25x 2H27-1H28E EPS, 57% implied upside). Raises **2027E AI ASIC revenue to US$20.3bn** (from US$12.3bn), **49% of total revenue** — above MediaTek's own high-end guidance of US$12bn; **2028E AI ASIC revenue raised to US$52.5bn** (from US$48bn), **69% of total revenue**. Factors in a **5% price hike starting 3Q26** to cover supply-chain cost inflation, with GM expected to stay steady; models **2027/28E revenue growth of 95%/83% YoY** (vs 61%/111% prior) with **operating margin expanding to 25%/33%** (vs 15% in 2026E); **EPS NT$181.92/NT$422.17** in 2027/28E (Goldman Sachs · Evelyn Yu/Jim Schneider/Ryan Huang, 2026-07-01).
- **Bull (JPMorgan "Addressing Key Investor Questions," Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01):** JPM stays **Overweight, PT NT$5,300** (Jun-27 horizon) vs price NT$4,335. **TPU v8t (Zebrafish)** ramp on track for 4Q26, supporting the **2026 target of US$2bn ASIC revenue** (400-500k unit shipments). For 2027, management guided **US$7-12bn ASIC revenue** (implying 10-15% share of a US$70-80bn ASIC market); JPM revised TPU estimates up in May to **8mn market TPU shipments in 2027** (vs 5mn six months ago). JPM sees upside to its own MediaTek enterprise ASIC estimate of **US$10.8bn in 2027** (~2.5mn TPU v8t units, ~30% share of the 8mn total), with further upside beyond US$12bn if MediaTek secures additional substrate and 3nm wafer capacity (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01).
- **Bull (JPM v9 Humufish / v10 roadmap detail, Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01):** **TPU v9 ("Humufish")** design on track to tape out **late 2026-early 2027**, revenue contribution starting **late 2027/early 2028**; ASP expected **2-3x v8t (US$12k+ vs ~US$4.3k)**, driven by 4 N2 compute dies + 4 I/O dies + 4 memory I/O dies vs 1 N3 compute die + 1 I/O die in v8t. Supply-chain checks indicate the competing **"Pumafish" project (US Fabless — implied Broadcom) has been cancelled or postponed**, a share-gain opportunity for MediaTek in **2028-29**; the competitor may still launch a **Sunfish v8i variant** (4 N3 compute dies), but MediaTek is expected 1-2 quarters ahead in mass production. JPM models **~2M units for Humufish v9 in 2028**, upside contingent on Intel EMIB-T packaging yield progress; also flags an extension design **("Triggerfish")** adding more SRAM and a controller die for inference. On **v10**: RFQ in progress, JPM sees high probability MediaTek secures at least one **TPU v10 ("Icefish")** project, with Broadcom supporting another v10 project; Google still using a semi-CoT model for v10 (not full CoT) and not embracing CPO for TPU in this generation; MediaTek's key v10 IP: 448G high-speed electrical SERDES, die-to-die interconnect, 3D packaging experience. Separately, a possible **fast-inference SRAM-based chip in the v9 family (codenamed "Merope")** is likely to be awarded to **Marvell**; MediaTek is in RFQ discussions with several hyperscalers believed to include **Tesla, Meta, Microsoft**, with the best win chance seen at Tesla within 1-2 quarters — though any win would not contribute revenue before late 2028-2029 (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, "MediaTek Inc.: Addressing Key Investor Questions," 2026-07-01).
- **Bull (UBS "Key Call," Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01):** UBS names MediaTek a **Buy and "Key Call"** among its cloud AI supply-chain top picks; expected to support **4mn units of Google TPU v8t capacity in 2027**. UBS also flags MediaTek-adjacent upside via **Google CPU (through GUC)** and the **Amazon Trainium3/4 opportunity (through Alchip)** (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).
- **Bull (UBS "Sector Keys July 2026" — Key Call Buy, PT raised to NT$6,500, Sunny Lin, 2026-07-02):** UBS makes MediaTek its favored Taiwanese fabless "for its structural share gain in a large Google TPU market" (+**203% YTD**, the best in UBS's coverage vs SOX +101% / TSMC +62%). Model: **TPU sales US$18bn/US$35bn in 2027/28**; total TPU units 4.8m (2026E)→10.7m (2027E) with **Broadcom shipping 91%/62% of TPUs 2026/27E while MediaTek's share expands 9% (2026E)→38% (2027E)**; scenario has MediaTek shipping **4m v8t units in 2027 = US$18bn = ~45% of MediaTek's business**. Cost edge: on a **per-TPU-die basis MediaTek's service fee is ~50% less than Broadcom's.** Google runs **dual tracks** — Broadcom v8i + MediaTek v8t — over H226-2027 and **into the TPU v9 ramp (2028)**, with MediaTek ultimately diversifying across **TSMC CoWoS (smaller package, 2 TPU dies) and Intel EMIB-T (larger package, 4 TPU dies)**. **Identified new growth drivers = ~83% of MediaTek's 2028E revenue vs ~40% in 2023** — the clearest single framing of the transformation. Strong operating leverage: Cloud-ASIC OPM potentially **>37%**, contributing **EPS of NT$111/NT$219 in 2027/28E**. Also: **5-10% price hikes across products from Sep-2026** (advanced-node close to 10%); SerDes on track to **336G** (from 224G); potential first penetration of **Samsung's flagship in 2027** (S27 FE using D9600) and an **OpenAI edge-AI** engagement (wearables/IoT, possibly D9600 in 2027); flagship 5G volume 20m (2026E)→26m (2027E). PT NT$6,500 = 27x 2027-28E PE (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02).
- **Bull (Citi "Mediatek and GUC riding on Google," Laura Chen, 2026-07-06):** Citi prefers Mediatek most among TW design-service names and **raises Mediatek AI-ASIC revenue to US$18bn in 2027 (from US$13.6bn) and US$40bn in 2028 (from US$18bn)** — ~+50% in 2027 and roughly doubling into 2028 — on higher dollar content (value-add in both the **I/O die AND the compute die**; potential optics / custom-memory die longer term). Mediatek & GUC seen with the **strongest AI-ASIC growth momentum into 2027**, with Taiwanese design-service names that have better supply-chain management (foundry / advanced packaging / HBM / ABF) standing out. The 2027 US$18bn lands in line with UBS's US$18bn'27 mark and above JPM's US$10.8bn / MediaTek's own US$12bn high-end guide; the 2028 US$40bn sits between JPM's implied range and GS's US$52.5bn. (Citi · Laura Chen, 2026-07-06)
- **Bull (Deutsche Bank "Trigger for more gains," Robert Sanders / Ross Seymore, 2026-07-02):** DB reiterates **Buy, PT up to NT$5,000** (close NT$4,345), thesis "capturing the potential for more share gain at TPU v9." Adds a second raising broker to the v9-share-gain camp — lands below GS (NT$6,800) / Fubon (NT$6,000) and near JPM (NT$5,300), all well under Macquarie's NT$10,000 Street-high (Deutsche Bank · Robert Sanders/Ross Seymore, 2026-07-02).
- **Bull (Goldman Sachs "TSMC & MediaTek: Stronger Growth Trajectory," Evelyn Yu, 2026-07-09):** GS expects MediaTek to ride stronger AI ASIC momentum — positive on the ASIC/AI growth trajectory. A follow-on thematic reinforcement of GS's 2026-07-01 Buy / PT NT$6,800 (no PT or estimate change). (Goldman Sachs · Evelyn Yu, 2026-07-09)
- **Bull (Goldman Sachs TSMC webinar — chip-level build behind the raised numbers, Evelyn, 2026-07-09):** the GS TSMC webinar adds the granular build under the 2027E ~US$20bn / 2028E ~US$52bn ASIC marks (already on the page from GS 07-01, so no supersession). By GS's naming: **"HomoFish" 2027 shipments ~4.5M units at ASP ~US$4,500**, stepping to **2028 HomoFish ~3M units at ~US$16k plus "ZebraFish" +1M units** — driven by Google asking aggressively for wafer + CoWoS allocation over the past ~3 months. GS is **among the highest on the Street** and, critically, **models a 50/50 Google-TPU share split between Broadcom and MediaTek in 2027** — flagged as different from what US investors expect (they still model **higher Broadcom share**). On the yield concern for the next-gen chip built on **Intel IMT**: GS's supply-chain checks put **substrate yield ~70% and Intel bumping/assembly yield ~90%**, with **~1.5 years to ramp IMT** — progressing well enough that GS is "pretty positive" on that front. (Goldman Sachs "TSMC preview", 2026-07-09)
- **Mixed (Susquehanna "2Q26 MobileSIGnals," Christopher Rolland, 2026-07-08):** MediaTek secured substantial share in a China-heavy quarter (**~51% share overall**), while Chinese silicon (e.g. UniSoc) dominates low-end launches. SIG modestly **cut its 2026 handset sell-through forecast to −11.5% YoY (from −10% prior)** — the share-gain read (bull) offset by a softer handset-demand read (bear). First Susquehanna datapoint logged on this page. (Susquehanna · Christopher Rolland, 2026-07-08)
- **Bull (BofA Global Big Ideas — Zach Schwartz / BofA APAC Sales, 2026-07-08):** BofA pitched **Long MediaTek** on its Global Big Ideas ideas call (alongside Long CATL and Long Tencent). (BofA · Zach Schwartz / BofA APAC Sales, 2026-07-08)
- **Competition (DIGITIMES, 2026-07-02):** "[Qualcomm](QCOM.md)'s Dragonfly raises the stakes, but MediaTek's TPU and ASIC ties keep it in the race" — DIGITIMES frames QCOM's new Dragonfly cloud-AI platform as intensifying the cloud-AI competition, but reads MediaTek's entrenched Google-TPU / custom-ASIC relationships as a durable moat vs QCOM's still-nascent DC push (DIGITIMES, 2026-07-02).
- **Bear (2026 ramp-timing skepticism, Citi/Jefferies Asia checks):** Citi's Blayne Curtis flags that **MediaTek's TPU compute die was "still in the fab"** as of late May — and since Broadcom "essentially takes a year to get to volume production," if MediaTek's chip "hasn't even started the clock, I really don't know how they ship units this year" (vs MediaTek's ~$2bn 2026 talk); the "MediaTek frenzy" is pushing buy-side estimates up while Broadcom's TPU share "should remain substantial, like potentially 80%+" (Citi "Asia recap," 2026-05-22). Jefferies' Connor (Asia tech) is even more cautious near-term: he models **MediaTek 1-2m TPU units next year vs Broadcom 5-6m**, calls the investors at "3-5m for MediaTek next year" view "totally impossible from a Co-Packaged-Optics point of view," notes MediaTek is **"struggling to make the current ASIC"** (prompting a Sherman/Jefferies downgrade), and warns long-onlys are taking profits in MediaTek (the early-2026 TPU play) and rotating into Samsung — "be very, very careful on MediaTek in the short term" (Jefferies "Asia tech," 2026-04-07). The same note flags the **smartphone risk**: the ~15-31% Android unit decline is concentrated in the **non-Samsung Android camp — i.e. MediaTek's clients** — "that's why MediaTek's looking really bad" (Jefferies "Asia tech," 2026-04-07).
- **Bear/competition (Broadcom dismissive — the supplier-side counter, JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07):** on the JPM-hosted Hock Tan call, Broadcom was pointedly dismissive of MediaTek's Google-TPU "COT" **A8 program — "still in the Fab"** (nowhere near production), and dismissed the aggressive Taiwan sell-side share checks as **"the PR agency of MediaTek"** whose wafer-count numbers are "all incorrect." Hock argues Broadcom's TPU/XPU programs are **12-18 months ahead** of these COT efforts on performance/complexity/IP (multi-die packaging to 8 dies/chip, sole working 400G-copper SerDes) and that hyperscaler/COT custom efforts run **~2x less performance/complexity** — "not a chance" they keep up. A direct, self-interested counter to the MediaTek-share-gain bulls above (Broadcom talking its own book) — but a reminder the A8/next-gen timeline and share split remain contested between the two design partners. (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07)
- **Bear/mixed (MS defends AVGO's TPU majority — a dissent to the aggressive share-gain bulls, Morgan Stanley via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14):** MS's view: **"MediaTek participation is real, but not disruptive — AVGO should remain the majority TPU supplier over time, with ~80% share, and we see the bearish calls for 50% share or eventual displacement as premature."** A notable counterweight to the stacked 2027-share bulls on this page (UBS 9%→38%, Nomura 30%+, GS 50/50, Citi/GS US$18-20bn'27) — MS is materially more conservative on the *magnitude* of MediaTek's TPU share capture even while conceding the win is real. Sharpens the still-contested Broadcom-vs-MediaTek share-split debate (see the Broadcom-dismissive and Citi/Jefferies bears above). (Morgan Stanley, via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14) **✅ CONFIRMED IN A PUBLISHED MS NOTE (2026-07-27): the @firstadopter secondhand tweet above is now sourced to a primary document — Joseph Moore, in MS's "Playing the AI Infrastructure Dip," writes that "the debate around MediaTek versus Broadcom share on Google TPU is an overhang" for [[AVGO]] but that "AVGO should remain the majority TPU supplier over time, with ~80% share." Same number, same framing, now citable.** ⚠️ Cross-check the other way: Fubon's 07-28 CoWoS book has **MediaTek's allocation jumping 1.5% → 7.3% (17,400 → 137,700 wafers) in 2027 on the TPU program** while AVGO holds 22.3% — wafers and revenue share are not the same denominator, but the two houses are not modelling the same ramp. (Morgan Stanley "Playing the AI Infrastructure Dip", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Morgan_Stanley_700HK_Global_Thematics_Playing_the_AI_Infrastructure_Dip_Whe.html)
- **Bull (Pixel 11 5G-modem design win — a Smart-Edge/connectivity win vs QCOM, @dnystedt, 2026-07-15):** Google has **tapped MediaTek for the 5G modem in the upcoming Pixel 11**, edging out **Qualcomm and Samsung**, per supply-chain sources — MediaTek has been selling standalone 5G modems to gain modem-market influence. A discrete connectivity/Smart-Edge design win (separate from the Google-TPU ASIC story) that takes modem share directly from Qualcomm (negative read for [QCOM.md](QCOM.md)). (@dnystedt, 2026-07-15)
- **Bear (FY smartphone outlook cut again; TSMC allocation the ASIC swing, @dnystedt, 2026-07-14):** MediaTek has **lowered its full-year smartphone shipment outlook again**, per supply-chain checks, which also flag that **the amount of wafers TSMC can allocate MediaTek this year is the key variable for its ASIC business** — corroborating both the handset-cyclicality bear and the CoWoS/N-node-allocation dependency already on the page (Susquehanna's −11.5% handset read; Fubon's wafer-priority caveat). (@dnystedt, 2026-07-14)
- **Bull (MS Asia checks):** Morgan Stanley's Charlie Chan is constructive on the TPU ramp — MediaTek is **"on time to enter mass production this year"** with **~2.5m units of TPU production next year**, MS is "still very confident on MediaTek's TPU market share," and the supply chain shows MediaTek's TPU supporting **HBM3E + T-glass**; importantly MS is "not seeing MediaTek cutting their TPU production" (MS "Memory/Asia semis," 2026-04-14).
- **Bear:** Extreme **single-customer concentration** — TPU is "pretty much the only project they have right now" (Gopal, 2026-06-10). UBS clarified that the **Anthropic 5-gigawatt build is "all Broadcom… doesn't include any MediaTek,"** trimming one speculated incremental MediaTek path (UBS "AVGO," 2026-06-04). Longer term, Google's **COT (customer-owned-tooling) insourcing** risk: as Google spends >USD 100bn on silicon it will want to own more of the design and may shift volume to lower-margin players like Alchip (Gopal, 2026-06-10). On V8 the MediaTek logic ASP is only ~USD 4.5K at ~40% logic margin with **no margin on HBM** — economics thinner than Broadcom's (Bernstein triangulates Broadcom at ~70% GM on logic/CoWoS, ~10% on HBM; 2025-05-05). Core handset cyclicality is real: China smartphone "nothing is improving," units still declining; risk of a post-Q3 "double dip" after the Oct/11-11 flagship build (Gopal, 2026-06-10). Auto is years behind Qualcomm (~USD 1bn by 2028, "negligible"); the NVIDIA N1X Windows-on-Arm chip likely low volume (Gopal, 2026-06-10). FY26 GM compresses (Q2 guide 43% vs FY25 47.5%).
- **Read-through (mobile 4nm demand cut + Google-TPU packaging candidate, JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08):** Gokul's TSMC preview flags that **mobile 4nm demand has been cut** — the mid-end of the portfolio, where **Android brands have pulled back given higher memory-cost sensitivity and demand sensitivity to price cuts** (TSMC reallocated that freed 4nm capacity to CPU/accelerators). A negative read for MediaTek's core smartphone-SoC volumes. On the DC side, the preview confirms **Google's next-gen TPU is packaged by Qualcomm or MediaTek** (on CoWoS, not SOIC) — reaffirming MediaTek as one of the two candidate packaging/design vendors for the next Google TPU generation. (JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08)
- **Read-through (smartphone 2nm shift + price hikes, Rothschild & Co TMT Daily / Rowan Adley, 2026-07-01):** MediaTek reportedly shifted smartphone chips to **2nm in 2026** (alongside Qualcomm) after 3nm capacity became crowded with AI chips — potentially driving another leg of smartphone price increases (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-01). Tape context: MediaTek shares **+9% in Asia trading** on 2026-06-30, a standout regional mover, on the Korea AI-plan news read-through (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily, market color, 2026-06-30).
- **Bear (Arm/CSS royalty dispute):** SoftBank claims **Arm is charging MediaTek full CSS (Compute Subsystems) royalties** — MediaTek says it doesn't use CSS (only one part of the IP), Arm counters that even partial use of the packaged CSS solution triggers the full licence, and MediaTek denies. Context: Armv9 royalties are ~2x v8 and CSS is ~2x v9, so an adverse outcome would be a step-up in Dimensity per-unit IP cost (Jefferies, 2025-12-17). Watch as a smartphone-SoC gross-margin overhang.

Robert Sanders/Ross Seymore, 2026-07-02). **Citi** (Laura Chen, 2026-07-06) prefers Mediatek most among TW design-service names, raising AI-ASIC revenue to **US$18bn'27 (from US$13.6bn) / US$40bn'28 (from US$18bn)** on higher dollar content (I/O + compute die; Note the wide dispersion in PT convention across desks (NT$1,410 for the older MS mobile-led coverage; NT$5,000 DB; NT$5,300 JPM; NT$6,000 Fubon; NT$6,500 UBS; NT$6,800 GS; NT$10,000 Macquarie Street-high) reflects both share-count/split conventions and how much of the DC-ASIC ramp each desk capitalizes — GS's and JPM's current PTs are simply two banks' independent views (not a contradiction) and both sit below Macquarie's Street-high. UBS separately names MediaTek a **"Key Call"** among its cloud AI supply-chain top picks (Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01). Twitter/channel chatter corroborates Google adopting **EMIB for MediaTek's TPU** (briefing 2026-05-11).

Consistent with the Fubon/JPM EMIB-T-yield gate already on the page. (BofA · Haas Liu, 2026-07-13) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_13_tsmc_baml_2q_preview_13_jul_26.html)

Reiterates **~US$2bn 2026 AI-ASIC revenue** (JPMe US$2.2bn, ~500k TPU v8t units); **RAISES its 2027 ASIC estimate +20%** (JPMe now ~**3.0mn v8t units** vs 2.5mn prior) and expects MediaTek to **guide the high end of its US$7-12bn 2027 range**. **TPU v9 "Humufish" 2028 revenue estimate RAISED to US$36bn (from US$25bn), ~3mn units (from ~2mn)** — the competing **"Pumafish" (US fabless) appears cancelled/delayed**; Intel **EMIB-T packaging yield ~60%+** (v9's primary packaging). **TPU v10 in bidding stage** (decision in a couple months), high chance MediaTek wins the larger share (Google moving toward semi-CoT). **New customer win likely 3Q26 — SpaceX most likely.** RAISED 2028 EPS +20%. (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-21)
- **🆕 Neutral/mixed (the Helio G99 is the industry's process yardstick — and the Dimensity gap over Huawei is 2.4-3.2x; SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14):** MediaTek turns up in an unusual place in the first SemiAnalysis STEEL teardown — **the MediaTek Helio G99, on TSMC N6, is the reference die SemiAnalysis tore down as the process baseline against SMIC N+3**. The setup is a deliberate apples-to-apples on *process* rather than product: the G99 is a **~29mm² budget SoC** ("built for budget smartphones," ~$100 handsets) versus the Kirin 9030's **~140mm², "roughly one-fifth the area"** — but "the underlying TSMC process technology, however, is directly comparable as a baseline for analyzing SMIC's," which is what made the whole comparison possible. Detail worth keeping for the cost-engineering story: **MediaTek used HD cells (240nm cell height) in the G99 "to minimize die size and thus cost. As an SoC for budget smartphones at ~$100, this is essential"** — and N6's density gains "came from DTCO instead of tighter pitches" (fin pitch measured 34nm), with M0/M2/M3 at a relaxed ~40nm on SADP, no quadruple patterning anywhere. Being the industry's chosen N6 reference die is a small reputational datapoint, not a P&L one. **The competitive number is the P&L-relevant part:** the **Dimensity 9500 is ~2.4-2.6x faster than Huawei's Maleoon 935 in 3DMark Wild Life Extreme and ~3.2x faster in Steel Nomad Light** (the older **Dimensity 9200 is roughly matched in WLE**), and MediaTek is named among the current flagship SoC vendors that the **Kirin 9030 Pro "trails far behind," with the efficiency gap wider still** — the Kirin 9030 Pro performing "similarly to three-year-old Android flagships." The mechanism is node access, i.e. structurally durable while controls hold: MediaTek builds on leading-edge TSMC (the **2nm Dimensity flagship lands end-Q3 FY26**, see Current state) against SMIC N+3 at N6-class density. Read for the page: the smartphone-SoC franchise that funds the DC-ASIC build-out is exposed to **China handset demand and OEM in-housing** (the standing bear), but not to Chinese *silicon* displacing Dimensity at the premium tier. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **🆕 Neutral (design-tool footprint + a named Cerebrus result; SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21):** two small MediaTek datapoints from the EDA primer, logged for completeness rather than as thesis movers. MediaTek is named among the **largest fabless EDA buyers** — the cohort spending **$80-150K per engineer annually** on tools, IP and verification (versus IDMs at $40-80K), where "EDA is their core technical infrastructure." And MediaTek is a **named Cerebrus AI customer result: 5% die-area reduction and 6%+ power reduction** using Cadence's AI-driven implementation (the same exhibit lists Renesas at 75% TNS improvement, Samsung SARC 4x productivity, Samsung India 8-11% PPA). The Cerebrus datapoint is the more interesting of the two here: it puts a number on the PPA MediaTek extracts from AI-driven implementation flows — relevant to the cost/PPA edge that underpins its **~50%-below-Broadcom per-die service fee** (UBS, 07-02) and to how a semi-COT design house delivers competitive silicon without Broadcom's margin structure. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **Bull (Fubon/Jefferies 2Q26 preview, Sherman Shang, 2026-07-22):** BUY, **PT NT$6,000** (~56% upside; unchanged). Flags MediaTek is **"potentially raising its ASIC market-share target to 15-20%"** on a Google TAM update; frames the recent share weakness as a re-entry point and sees another customer/project win possibly coming later. (Jefferies · Fubon Securities [Sherman Shang], 2026-07-22)
- **Bull/bear both sharpened this week — MS previews the revenue bottom while UBS attacks the packaging dependency (2026-07-27):** **MS (Charlie Chan) 'sees the revenue bottom as MediaTek transitions to Cloud AI ASIC revenue. He expects a STRONG TPU REVENUE RAMP STARTING IN 4Q26 TO >50% OF REVS BY END OF 2027. He also notes that smartphone weakness informs his estimate of 3Q26 REVS -5% Q/Q. Charlie acknowledges that [[AVGO]] should remain the MAJORITY GOOGL TPU SUPPLIER over time, but 20% REV SHARE IMPLIES $20B REV FOR MEDIATEK, ABOVE CURRENT GUIDE FOR $7-12B.'** That is the clearest statement yet of the asymmetry: MediaTek can lose the share argument and still beat its own guide by a wide margin. **Against it, UBS (Timothy Arcuri) attacks execution rather than share: he argues investors 'have become too negative on the Google situation' for AVGO because Google's decision to go with a CHIPLET DESIGN FOR v8t DE-RISKS AVGO'S RAMP VERSUS MEDIATEK, WHICH IS HEAVILY RELIANT ON [[INTC]]'S EMIB-T PROCESS — 'a process we think STILL SUFFERS FROM VERY LOW YIELDS AND IMMATURITY.'** Note the intra-UBS tension this creates: Arcuri (US) is using MediaTek's packaging risk to defend AVGO, while UBS's own Sunny Lin (Asia, 2026-07-02) models MediaTek's TPU unit share rising 9% → 38% in 2026/27E. **A third, timing-specific datapoint from the test side: MS's Shane Brett thinks [[TER]] 'may have opportunity via the MEDIATEK CHIP SET TO RAMP IN 4Q26' (versus [[ADVANTEST]]'s strong position via AVGO), and frames 'the MediaTek vs AVGO TPU debate [as] really about 2028 (so late 2027 ramp)' — with MS's Charlie Chan and Joe Moore holding differing views on the share split.** Also relevant: [[GOOG]] disclosed **$707bn of future guaranteed commitments** through 2030, which UBS reads as including TPU purchase commitments to both AVGO and MediaTek. (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan / Amir Amerian; UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-27)
- **🆕 Mixed, and the most precise EMIB-T read the page has — MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype has already been through EMIB-T trial production runs at ~90% packaging yield, and UBS's numbers reconcile the yield spread this page has been carrying (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri / Shingo Hirata / Jerry Su / Diana Chang, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership? — Intel's EMIB-T vs TSMC's CoPoS", FULL note, 2026-07-27).** The 07-26 bullet above captured UBS's stance ("we like MediaTek on its TPU opportunity and see the EMIB-T execution risks as manageable") from the preview; the full PDF supplies the evidence behind it. **Rating unchanged and confirmed against the Figure-1 valuation table: Buy, PT NT$6,500**, share price NT$3,750 priced 24 Jul 2026 (mkt cap US$192,527m; 2026/27E P/E 58.7x/20.7x, P/BV 15.2x/11.3x, ROE 25.7%/62.7%) — the same PT UBS/Sunny Lin has carried since 07-02, so nothing is superseded. **The execution datapoint that matters most here: UBS's ~90% EMIB-T packaging-yield figure is not a generic estimate — it is measured "based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype."** That is a dated confirmation that MediaTek's v9 silicon has already run through EMIB-T trial production, which is materially further along than "design intent," and it is the first source on this page to attach the packaging-yield number to MediaTek's own part. **The reconciliation, which retires part of the confusion on this page and on [[INTC]]: UBS gives four distinct figures — standard-EMIB *packaging* ">90%"; standard-EMIB *substrate* "close to 60%"; EMIB-T *substrate* "around 50% for the leading Japan suppliers"; EMIB-T *packaging* "about 90%" (the MediaTek prototype runs).** So Fubon's flat "60-70%" and UBS's "~50%" are *substrate* marks, while the 95%+ gate Google reportedly set and the 98-99% marks elsewhere are *packaging* marks. **The bar UBS states is the live gate: "for HPC products… packaging yields ideally should improve to the high 90% range, considering the substantial value of the compute chips and HBMs being integrated"** — i.e. ~90% today against a high-90s requirement, which is a narrower gap than "EMIB-T is not MP-ready" implies but is not closed. **Second, the substrate constraint is now a capacity question with a named owner and a named ceiling, and MediaTek is on the wrong side of it: Ibiden's ¥220.0bn Gama-plant investment (Feb-2026) puts EMIB-T mass production at late 2027 with a meaningful ramp in 2028 — but UBS's checks say "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity, increasing the likelihood of additional capex announcements as demand visibility improves."** Read for this page: v9 volume beyond the first tranche depends on **Ibiden's next capex decision**, not on MediaTek or even on Intel's yield — a supply dependency two steps removed from anything MediaTek controls, and one worth raising on the 7/31 call alongside the EMIB-T-yield question. UBS also notes **Intel will be selective early, "prioritizing internal products and Google TPU v9, which is being developed by MediaTek and set for mass production in 2028, to ensure a smooth ramp and yield improvement"** — MediaTek is first in the external queue, which cuts both ways (priority access, but also the program that absorbs the ramp risk). **Third, UBS's Google-TPU volume path on EMIB-T, net-new to this page and worth holding against the unit forecasts already here: 2.3mn packages (2028E) → 3.5mn (2029E) → 5.0mn (2030E)** in the base case, **2.3 / 4.5 / 6.5mn** in the upside case, at **EMIB-T content of US$2,175 / 2,393 / 2,632 per package**. ⚠️ **Read the unit basis carefully before comparing:** these are **packages, each carrying ~4 XPU dies** on UBS's own framing ("~24mn AI accelerate dies or ~6mn chipsets with four XPUs per package"), and the dollar figure is **Intel's packaging revenue per package, not MediaTek's ASIC content** — so it is not directly comparable to JPM's "~3mn Humufish units in 2028" or Fubon's US$15-16k/chip content, and should not be netted against them. (The associated revenue rows — Google TPU **US$5,003 / 8,374 / 13,159mn** base, **US$5,003 / 10,766 / 17,106mn** upside — are **Intel EMIB-T packaging revenue in US$mn, not TPU unit counts**; the 2030 base-case total of ~6.0mn chipsets is what reconciles to UBS's "~24mn accelerator dies at four XPUs per package.") **Fourth, and left unreconciled because UBS does not reconcile it: the note gives TWO different TPU v9 mass-production dates.** The manufacturing-challenges section reads **"slated for mass production in late 2027, with Google's TPU v9 expected to be its first major external product"**; the stock-implications section reads **"Google TPU v9, which is being developed by MediaTek and set for mass production in 2028."** Both verbatim, same document. For this page the gap is the difference between v9 revenue starting in late 2027 or in 2028 — worth putting to management on 7/31 alongside the yield question. **Fifth, a house-level flag: UBS is not speaking with one voice on EMIB-T on this date.** Sunny Lin (Asia) here calls the EMIB-T execution risks "manageable"; **Timothy Arcuri (US, "SemiBytes," same 2026-07-27 date) calls EMIB-T "a process we think still suffers from very low yields and immaturity"** and uses MediaTek's dependence on it as a reason to prefer [[AVGO]] — the tension already noted in the 07-27 bullet above, now confirmed as running through the same note-day rather than being a stale mark. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_adv_packaging.html)
- **🆕 ⚠️ THESIS DRIFT — and it is Fubon moving against itself on the single most important open question on this page: the v9 packaging path. Fubon now says "Intel's supply is a MUST by 2028." (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28.)** This page has carried Fubon as the **anchor of the EMIB-T bear case** in two places: **06-05** ("Intel EMIB-T is not yet MP-ready, so the V9 priority stays TSMC CoWoS-L — 'risky to bet on one side'"; EMIB yield 60-70% vs CoWoS 95%+) and **07-08** (Google asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to **95%+** before production, judged "very challenging to achieve by mid-2027," concluding Google would **"at last rely on TSMC's [2028 CoWoS] expansion plan"**). **The 07-28 note reverses the conclusion while keeping the caveat.** Verbatim: *"Based on our checks, **Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028**. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the **V9 generation with four compute dies**, suggesting that their **capacity consumption will more than double in 2028 vs. 2027**. Although we do not have the detailed allocation yet, **we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and Intel's supply is a must by 2028.**"* And on Intel itself: *"although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its **learning curve improvement**, we see **Intel becoming more positive in capacity expansion, which is positive to the Google supply chain**"* — with **EMIB capacity at 10-12k/month by end-2026 and a plan to double to 24-25k/month by end-2027**, and Intel's CFO flagging that a large portion of the announced capex increase is earmarked for advanced packaging such as EMIB-T. **What actually changed, stated precisely: the 07-08 position was that the TSMC fallback would absorb the demand; the 07-28 position is that the demand is too big for TSMC to absorb, so Intel is required regardless of the yield debate. The yield/learning-curve caveat is NOT withdrawn — it is now framed as a risk to the ramp rather than as a reason the ramp does not happen.** **Why this matters here rather than only on [[INTC]]:** it removes the last major house arguing MediaTek's v9 has a clean TSMC CoWoS escape hatch. That question is item (2) on MS's list for the **7/31 call** ("Intel EMIB-T production yield and whether there is a TSMC CoWoS backup plan"), and Fubon — the source of the "backup plan" thesis — has now effectively answered it in the negative on capacity grounds. **Read alongside the still-live counter-evidence rather than as a resolution: UBS's ~90% EMIB-T packaging yield on MediaTek's own v9 prototype against a high-90s HPC bar (07-27), UBS's Ibiden-capacity ceiling (Google TPU demand alone "could largely absorb the planned capacity"), and Arcuri's same-day "very low yields and immaturity." The design intent and now the capacity necessity are corroborated; the execution gate is not closed.** ⚠️ **One more Fubon watch item, net-new: "Intel's major supplier Ibiden's upcoming earnings call and potential capex increase in the next two years will be another highlight"** — the same Ibiden dependency UBS identified, now flagged by a second house as the thing to track. The **12-15mn TPU / 2028** figure also independently corroborates the same number relayed via Jefferies sales (Cristina Titu, 07-23) and the Jefferies/Fubon post-TSMC call (07-17), now from the primary. See [[INTC]], [[GOOG]], [[TSM]]. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)
- **🆕 🔴 THE PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT OF THE 08-03 FUBON CALL IS NOW ON DISK, AND IT MOVES THREE NUMBERS THIS PAGE CARRIES WHILE RETIRING THE "SHARE PEAKS IN 2028" FRAMING ALTOGETHER (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03).** ⚠️ **Transcript-quality caveat, flagged once for this page: this is a machine (ASR) transcript, so company names are garbled and are corrected inline on first use — `Fubon` (ASR: "Foobon") · `CoWoS` (ASR: "CoOS"/"COAS") · `SerDes` (ASR: "services") · `Broadcom` (ASR: "Borcom") · `Ibiden` (ASR: "Epident") · `ASE` (ASR: "ASC"/"ASP") · `SoIC` (ASR: "SOIC").** The Jefferies **desk relay** of this same call is already logged in `## Intra-quarter` (08-03) and is **not re-entered** — only what the primary transcript adds is recorded here.
  - ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT #1 — 2027 TPU VOLUME MOVES UP: Fubon now estimates 3.5-4mn units, superseding the 2.5-3mn 2027 "Zebrafish" volume it published on 06-05.** Verbatim, and the causal chain is the one this page has been arguing (allocation is the cause, volume/share the consequence): *"we have seen MediaTek is able to secure more CoWoS capacity in 2027. So we now estimate the volume to possibly reach 3.5 million to 4 million."* Old value moved to `## Changelog`.
  - ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT #2, AND IT IS THE BIGGEST THESIS CHANGE ON THIS PAGE TODAY — the 400G SerDes pull-forward supersedes Fubon's "share peaks in 2028 / gives V10 back to Broadcom" framing, and the OLD framing is stated in full here for the first time.** Prior view, verbatim: *"based on my previous supply chain check, I heard MediaTek's 400G SerDes will not be ready in the next two years… I also heard Google they plan to adopt 400G, 448G SerDes in their V10 chip. So if that is the case, that means MediaTek will probably lose some market share or their market share will be peaked in 2028"* — because **[[AVGO]]'s 400G is already ready.** New view: management's 2H27 readiness (given on the 07-31 call) is *"a huge surprise to us because that means they will be there in the TPU V10 in 2029,"* and *"Google will probably allocate more resource to MediaTek and help MediaTek to increase their market share even in 2029."* **So the share curve no longer peaks at V9 — it extends through V10.** Hedge retained rather than netted away: *"for the 400G SerDes details, right now I'm still double checking,"* plus *"more time to monitor the development in the next six to 12 months."* ⚠️ **Note this does NOT resolve the 400G-vs-448G generation-label inconsistency this page has carried since 07-31 — Fubon says 400G throughout; MS/BofA cite 448G from the prepared remarks.**
  - ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT #3 (minor, but log it) — the 2028 custom-ASIC share top end moves to 30-33%, from the 30-32% this page took off the Jefferies desk relay the same morning: "in 2027, MediaTek's market share will increase to 17 to 18%. And in 2028, it could potentially increase to 30 to 33%. But that does not include the second project."**
  - **THE 500k-Q4'26 SKEPTICISM, ANSWERED HEAD-ON — and the answer is that the bear case is HIS OWN, already retired.** The question put to him: US analysts are *"universally skeptical, with several pointed to data points from TSMC saying there is no way MediaTek does 500,000 TPU units this Q4."* Fubon: *"that was my concern like two, three months ago. I put out a note saying Google is doing a re-spin and that will probably cost some production push out. But later, based on our checks, the issue has already been fixed and it is already in production."* Management is *"super confident saying the TPU revenue will be there in Q4. They even raise the TPU revenue target in 2026."* His mark: *"MediaTek can book 2 million, even more than 2 million TPU revenue in 2026."* ⚠️ **Unit basis is ambiguous in the transcript and is deliberately NOT converted here: read against the company's own "more than US$2bn" 2026 ASIC guide and JPM's ~0.5mn v8 units, "2 million" is almost certainly US$2bn of revenue rather than 2mn chips. Carried verbatim with the ambiguity flagged.** **The useful part for the page is the mechanism: the delay bear that dominated May-June was a Google RE-SPIN, it is fixed, and the part is in production — which is the specific, dated rebuttal to the "still in the fab" line ([[AVGO]]'s Hock Tan, 07-07; Citi, 05-22).**
  - **ECONOMICS PER UNIT — the ASP step restated, and for the first time with a GROSS-MARGIN assumption attached. ASP: TPU V9 dollar content to MediaTek "will increase to around 15,000… that is a big increase compared to Zebrafish. I mean Zebrafish, the ASP is only around 4,000. So we will see three times, four times ASP increase in 2028"** (codename quoted as the transcript renders it; Fubon's own 06-05 marks were US$15-16k vs ~US$4.5k, so direction unchanged and the Zebrafish base a touch lower). **GM: against the questioner's stated 30-40% estimate for TPU V8, Fubon — "I would assume the gross margin for V9 will be a lot higher than V8… maybe 50% or even higher"** — the mechanism being that **on V8 MediaTek did only one IO die plus the production, while on V9 "they are even involved in the compute design."** ⚠️ **Flagged explicitly as an assumption, not a datapoint: "I don't have the actual number… the chip is not yet a tape out. I think it will be tape out by the end of this year." Hold it against JPM's ASIC GM ">40%" and MS's "GM DILUTIVE but OpM sizable and accretive" — a 50%+ V9 GM would sit ABOVE MediaTek's ~46% corporate GM and would BREAK the dilutive framing this page currently treats as settled. That makes tape-out (end-2026) the event that adjudicates it.** Schedule reconfirmed off the 07-31 call: **V8 in production Q4'26, V9 early 2028 ("I think Q128").**
  - **2028 DEMAND-SIDE ALLOCATION: "from the demand side I think Google will probably allocate 4 million to even 5 million for MediaTek."** ⚠️ **Basis guardrail, and Fubon states it twice himself: this is DEMAND, not production — "from the production side, I mean we should still closely monitor Intel's expansion, TSMC's expansion… the real production number, I think it is still too early to talk about." Do NOT compare 4-5mn against JPM's ~3.0mn v9 units or GS's ~3mn 2028 HomoFish units, which are supply-side forecasts. On 2028 allocation between MediaTek and Broadcom he is explicit that the checks do not exist yet: "in the supply chain, we don't we're not yet have the real data point for 2028 capacity allocation."**
  - **PACKAGING IS A GENUINE TWO-TRACK, and the official line and the supply-chain check are two DIFFERENT claims that should be carried separately.** Official: *"MediaTek now they officially say they will only use Intel, they will only use Intel… what the management is trying to tell the investor is they will use Intel's for their V9 production."* Fubon's check: *"from our supply chain check, that doesn't mean MediaTek is giving up TSMC's at all. They are still they still have two advanced packaging technologies in their in their pocket. I mean both Intel's and TSMC's."* **Corroborates the MS relay already on this page (08-01) that MediaTek has "back-up plans (e.g. TSMC CoWoS/CoPoS)" with "switching the technology might take time"** — and it is the honest reading of a public "only Intel" commitment. See [[INTC]], [[TSM]].
  - 🔴 **THE RISK IS NAMED, AND IT IS INTEL'S PRODUCTION — asked what could send the business back to Broadcom given elevated expectations: "I think Intel's production is the biggest risk… if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I think like I say, I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some potential downside for the numbers."** ⚠️ **Setup context supplied in the same exchange and worth keeping as the expectations bar: sell-side 2028 ASIC-revenue estimates now span US$35bn to US$52bn** (consistent with the marks on this page — JPM US$43.3bn, BofA US$45.8bn, GS US$52.5bn, Citi US$40bn). **So the top-line number the Street is underwriting hangs on a third party's packaging yield.** The watch Fubon sets: *"closely monitor Intel's expansion and production learning curve improvement in the next six months."* See [[INTC]].

But you know, I think everyone knows that the second customer, the second project is XAI or SpaceX. But currently, I'm not sure about the detailed spec of this chip."** ⚠️ **Record this as attribution-by-market-consensus, NOT as a check — he has no spec, offers two names as alternatives, and confirms it is NOT inside the 30-33% 2028 share number.** It sits alongside JPM's "we believe SpaceX" (08-02) and BofA's asserted xAI co-development with 4Q27 tape-out (07-26): **three houses, two names, zero disclosure.** Management's own framing (07-31), which Fubon is responding to: a meaningful compute-performance upgrade, in production simultaneously with TPU through 2028. See [[SPCX]].

So Hon Hai probably will be get the V8 rack system for TPU for 2027"** — i.e. **[[GOOG]] needs additional ODMs for L11 rack assembly**, with Arthur's read being *"very in line with MediaTek, whatever Broadcom say, Google is very aggressive"* for 2027-2028. Also from Arthur, as the counter-detail on the same ODM checks: **for 2027 the big ODMs (Hon Hai and "Weichuang" [ASR-garbled Taiwanese ODM]) see NO memory-spec downgrade in the AI-server BOM** — they have raised capex to fund buy-and-sell of GPUs instead. ## CoWoS allocation — the 2027 supply unlock (Jefferies, 2026-07-28)
**Jefferies materially raised MediaTek's 2027 CoWoS allocation and named it a top pick on the pullback.** In Sherman Shang's updated CoWoS model: **“Mediatek — biggest change here, where we now assume they will get 7.3% share allocation in 2027E (up from our previous assumption of 5.3%), or 138k wafers.”** The rationale given is **increased CoWoS allocation and higher total TPU shipment targets by [[GOOG]]** (Jefferies · Rankie Wong / Sherman Shang, 2026-07-28).

**Why this matters for the ASIC thesis on this page:** allocation has been the quiet gating factor behind the 2027 TPU revenue debate — the sell-side sits US$1-8bn above the company's own US$7-12bn guide, but the guide has been constrained as much by packaging supply as by demand. A **~38% relative increase in assumed 2027 allocation (5.3% → 7.3%)** loosens exactly that constraint. Note the composition of the same revision: **[[NVDA]] held flat at 50%, [[AVGO]] was TRIMMED to 22.3% (from 24.3%), and [[AMD]] rose to ~300k wafers (mostly via ASE)** — so MediaTek and AMD are the only two customers taking incremental 2027 allocation, and **MediaTek's gain comes partly at AVGO's expense**, which is the head-to-head that matters for the custom-ASIC share thesis.

**Positioning:** Jefferies is explicit that **MediaTek remains its top TPU pick and “we view the recent pullback as another opportunity to re-enter”** (Jefferies · William Beavington, Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-07-28) — published into a session where the SOX fell 4.5% and Taiwan fell 4.6%. The same desk is **more confident in the CPU, SPE and TPU supply chains than in the [[NVDA]] GPU chain**, where Rubin volumes were cut again to ~1mn units this year.

**✅ Confirmed against the primary PDF, and enriched — Fubon Research (Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang), "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates," 2026-07-28.** The Jefferies desk notes above are relays of this Fubon note; the numbers check out and are **not re-logged**. What the primary adds:
- **The full two-year series, not just the 2027 point: Fubon's Figure 5 has MediaTek at 0.0% (2023-25) → 1.5% (2026) → 7.3% (2027) of TSMC CoWoS = 0 → 17,400 → 137,700 wafers.** The 2026 base is the number worth holding — **17,400 wafers is a standing start**, so the 2027 step is roughly **8x in one year** off a base that did not exist two years ago. Against a total TSMC CoWoS pool going 675k → 1,170k → 1,890k wafers, MediaTek is going from a rounding error to the **fourth-largest customer** (behind NVDA 945,000, AVGO 421,800 and AWS 196,500, and ahead of AMD's 132,300 TSMC-CoWoS wafers).
- **Fubon's own causal statement, verbatim, which is stronger than the relay's phrasing: _"we see that MediaTek is able to get more capacity allocation in 2027, and therefore, we think it is possible that MediaTek could further raise its custom-ASIC market share in 2027."_** Note the direction of the logic — **allocation is the cause and share is the consequence**, which is exactly the framing this page has been arguing (supply, not demand, has been the gate on the 2027 guide).
- **Positioning, in Fubon's own words: _"For TPU, MediaTek is our top pick, and we view the recent pullback as another opportunity to re-enter"_** — and it sits inside a broader stance that is **short-term cautious on the [[NVDA]] supply chain** ("could potentially underperform due to Rubin's lower-than-expected volume in 2H26 and uncertain design change in the next generation rack design") **and more confident in the CPU, SPE and TPU chains**. ASE is its top pick for CPU on Vera/Venice exposure.
- ⚠️ **Basis guardrail: 137,700 is TSMC-only CoWoS.** Fubon quotes **total** allocations elsewhere in the same note on a different basis — **[[NVDA]] 1.03mn in 2027 vs 629k in 2026** (TSMC **+ SPIL**, the SPIL leg for Vera CPU) and **[[AMD]] 300k** (TSMC **+ ASE**, majority from ASE). No total-allocation figure is given for MediaTek, so **137,700 should not be compared against NVDA's 1.03mn or AMD's 300k** — only against the TSMC-CoWoS column.
- **Cross-page: [[AVGO]] is trimmed to 22.3% / 421,800 wafers in the same table, superseding Fubon's own 24–25% / ≈459.6k from 06-05** — so MediaTek's gain is, on Fubon's own numbers, partly at Broadcom's expense. That is now stated by the primary source rather than inferred from a desk relay. (Fubon, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴🔴 TPU v10 (ICEFISH) FINAL PROJECT AWARD — "IN THE NEXT SIX MONTHS", i.e. BY ~FEB 2027 (JPM Asia · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-08-31).** *"We expect the final project award in the next six months, which should clarify Mediatek's involvement and reduce the uncertainty currently priced into the stock."* [[GOOG]] *"appears to have approached ALL design-service vendors at the RFQ stage to propose a disaggregated, full CoT model."* ➜ **JPM's base case: MediaTek keeps SerDes IP + I/O die + packaging design + program management; GOOGL designs the compute die in-house; GUC or Alchip may take the compute-die back-end, with MediaTek receiving KGD. Falsifier for the bull case: an award that moves the I/O die or the packaging integration away from MediaTek. Watch also the 448G SerDes milestone that gates it.** ⚠️ **This is a DECISION date, not the 2029 shipment date this page carries — the two clocks stay separate.**
- **🆕🔴 THE SEPTEMBER MONTHLY REVENUE (published by 2026-10-10) IS NOW A SHARPER TEST THAN IT WAS.** JPM has raised the 2027 datacenter-ASIC line to **US$18bn, ABOVE the company's own US$12-16bn guide**, on **Zebrafish already in production at [[TSM]]** and **2026 revenue *"on track to reach $2bn+"***. ➜ **The Q4'26 monthly prints are the public falsifier for BOTH the guide and JPM's above-guide number, and the gap between them is now ~US$2-6bn of 2027 revenue.** (JPM Asia · Hariharan 08-31, building on BofA's 08-20 framing of the same disclosure.)
- **🆕 EMIB-T SUBSTRATE YIELD — THE NUMBER TO TRACK IS 60%, AND THE TARGET IS "IMPROVING TOWARD MASS PRODUCTION BY END-2027" (JPM Asia, 2026-08-31).** ABF substrate yield *"approaching 60%"* for Humufish (16 packages per 515×510 panel, 9+x reticle, 25+ embedded silicon bridges) vs **80-85% for Rubin at 5.5x reticle**; final package yield **90+%**. ✅ **Loss mechanism now stated: scrap is substrate and bridges, NOT logic or HBM dies — so the risk is cost, not catastrophic write-off. This materially softens the "#1 risk" framing this page has carried since 08-03, and Fubon's "~75% in production vs ~90% lab" mark should be re-read against JPM's explicit substrate-vs-package split rather than treated as a contradiction.**
- **🆕 WHETHER THE [[NVDA]] STAKE CARRIES ANY GOVERNANCE OR ROADMAP RIGHTS.** Neither the BofA note nor the NVIDIA release addresses it, and it is the most consequential unanswered question created by the 08-31 ECB: NVIDIA now holds ~90% of a US$3.9bn convertible in the integrator of its largest customer's in-house accelerator programme. **Watch the ECB terms filing and any Google commentary on supplier independence.**
- **🆕🔴 MEDIATEK'S MONTHLY REVENUE DISCLOSURE, LATE SEPTEMBER / EARLY OCTOBER 2026 — the cheapest and most public falsifier on this page.** Taiwan issuers must report monthly sales by the **10th of the following month**, so the September figure (published early October) is **BofA's named verification of whether the TPU V8 ramp is on schedule** (BofA Asia tech team call, 2026-08-20). ➜ **Tests the 2026 guide directly — "more than US$2bn" of ASIC revenue with production starting Q4'26 — with no broker check, no channel work and no management access required. Falsifier: monthly revenue that does not inflect into Q4 puts the V8 ramp, and therefore the 2027 US$12-16bn ASIC bridge, behind schedule.**
- **🆕 V10 VENDOR SELECTION, ~2028 (the decision window, NOT the 2029 shipment date).** BofA (2026-08-20) reports the live investor debate is whether **[[AMD]], [[AVGO]], Alchip or GUC penetrate the TPU supply chain "~two years out, i.e. at TPU V10"** — while MediaTek's own feedback is that it has been working with Google on the v10 **"triplet"** architecture since **2H2025** and **owns the INTEGRATION** (die-to-die stacking IP + 2.5D package layout) even where individual tiles are multi-sourced. ➜ **Watch for: confirmation of MediaTek's integrator seat, any named tile award to GUC/Alchip/AMD, and the 400G/448G SerDes readiness milestone in 2H27 that gates it. ⚠️ Do not read "~two years out" as moving the v10 shipment year — this page carries 2029 (DB/JPM/Fubon) and that stands.**
- **🔴 [[INTC]]'s EMIB production learning curve — the next SIX MONTHS, and Fubon has made it the single gating item on the TPU numbers (Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03).** *"We still need to closely monitor Intel's expansion and production learning curve improvement in the next six months."* The reason it is a catalyst rather than a background risk: **if Intel cannot enter production, MediaTek has to fall back to TSMC, which Fubon does not believe can support [[GOOG]]'s 12-15mn 2028 TPU target — so the numbers come down.** ⚠️ **The yield datapoint to track it against, and it is materially worse than the bull headline: Fubon says the widely-reported ~90% is a LAB figure and the production rate he hears is "only around 75%"** (see [[INTC]] for both marks and the lab-vs-production distinction).
- **🔴 Ibiden earnings call (2026-08-04, the day after the Fubon call) — "another important indicator for Intel's EMIB" (Fubon, 2026-08-03).** Fubon hears Ibiden (ASR: "Epident") *"is also very aggressive in expanding their substrate capacity to support Intel's production in the next two years,"* following Unimicron's positive capex commentary. This is the third house to flag the Ibiden capex decision as the gate on v9 volume beyond the first tranche (after UBS 07-27 and Fubon's own 07-28 note). See [[INTC]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **🔴 TPU V9 TAPE-OUT, expected END-2026 — the event that adjudicates the ASIC gross-margin question (Fubon, 2026-08-03).** Fubon's *assumption* is V9 GM at **"maybe 50% or even higher"** vs 30-40% on V8, on the grounds that MediaTek moves from IO-die-plus-production into compute design — but he is explicit that *"the chip is not yet a tape out… when the chip is tape out, we should have more detailed information."* ⚠️ **A 50%+ V9 GM would sit ABOVE the ~46% corporate GM and would break the "ASIC is GM-dilutive / OpM-accretive" framing this page currently treats as settled (MS 08-01, JPM 08-02). Tape-out is where that gets decided.**
- **🔴 Q2 FY26 earnings call — FRIDAY JULY 31, 2026 (date confirmed by MS, 2026-07-26). Three specific things to listen for, in order of how much they move the model:** **(1) the 2027 TPU revenue guide.** MS expects a raise **from US$7-12bn to US$12-15bn** (MS forecast ~US$15bn), on the arithmetic that a 20% revenue share of a US$100bn 2027 TPU TAM = US$20bn. BofA is at **US$13.3bn '27E**, GS at **US$20.3bn**, Citi/UBS at **US$18bn** — so the sell-side is already US$1-8bn above the top of the company's own guide, and the guide is the constraint, not the estimates. **(2) Intel EMIB-T production yield and whether there is a TSMC CoWoS backup plan for the 2nm TPU** — the single unresolved gating item on the v9 thesis, with UBS's checks at ~90-92% EMIB-T validation yield and ~50% substrate yield vs Fubon's flat "not yet MP-ready." **(3) 3Q26 guidance**, where both BofA (−6% q/q) and MS (down mid-single-digit q/q) sit **street-low against Consensus at +2% to +4% q/q** — i.e. the near-term guide is set up to disappoint even if the ASIC narrative improves. Also on the list: the second CSP customer, 1.4nm TPU v10 competition, and **Qualcomm's entry into ASIC design services** (MS, 2026-07-26).
- **⚠️ Unresolved on this page — the 2Q26 GM guide itself:** this page carries **43% ±1.5pt** (from the Q1 FY26 call, 2026-04-30) while BofA cites a **44.5-47.5%** guide and says the quarter came in at the upper half of it (BofAe 46.4%), and MS models **45-46%** for both 2Q and 3Q. A ~3pt discrepancy on a guided range. Settle it against the 7/31 call rather than picking a number.
- **Q2 FY26 print (late-July 2026)** — GM step-down to 43% guide; smartphone trajectory into H2.
- **Upcoming earnings call — potential raise of the custom-ASIC market-share target** (Fubon read off the TSMC 2Q26 CoWoS-shortage / EMIB-T commentary, 2026-07-16).
- **2nd US CSP ASIC win "coming soon"** (Macquarie, via @dnystedt 2026-06-30) — the named incremental catalyst behind the Street-high NT$10,000 PT; watch for confirmation and customer identity.
- **New ASIC customer announcement before end-2026** (Tesla / Microsoft / Meta order of likelihood per Gopal, 2026-06-10); **Meta TPU/MTIA-class vendor decision "in the next few months"** vs a US competitor (Jefferies, 2025-12-17). Fubon expects the **Meta Arke project to finalize late 3Q26** (Fubon, 2026-06-05).
- **Triggerfish / Humufish (v9) ramp** — Triggerfish ~USD 18K ASP, ~50% of v9 mix, extends MTK from training into inferencing via large SRAM (~250MB×4) + 5nm simulator; MTK framed as "the only 2nm project" at v9 (Jeff Pu / @sssjeffpu; @jukan05, 2026-06-30).
- **TPU V9 packaging path** — Fubon sees EMIB-T not MP-ready, V9 priority on TSMC **CoWoS-L**; V10 migrates to **400G SerDes** (a gating capability for MediaTek's further share gains) (Fubon, 2026-06-05).
- **V8 respin (A1/B0 silicon)** back ~now — A0 ships ~300–400K units, B0 carries the ~2.5–2.6M unit 2027 volume (Gopal, 2026-06-10).
- **V9 / V10 selection** — V10 RFQs out to Broadcom, MediaTek, Marvell; selection expected within ~3–4 months of the call (Gopal, 2026-06-10). JPM (2026-07-01) sees high probability MediaTek secures at least one **v10 "Icefish"** project (Broadcom backing another); v10 stays semi-CoT (not full CoT) and non-CPO; MediaTek's IP edge is 448G electrical SERDES, die-to-die interconnect, 3D packaging (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01).
- **Broadcom "Pumafish" cancellation/postponement** — supply-chain checks say the competing v9 project has been cancelled or postponed, opening a 2028-29 share-gain window for MediaTek's Humufish/Triggerfish; watch for a possible Broadcom "Sunfish v8i" variant launch and the 1-2 quarter MP-timing gap (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01).
- **"Merope" fast-inference SRAM chip (v9 family)** — likely awarded to Marvell; MediaTek in parallel RFQ talks with Tesla/Meta/Microsoft, best win odds at Tesla within 1-2 quarters, but no revenue before late 2028-2029 even if won (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01).
- **CoWoS allocation for 2026** — MediaTek's key 2026 TPU constraint is securing enough CoWoS; allocation is expected to loosen materially in 2027 (Jefferies, 2025-12-17).
- **Arm/CSS royalty resolution** — potential Dimensity IP-cost step-up if Arm prevails (Jefferies, 2025-12-17).
- **2nm Dimensity flagship launch** (phones by end-Q3 FY26); Oct/11-11 China flagship cycle.
- TSMC EMIB-vs-CoWoS-L decision on the TPU V9 path (Gokul/JPM, 2026-05-27, via [TSM.md](TSM.md)).

- **🆕🔴🔴 2026-11 — TPU v9 "HUMUFISH" TAPE-OUT, DESCRIBED AS "REMAINING ON TRACK" (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-09-14).** The first of UBS four named re-rating catalysts, and now also the falsification test for the @QQ_Timmy claim that a next-gen TPU ("Triggerfish", TPUv9i) may drop OPTICAL inter-chip interconnect for FULL ELECTRICAL switching. ⚠️ **The two codenames are not established to refer to the same part — do not merge them before the tape-out.**
- **🆕🔴 2026-10-26 AND ~JAN/FEB 2027 EARNINGS CALLS — UBS FLAGS BOTH AS VENUES FOR "POTENTIAL UPWARD REVISIONS TO GOOGLE TPU-RELATED GUIDANCE."** Between them: **Google system-level validation of v9 in H127**, giving visibility into v9 performance and 2028 deployment volumes during Q1-Q227.
## Risks
- **🔴 THE #1 NAMED RISK IS NOW SOMEONE ELSE'S FAB: "Intel's production is the biggest risk" (Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03).** Mechanism, verbatim: *"if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some potential downside for the numbers."* ⚠️ **This is a supply dependency two steps outside MediaTek's control (Intel EMIB yield → Ibiden/Unimicron substrate capacity) sitting under sell-side 2028 ASIC-revenue estimates that already span US$35bn to US$52bn — i.e. elevated expectations resting on a third party's learning curve. Fubon's own yield check is the bear input: ~90% is a LAB number, production is "only around 75%."** See [[INTC]], [[TSM]].
- **⚠️ The "will only use Intel" commitment is a public one, and switching back has a time cost.** Management's official line for V9 is Intel-only (Fubon, 2026-08-03); Fubon's supply-chain check says both paths are held "in their pocket," and MS's relay of the same call has TSMC CoWoS/CoPoS as a named back-up with the caveat that **"switching the technology might take time" (MS · Charlie Chan, 2026-08-01).** So the fallback exists but is not free, and it lands into a TSMC CoWoS pool that Fubon says is already too small for Google's 2028 target.
- Single-customer (Google TPU) concentration; one major program today.
- Google COT insourcing / shift to lower-margin ASIC vendors over time.
- HBM carries no margin; logic margin (~40%) below merchant-ASIC peers (Broadcom ~70% on logic/CoWoS).
- Arm/CSS royalty dispute — full-CSS-royalty claim could raise Dimensity per-unit IP cost.
- China handset weakness / unit declines; memory-cost-driven BOM inflation; post-flagship double-dip risk.
- TSMC wafer/CoWoS allocation dependency (N3/N2 shortage; ~600K-wafer supply-demand gap cited; 2026 TPU constrained by CoWoS).
- Geopolitical / export-control exposure on Chinese ASIC engagements (TSMC could pull support).
- Auto and NVIDIA N1X (Windows-on-Arm) likely small near-term; resources concentrated on DC ASIC.

It is NOT answered on the YIELD axis by any of them.**
