- ⚠️ **`Produto / TPU v9 Humufish` — HELD, with a BASIS FLAG that must travel with every v9 number on this page from today.** UBS's *">2m"* is a **SUBSTRATE** count. This page already carries **JPM ~3.0mn UNITS (2028, raised from ~2mn, 07-21)**, **JPM's US$38.0bn / 3.0mn at ASP US$12,667**, **GS ~3mn**, and **Fubon's 4-5mn 2028 DEMAND allocation** which Fubon himself twice said is not production. **Four figures, at least three bases (substrates · units · demand allocation). NONE is superseded and they must NOT be netted against each other.** **Naming, settled here once: UBS and MS write *"MediaTek's TPU v9"*, Nomura writes *"Google's next-generation TPU v9 (partnering with MediaTek)"* — same programme; **GOOGLE customer · MEDIATEK ASIC/design partner · INTEL packaging (EMIB-T) · TORAY TCB bonder**, codename HumuFish. Full map on [[GOOG]] and [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).**
- ⚠️ **NEW SINGLE-SUPPLIER DEPENDENCY, added to the risk side of the same row: **Toray is the leading TCB supplier and ASMPT only reaches qualification in EARLY 2027** (UBS) — the v9 packaging ramp runs on one bonder vendor until roughly a year before HVM. Not previously on this page.**

**🆕 Update 2026-08-17 — the packaging leg gained a third house and a number that cannot be used.** **Mizuho US (08-16) is now the third house dating MTK's EMIB-T volume to 2028** (with management, Nomura and MS) and the **first to claim MTK is the FIRST high-volume EMIB-T customer** — i.e. the anchor tenant of Intel's external packaging ramp rather than one of several, which is a real upgrade in positioning **if the ordering holds** (no basis given; [[AVGO]] and [[META]] are also EMIB-T evaluators on this wiki). It also puts **TPUv9 "potentially" on EMIB-T**, corroborating UBS's 07-24 check and management's own "we are more committed to EMIB-T." 🔴 **But the accompanying "good yields >97%" is unusable as printed: Mizuho never states the measurement level, and this page has already established that EMIB-T yield differs by ~1.6x between substrate (~50–60%) and chip-on-substrate (~90–92%). So >97% does NOT clear [[GOOG]]'s 95%+ gate and the v9 packaging risk on this page is UNCHANGED — all marks kept side by side rather than netted.** The genuinely additive number is on the output side: **CoPoS/EMIB-T unlocking "a 5–10x increase in ASIC outs" in 2028E vs CoWoS-L's 5.5x reticle today**, which widens the routes to the 2028 ASIC revenue line without requiring the unverified ~2.8x v9-vs-v8 ASP step to carry it. **No MTK rating, PT, guide or estimate moved. ⚠️ Router miss — the page was not proposed for this note and was recovered by hand.**

**🆕 Adendo 2026-08-25 — os NÚMEROS por trás da manchete de TPU v10 que esta página já carrega. O headline (MS · Charlie Chan mantendo a MediaTek como parceira primária de v10, e a Alchip dobrada para US$8bn em Trainium4) foi ingerido às 21h; o que segue é a aritmética da mesma nota, que o resumo não carregou** (Morgan Stanley Asia · Charlie Chan, Daniel Yen, Daisy Dai, Tiffany Yeh, Lucas Wang, Ethan Jia, Henry Zhao — *"AI Supply Chain: Google ASIC, who gets what for design services?"*, 2026-08-24; **OW reiterado**).
- 🔴 **A CURVA DE RECEITA DE TPU, E ELA É O ITEM MAIS LOAD-BEARING DESTA PÁGINA:** *"MediaTek TPU revenue could reach **US$70bn by 2029** even under a COT (customer-owned tooling) model."* As premissas de 2027 e 2028 ficam **INALTERADAS em US$13-15bn e US$43-45bn**; para 2029 a MS projeta **4mn unidades de TPU v9 + ~1mn de v10**. A tabela: **2027 = 3mn v8t (Zebrafish) × ASP US$4,5k = US$13,5bn**; **2028 = 1mn v8t + 3mn v9 (Humufish) × US$13k = US$43,5bn**; **2029 = 4mn v9 + 1mn v10 (Icefish) × US$18k = US$70bn**. ► **A linha que fecha a tese de concentração: TPU = 38% da receita total em 2027 (US$35bn) e 65% em 2028 (US$67bn).**
- 🔴 **O ASP DE v10 SOBE MESMO COM A MediaTek PERDENDO O COMPUTE DIE — e este é o ponto que a manchete não carrega:** *"even without recognizing the KGD/compute die revenue, our industry checks suggest MediaTek could still book **higher single chip ASP/revenue than TPU v9**, where we now expect the **ASP for MediaTek could be US$18k per TPU v10**"* — contra US$13k no v9. O mecanismo é físico: *"MediaTek's content per chip (ASP) can increase at TPU v10, given **larger die size of the I/O dies** vs. TPU v9."* ► **Cruza com a âncora de ~USD 18K que esta página já carrega de Jeff Pu para o "Triggerfish"** — duas derivações independentes chegando ao mesmo ASP.
- **O ESCOPO EXATO DO QUE A MediaTek FAZ NO v10, que é o que decide se o COT machuca ou não:** *"we believe MediaTek remains the **major integrator** of TPU v10 (IceFish) and its responsibility is **very similar to what it does for TPU v9** (HumuFish): 1) **I/O die design with its own SerDes IP**; 2) **chip packaging integration** — compute die KGD, I/O die, and HBM chips."* O Exhibit 1 desagrega a pilha: **Google fica com o compute die (KGD)**, os fornecedores de memória com o HBM, **a MediaTek com o Electrical I/O Die e o advanced packaging via Intel EMIB-T**, **Marvell ou outros com o Memory I/O**, e **GUC ou outros com 3D IC / CPO**.
- **AS TRÊS MUDANÇAS TÉCNICAS DO v10 QUE ABREM ESPAÇO PARA MAIS FORNECEDORES** (e portanto o motivo de o winner set alargar sem ser zero-soma): **(1) KGD para o compute die** — a Google passa a ser dona do design e do photomask, o que lhe dá flexibilidade para incluir outros serviços de design no backend desse die; **(2) packaging 3.5D**, que exige um vendor de IP de 3D IC; **(3) reticle maior** — *"the chip reticle size may further expand to **>12 reticles**, vs. currently around **9 reticles**."* ► **E é justamente o reticle maior que a MS usa para argumentar continuidade: *"we think TPU v10 continues to use Intel EMIB-T given the larger reticle size, while MediaTek's SerDes IP is also improving, creating some stickiness to MediaTek's design service."***
- **ALOCAÇÃO DE CoWoS, que dá a checagem física do ramp:** no corte por vendor de CoW da MS, a MediaTek sai de **40k wafers em 2026 (3% do total) para 180k em 2027 (7%)**, tudo em **CoWoS-S na TSMC** — contra NVIDIA 780k → 1.222k (56% → 45%), Broadcom 300k → 484k (22% → 18%) e AMD 130k → 530k (9% → 20%).
- 🔴 **A VALUATION, e é a única coisa que se aproxima de uma chamada de ação na nota:** *"We suggest to **accumulate MediaTek on its recent share price dip**. The stock trades at **12x 2028 EPS**."* ⚠ **Sem PT novo nesta nota** — os PTs revisados são de GUC e Alchip, não da MediaTek.
- ⚠️ **INCONSISTÊNCIA INTERNA REGISTRADA E NÃO RESOLVIDA (não afeta esta página, mas afeta como se lê a nota):** o sumário de primeira página e a caixa "What's Changed" dão o PT novo da **GUC como NT$6.288**, enquanto o corpo da nota diz **NT$6.388** em dois lugares distintos. Pela regra da casa (sumários de capa ficam uma revisão desatualizados; prefira a tabela/exhibit), a caixa "What's Changed" é a fonte mais forte — mas **os dois números estão no mesmo PDF e a GUC não é nome deste wiki, então registro a discrepância sem escolher.**
- ⚠️ **NÃO ADOTADO:** (a) nenhuma estimativa da casa foi movida — as premissas de TPU de 2027/2028 da MS estão explicitamente *"unchanged"*; (b) o *"12x 2028 EPS"* não foi convertido em EPS implícito nem cruzado com o NT$296,89 de FY28 do JPM que esta página carrega — bases e datas diferentes; (c) as unidades de TPU do Exhibit 3 (total >6.500k em 2028, com v10/v10a marcados "unknown") ficam fora do corpo porque a própria tabela não fecha com o texto para v10.

**🆕 06-04 (backfill de arquivo) — UM SEGUNDO EIXO ÓPTICO PARA A MediaTek QUE ESTA PÁGINA NÃO CARREGAVA, e ele vem com um hyperscaler junto** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "MicroLED: The Optical Technology All Three Memory Makers Are Watching", **2026-06-04**). ⚠️ **ARQUIVO TIER-2 — Substack coreano independente de semis, PAGO (~9k assinantes, lançado 2026-03-15). NÃO É BROKER: sem rating, sem price target, sem estimativa formal. Não supersede nenhum número desta página.** Em **17 de março de 2026**, dentro da mesma semana em que Marvell anunciou joint development de interconnect MicroLED com a Mojo Vision e a ams OSRAM mostrou protótipo próprio, *"**MediaTek unveiled a MicroLED-based AOC co-designed with Microsoft Research**"* — o **Active MicroLED Cable**, produto de co-design com o projeto **MOSAIC** da Microsoft Research. Arquitetura: *"the structure integrates **logic, MicroLED drivers, and TIAs on a single CMOS die**, with the **MicroLED array and PD array bonded directly on top**."* Performance reivindicada: *"**up to 50% lower power than existing VCSEL-based AOCs with copper-grade reliability**"*, com **400Gbps por fibra demonstrados na Computex 2026** e **comercialização mirando 2027**. ► **A leitura do autor é que o sujeito da notícia não é a MediaTek:** *"the heavy part of this announcement is not MediaTek but **Microsoft**. A hyperscaler's research organization looked at the **wide and slow** architecture as the solution to its own datacenter problems and co-designed a product with a merchant silicon vendor, commercialization schedule included. It is the **first public evidence that the field has crossed from technology chasing customers to customers pulling**"* fornecedores. ⚠️ **NÃO ADOTADO como driver de receita:** a TrendForce, no mesmo texto, projeta o mercado de **CPO baseado em MicroLED começando a se formar apenas em 2028 e chegando a US$850M em 2030** — pequeno demais para mover esta página. **Registro como opção de arquitetura e como evidência de relação direta com a Microsoft Research, não como linha de receita.** ⚠ **Nota de roteamento:** o router props este arquivo com MRVL(23)/MEDIATEK(17)/CRDO(11) e o título fala de memória — verifiquei, e as menções à MediaTek **são genuínas e nominais** (seção 4, *"The Companies Following Avicena: Marvell, MediaTek, Credo, OSRAM"*), não incidentais.

**Síntese do quarter:** three days into the window the guide raise is no longer the story — **the ASIC dollars are corroborated by every house that has published (JPM US$15.1bn, Nomura US$14bn, MS unchanged, all inside the US$12-16bn band), and the debate has migrated entirely into the SUPPLY CHAIN.** Two things changed with the 08-03 Fubon primary. **First, the bear case that had capped the story — MediaTek's 400G SerDes arriving too late, so share peaks at V9 in 2028 and V10 goes back to [[AVGO]] — has been WITHDRAWN BY THE HOUSE THAT MADE IT**, on a 2H27 readiness date one full year ahead of its own model; the share curve now extends into 2029, and the 2027/2028 share path is marked up to 17-18% / 30-33% (ex the second project) on 3.5-4mn 2027 units. **Second, and offsetting, the residual risk has been named and it is not MediaTek's: "Intel's production is the biggest risk," with Fubon's own yield check at ~75% in production against a ~90% figure he says is lab-only** — under a Street 2028 ASIC-revenue range of US$35-52bn. **Net: the demand and design debates are closing in MediaTek's favour; the entire remaining variance sits in [[INTC]]'s EMIB learning curve and the Ibiden/Unimicron substrate ramp behind it.** Watch order for the next six months: Intel's yield/expansion → Ibiden capex (call 08-04) → V9 tape-out (end-2026, which also adjudicates the 50%+ GM assumption) → second-customer confirmation.

**🆕🔴 Síntese — UPDATE 2026-08-20: the axis of the story moved again, and this time it moved AWAY from packaging risk and onto SHARE OF PROGRAMME.** For three weeks this window's debate has sat entirely in [[INTC]]'s EMIB learning curve. The BofA Asia call (08-20) reframes it: **MediaTek took up its volume for the Google TPU project *as a whole* for the first time and put it at "MORE THAN 50%" — against a consensus BofA says had it winning only *a socket*** — and named the v10 seat as **INTEGRATION of a "triplet" architecture (IO tiles + compute tiles + HBM, multi-sourceable tiles, MediaTek owning the die-to-die stacking IP and the 2.5D package layout), worked on with Google since 2H last year.** ➜ **Three consequences. ① The bull case stops depending on a share-of-TAM assumption and starts depending on a described architectural role, which is a better class of evidence and independently corroborates JPM's "only the compute-die back-end may go to GUC/Alchip" check from 08-02 — two houses, same structure. ② The volume base is now double-sourced on the same day: BofA at ~4-4.5mn 2026 → 8-9mn 2027 (sourced to MediaTek's own feedback from Google) and JPM at ~4-4.5mn upstream / ~3.5mn module with "basically double" next year — agreement on both the level and the doubling, but with a ~1mn upstream-vs-module measurement gap that every unit number on this page must now carry. ③ The remaining variance splits in two rather than sitting wholly with Intel: the packaging leg is unchanged, and a NEW basis question opens — >50% of an 8-9mn programme implies ~4-4.5mn+ MTK 2027 units against Fubon's 3.5-4mn, which is either an upgrade or a definitional mismatch and cannot be resolved from these sources.** ⚠️ **Nothing is superseded: the 15-20% accelerator-SAM guide, the 20-30% system-value share and the ~20% TPU-programme revenue share are all DIFFERENT BASES from the >50% volume figure and all stand side by side.** **Cheapest forward check now added: MediaTek's monthly revenue disclosure in late September / early October (Taiwan's 10th-of-month rule) is the public verification of the TPU V8 ramp.**

**🆕🔴🔴 Síntese — UPDATE 2026-09-01: the axis moved a THIRD time, and it is now an INTRA-HOUSE FACTUAL DISPUTE rather than a supply-chain one.** The 08-20 framing had the whole remaining variance sitting in [[INTC]]'s EMIB learning curve. That is still live, but it is no longer the biggest open item. **[[NVDA]] put US$3.5bn into the convertible and [[GOOG]] US$400m, five houses published inside 24 hours and every one stayed or went more positive — and yet MANAGEMENT ITSELF DECLINED TO RAISE THE 2027 ASIC TAM OR SHARE GUIDANCE, saying *"it would take time."* The Street is now marking share (UBS: high-20% → 40-45% die units; JPM: majority of TPUv8) at roughly 2-3x management's own ~15%-of-US$80bn goal, on a partnership that JPM says is **explicitly optional for MediaTek's ASIC clients and "unlikely to be used in TPU Super Pods by GOOG."** ** 🔴🔴 **And the resolving question is now embarrassingly concrete: J.P. Morgan's Asia and US analysts published contradictory FACTUAL claims about TPU v8t Zebrafish on the same morning — Gokul has MediaTek as GOOGL's primary v9 vendor, Harlan has GOOGL diversifying BECAUSE of *"struggles with the rollout of Mediatek's TPU v8t Zebrafish."* The firm printed *"we'll have to agree to disagree."* That is the first negative operational datapoint on v8t this page has carried, and it sits directly against the 08-27 four-axis EMIB validation.** **Net: the capital and the endorsements are unambiguous and dated; the OPERATIONAL premise underneath them is contested by two analysts at the same bank. Watch order for the next six months is unchanged in structure but re-ranked: v8t shipment/yield evidence → Intel EMIB yield/expansion → v9 tape-out (end-2026) → second-customer confirmation.** ⚠️ **The cheapest sceptical read is Jefferies': all three announced workstreams pre-date the deal (Computex 2025), so the CB is the only new fact — testable against MediaTek's own May-2025 release.**

**🆕🔴 Síntese — ADDENDUM 2026-09-01 (verbal, MS Asia memory webcast): the two-week de-rate gets a CAUSE, and it is confusion rather than a lost programme.** MS · Charlie Chan attributes the fortnight of underperformance to three datable inputs this page already carries — the **[[GOOG]]-[[MRVL]] collaboration announcement**, **SemiAnalysis writing about [[AMD]] being part of TPU**, and **Alchip and GUC saying they could be involved** — *"so I think it's kind of a created a lot of confusion and MediaTek stock over the past two weeks like kind of a going nowhere, kind of underperformed."* **Against that, he reiterates verbally what the 08-31 PULSE note said in print — *"MediaTek remains to be the major coordinator for TPU V10, ice fish"* — and for the first time SPLITS THE PARTNER ROSTER BY BLOCK: [[MRVL]] on the memory interface, Alchip / GUC on the backend 3D IC, with MediaTek assembling the blocks.** ⚠️🔴 **That roster creates a NEW, live contradiction the page must carry unresolved: JPM · Gokul (08-31) says the Google-Marvell agreement is *"MOSTLY FOR XPU ATTACH AND NON-TPU ACCELERATOR PROJECTS, which doesn't directly impact Mediatek's prospects."* Chan has Marvell inside the v10 part. Both keep MediaTek as integrator — they disagree on whether Marvell's Google work touches the TPU die at all, and the v10 block award (JPM: "in the next six months") is the observable.** ✅ **Two smaller items, both directionally positive: (1) the v9 gate is re-labelled — on Chan's frequent checks the constraint is mass-production SUPPLY and *"MediaTek's supply is kind of improving"*, which is a different variable from the [[INTC]] EMIB-T yield the 08-03/08-17 flow is arguing about, and both stay live; (2) the NVIDIA transaction is explained from the seller's side as a TIME-TO-MARKET trade (license the scale-up/scale-out IP so a customer that insists on owning its XPU can still get a rack quickly, worked through on SpaceX) — a *"win-win-win"* reading that lines up with JPM's *"OPTIONAL… newer hyperscalers or AI Labs"* and Bernstein's *"small/early ASIC customers"*. Three houses now describe the same tier-2 customer-acquisition mechanism.** ⚠️ **Nothing in this addendum supersedes anything: no unit forecast, ASP, share path, rating or PT moved, management's US$12-16bn 2027 guide stands, and the transcript's ASR-damaged reference to an *"AlphaBay [Alphabet] investment in MediaTek"* was NOT adopted as a fact — see the Capital row.**

The observable that settles it is the v10 block award JPM expects *"in the next six months."*** 🔴 **THE NVDA DEAL, EXPLAINED AS TIME-TO-MARKET AND SCORED A WIN-WIN-WIN.** NVIDIA's first preference is still to sell the whole package (accelerator + NVLink scale-up/scale-out + CPU), but *"since a year ago, NVIDIA also understand that some customers really wants to have their own XPU… they just want to have some kind of partial technology from NVIDIA"* — and then *"you need someone to help to convert or implement those NVIDIA's IP for scale up, scale out into the real chips."* His worked example is **SpaceX** (off-coverage; no page on this wiki): *"if SpaceX want to do their own server system, and they have their own XPU, it will take years to get to the… full rack system design. So, it's kind of a very important for time to market."* The three-way win: the customer owns its XPU; *"NVIDIA can win because though they can not sell the full package… those customers are still part of NVIDIA's ecosystem"*; and *"for MediaTek definitely there's also win. They got a very very strong IP partner to supply to their potential CSP customers."* ✅ **Independently consistent with the two scope limits already on this page — JPM's (*"NVLink Fusion… remains OPTIONAL for MediaTek's ASIC clients,"* aimed at *"newer hyperscalers or AI Labs"*) and Bernstein's (*"particularly useful for small/early ASIC customers"*). The cost exposure is real; the catastrophic exposure is not. ⚠️ Note the level discipline this page has insisted on all quarter is now satisfied by the source itself — 60% is SUBSTRATE, 90+% is FINAL PACKAGE, and they are stated separately. ✅✅ **AND THE TWO CROSS-READS THAT CLEAR NOISE OFF OTHER PAGES: *"the recent agreement between **Marvell and GOOG is MOSTLY FOR XPU ATTACH AND NON-TPU ACCELERATOR PROJECTS, which doesn't directly impact Mediatek's prospects**"*, and *"our research indicates that the **AMD engagement is likely to be mainly for CPUs, rather than accelerators**."* ➜ **Both corroborate JPM US (Sur) from an independent Asia check and both cut against the [[MRVL]]/[[AMD]] TPU-entry chatter. ⚠️ MS's same-weekend note explicitly treats *"reports around AMD involvement in TPU v10"* as live — logged as an open cross-house disagreement, not resolved.**
🔴🔴 **THE COT ECONOMICS, WHICH IS THE MOST IMPORTANT ANALYTICAL CONTRIBUTION IN THE NOTE AND THE STRONGEST DEFENCE OF MEDIATEK'S POSITION ANYONE HAS WRITTEN: the FIRST leg (AVGO all-integrated → semi-CoT at MediaTek, with HBM procurement moving back to GOOG) saves Google *"**~40-45% or up to $7.8k per chip** in the TPUv8 family - or **~$78bn in chip spend, assuming 10M unit TPU shipment per generation**."* But the SECOND leg (full CoT, third-party SerDes IP + design-service vendors) *"only results in **15-20% further savings**"* — *"~17% or up to **$2.6k per chip** or $26bn"* — and *"**we do not think such a move is realistic before 2030**, given **448G Serdes availability from third party vendors may lag by up to two years**."*** ➜ **The cost-out that took the socket from AVGO is ~75% harvested. What remains is small, and gated on SerDes IP MediaTek owns. ⚠️ Also: *"Unless GOOG is willing to settle for lower-end IP vs GPU competitors such as [[NVDA]] (for instance, sticking to merchant 224G Serdes for TPUv10, similar to **AWS approach for Trainium4**)"* — the Trainium4 comparison is the escape hatch and it is named.**
**AND THE HR ARGUMENT, WHICH IS THE PART THAT MAKES THE MOAT CREDIBLE: MediaTek's **OPEX per employee ~$250k**, vs **[[AVGO]] ~$450k** and **GUC/Alchip ~$150k** — *"Mediatek's OPEX cost differential from pure design-service vendors is **not that significant** and workforce mobility is likely high given the common location in Taiwan… **We haven't seen Mediatek lose significant market share in ANY vertical (Optical Disk drives, TVs, WiFi, feature phones or smartphones) to Taiwanese competition and aren't expecting this to happen in datacenter ASICs either.**"* Enabling condition stated honestly: Google's CoT push dates to **2020** and *"has taken until 2026 to realize results,"* achieved *"only because Mediatek was finally able to offer an IP suite… competitive with Broadcom, after significant investments in its IP team and **2-3 FAILED ATTEMPTS**."***
🔴 **THE LONG-DATED MODEL, AND ⚠️ IT IS EXPLICITLY NOT THE HOUSE FORECAST: ASIC revenue *"could rise to **$88bn by 2030**"*, TPU opportunity *"has the ability to reach **$80-100bn annual revenue run-rate by 2030E**"*, ASIC OP margins *"should reach **25-30% levels, if not higher**"*, and **EPS power *"could reach NT$750 levels by 2030, 2.5x our forecast EPS in 2028."*** **The table (labelled *"2029-30 based on TPU v10 scenario, NOT JPM official forecast"*): ASIC revenue US$mn **2,252 ('26) → 18,360 ('27) → 48,074 ('28) → 66,710 ('29) → 88,550 ('30)**; total TPU shipments **0.5mn → 4.1mn → 4.6mn → 5.1mn → 6.0mn**; by programme — **v8 Zebrafish 0.5/4.1/1.3/0/0mn at $4,250-4,500 ASP · v9 Humufish 3.3/5.0/2.0mn at $12,848-13,000 · v10 Icefish 0/0.1/4.0mn at $15,418-15,600**; corporate EPS NT$ **70 / 155 / 318 / — / 749**; TPU GM **40%/42%/40%/—/36%**.** ⚠️⚠️ **THREE GUARDS: (a) the 2029-30 columns are a SCENARIO the note itself disclaims as not the official forecast — do not carry NT$750 or $88bn as JPM's estimate; (b) the v9 ASP of ~$12.8-13.0k matches MS Asia's US$13k and JPM's own earlier US$12,667 — three independent derivations converge, so THAT number is well-supported; (c) the v10 ASP here is **US$15.4-15.6k**, BELOW the **US$18k** that MS Asia (08-24) and Jeff Pu both carry for v10/Triggerfish. **A ~15% spread on the single largest ASP in the 2030 model — flagged, not averaged.**
**VALUATION: *"The stock is currently pricing in **no growth beyond 2028** (trading at **12-13x 2028 PE**), which is quite bearish given further growth for TPUv9 in 2029 and a stronger position for TPUv10 in 2030"* — *"similar levels to when the company was ex-growth (post 5G cycle, post early smartphone cycle)."* Context for why the note exists: *"Mediatek stock has been under pressure in the past 1 month (**up 2% vs TWSE up 7%**), due to increased concerns about competitive positioning within the GOOG TPU foodchain, as more ASIC vendors (**GUC, Alchip, [[MRVL]]**) have confirmed engagements or contracts for custom chips with GOOG."*** (J.P. So the 137,700 number this page already carries from the 07-28 primary is now the FLOOR, not the estimate — and the same call says [[TSM]] is slowing SoIC to prioritise CoWoS (70k in 4Q28 vs 90k prior), i.e. the pool MediaTek draws from is being deliberately expanded.** **(2) NET-NEW AND THE BIGGEST NUMBER ON THIS PAGE TODAY — Fubon's custom-ASIC market-share path is put on the record: 17-18% in 2027 → 30-32% in 2028, and the 2028 figure "DOES NOT INCLUDE THE POTENTIAL SECOND AI ACCELERATOR PROJECT."** That is a near-doubling of share in one year with the second-customer optionality explicitly excluded — set it against the page's standing second-customer thread (JPM's "we believe SpaceX," confirmation before end-2026). **(3) On the v10 gating item, Fubon now quantifies how big the pull-forward was against its own model: "biggest surprise is mgmt mentioning 400G SerDeS READY IN 2H27, potentially for V10 — ONE YEAR EARLIER vs our expectations."** ⚠️ **The 2H27 date itself is not new to this page — it came off the 07-31 call and is already in the Changelog (with the unresolved 400G-vs-448G generation-label inconsistency). What is new is the baseline: Fubon is saying its prior model had this a full year later, which is why it reads as the single biggest surprise of the quarter to the analyst who covers the packaging chain. Given this page has carried 400G SerDes migration as the explicit gating capability for further share gains (Fubon, 2026-06-05), that is a direct de-risking of the v10 win.** **(4) The [[INTC]] EMIB-T dependency gets its capacity number: Intel EMIB capacity reaches 10-12k/month by end-2026 and "the plan is to DOUBLE its capacity to 24-25k per month by end-2027," which Fubon says is a NECESSITY for [[GOOG]]'s 12-15mn TPU 2028 target (v9 = 4 compute dies, capacity more than 2x vs 2027).** The desk's summary line: focus was **"MEDIATEK (+ve) + INTEL EMIB (+ve) + GOOGLE TPU (+ve) + NVIDIA (−ve)"** (William Beavington, Jefferies, 2026-08-03). (Jefferies/Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03) |

## Management commentary — evolution (last 4 quarters)

| Theme | Q2 FY25 (2025-07-30) | Q3 FY25 (2025-10-31) | Q4 FY25 (2026-02-04) | Q1 FY26 (2026-04-30) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | TWD 150.4bn, +18.1% y/y | TWD 142.1bn, −5.5% q/q (orders pulled to H1) | TWD 150.2bn, +8.8% y/y; FY25 record TWD 596bn | TWD 149.2bn, high end of guide; −2.7% y/y |
| Gross margin | 49.1%, but ~1.9pt one-off; ~47.2% underlying | 46.5%, −2.6pt q/q on mix + no one-off | 46.1% Q4; FY25 47.5% | 46.3%; Q2 guided to 43% ±1.5pt |
| Mobile / flagship SoC | Flagship ~USD 3bn on D9400; +40% y/y FY | D9500 stronger than expected; flagship +40% y/y | Mobile 59% of rev, +18% q/q; D9500/8500 | 49% of rev, −17% q/q; OEMs raised prices, H2 recovery |
| Smart Edge / connectivity | — | — | 37% of rev, FY +21%; Wi-Fi 7 3x, 5G modem 2x | 46% of rev, +23% q/q; guided double-digit % growth FY26 |
| Data-center ASIC | Multi-year cloud-ASIC opportunity reiterated | TAM ≥USD 50bn by 2028; target 10–15% share | >USD 1bn FY26, multiple bn 2027; TAM USD 50–70bn | ~USD 2bn in Q4 alone; 2027 TAM USD 70–80bn; OpM accretive |
| Forward message / guidance | 2nm tape-out begins Q3'25 | Q4 rev TWD 142.1–150.1bn; GM 46% ±1.5pt | FY26 mid-/high-single-digit USD; smartphone down on BOM | Q2 rev TWD 140.2–149.2bn; "AI megatrend" supports growth |

_Source: MEDIATEK earnings calls (dates above); management commentary, paraphrased._

Sections edited: **3 rows at the top of the `## Intra-quarter` full log**, **1 `Update 2026-09-06` block re-scoring 3 `Sinal vs gestão` rows** (`Supply / EMIB-T dependency`, `Demanda / ASIC 2027`, `Produto / TPU v9 Humufish`), this record.** ⚠️ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no unit forecast, ASP, share path, revenue line, guide, rating or PT on this page was replaced. UBS reiterates MediaTek as **"Key Call Buy"** and MS maintains **OW**, but neither restates a PT, so every house mark stands untouched (UBS NT$6,500 · GS NT$7,000 · JPM NT$5,300 · MS NT$5,588 · BofA NT$5,230 · Fubon NT$6,000 · Nomura NT$5,800 · Macquarie NT$10,000), as does management's US$12-16bn 2027 ASIC guide.** 🔴🔴 **THE TWO ANALYTICAL EVENTS. ① **THE PACKAGING SUPPLY LEG GETS ITS FIRST SUFFICIENCY CALL, FROM TWO HOUSES INDEPENDENTLY:** UBS is *"constructive on EMIB-T's supply supporting >2m substrate volume for MediaTek's TPU v9 demand in 2028"*; MS *"expect[s] MediaTek's TPU v9 (HumuFish) to get sufficient EMIB-T substrate supply in 2028"* — and Nomura supplies the arithmetic underneath: Toray die bonders are *"the critical tools for bridge die embedding at Intel's EMIB-T substrate partners,"* **~12-month lead time**, with the **move-in schedule MANAGED BY INTEL and planned shipments through end-2027E** — a tool envelope consistent with management's own HVM-early-2028 and inconsistent with a pull-in. **This answers the 08-03 Fubon residual (*"Intel's production is the biggest risk"*) on the SUPPLY axis only.** ② 🔴 **MANAGEMENT SAID THE CONSTRAINT OUT LOUD: MediaTek CEO Mr. Tsai, at the CEO Forum fireside, *"was asking for more capacity support from ASE, TSMC, and Unimicron"* — three names, three layers (back-end · front-end/CoWoS fallback · ABF substrate). Bullish on demand and a real nuance against the conversion assumption every share mark on this page rests on.** **NET-NEW: a single-supplier tool dependency the page did not carry — **Toray leads TCB and ASMPT only qualifies in EARLY 2027**, so the v9 packaging ramp runs on one bonder vendor until ~a year before HVM. Also net-new as context: UBS raises **industry CoWoS capacity end-2027 to 260kwpm from 250kwpm** with the entire raise at **ASE (50 → 60kwpm)**, TSMC held at 180kwpm and Amkor at 20kwpm — incremental capacity at one of the three suppliers Tsai named.** ⚠️⚠️ **CONTRADICTIONS / BASIS PROBLEMS LOGGED AND NOT RESOLVED: ① **THE EMIB-T YIELD LEVEL IS UNTOUCHED — none of today's three notes publishes one.** It still spans JPM ~60%+ (07-21) · Fubon ~75% in production vs ~90% *"lab only"* (08-03) · UBS ~90% on v9 prototype trial runs (07-27) · Mizuho >97% (08-16) · Unimicron *"not before 2027"*, against Google's own 95%+ precondition. Nomura adds the first PHYSICS-level candidate explanation rather than a number — **CTE mismatch between silicon and organic films** and **build-up layer cavity precision**, both substrate-side and both consistent with yield VARIANCE rather than a level — **logged as a hypothesis, NOT adopted.** ② **v9 2028 VOLUME BASIS: UBS's ">2m" is a SUBSTRATE count**, sitting alongside JPM's ~3.0mn UNITS (US$38.0bn at ASP US$12,667), GS's ~3mn and Fubon's 4-5mn DEMAND allocation (explicitly not production). Four figures, at least three bases — **NOT netted, none superseded.** ③ **PROGRAMME NAMING: UBS and MS write *"MediaTek's TPU v9"*, Nomura writes *"Google's next-generation TPU v9 (partnering with MediaTek)."* Same programme; roles now stated explicitly — **GOOGLE customer · MEDIATEK ASIC/design partner · INTEL advanced packaging (EMIB-T) · TORAY TCB bonder**, codename HumuFish. Reconciliation written up on [[GOOG]] and [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).** **DEDUP NOTE: the Susquehanna-relayed headline *"NVIDIA and MediaTek announce expanded collaboration; NVIDIA also investing $3.5bn in MediaTek convertible bonds"* is ALREADY on this page (09-05 row, plus the full 08-31 ECB decomposition) and was NOT re-entered.** Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[GOOG]], [[TSM]], [[AVGO]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).

Superseded position, retained: "based on my previous supply chain check, I heard MediaTek's 400G SerDes will not be ready in the next two years… I also heard Google they plan to adopt 400G, 448G SerDes in their V10 chip. So if that is the case, that means MediaTek will probably lose some market share or their market share will be peaked in 2028" — with [[AVGO]]'s 400G already ready. This page had carried the consequence of that view since 06-05 ("V10 migrates to 400G SerDes — a gating capability for MediaTek's further share gains") and via Redburn 06-23 ("the 400G switch timing (V9 vs V10) the swing factor… Broadcom is still the only 400G supplier"). New position: management's 2H27 readiness is "a huge surprise to us because that means they will be there in the TPU V10 in 2029," and "Google will probably allocate more resource to MediaTek and help MediaTek to increase their market share even in 2029." So the share curve NO LONGER PEAKS AT V9. The hedge is retained rather than dropped — Fubon is "still double checking" the SerDes details and wants six-to-twelve months of monitoring — and the 400G-vs-448G GENERATION LABEL remains unresolved (Fubon says 400G throughout; MS and BofA cite 448G off the prepared remarks).** **NET-NEW, in order of how much it moves the model: (1) the 500k-Q4'26 skepticism is answered from the source of the original bear — US analysts are "universally skeptical," citing TSMC datapoints; Fubon says that was HIS concern 2-3 months ago (he published a note flagging a [[GOOG]] RE-SPIN that would push production out), "the issue has already been fixed and it is already in production," management is "super confident" the Q4 TPU revenue lands and "even raise the TPU revenue target in 2026," and his own mark is "2 million, even more than 2 million TPU revenue in 2026" — ⚠️ unit basis ambiguous in the transcript and deliberately NOT converted: against the company's "more than US$2bn" guide and JPM's ~0.5mn units it is almost certainly US$2bn of revenue, not 2mn chips, and it is carried verbatim with the ambiguity flagged. (2) THE FIRST ASIC GROSS-MARGIN NUMBER FROM THIS ANALYST, and it cuts against a framing this page treats as settled: against the questioner's 30-40% GM estimate for TPU V8, Fubon assumes V9 GM "a lot higher… maybe 50% or even higher," because V8 was one IO die plus production while on V9 "they are even involved in the compute design." ⚠️ Explicitly an ASSUMPTION — "I don't have the actual number… the chip is not yet a tape out. I think it will be tape out by the end of this year" — and a 50%+ V9 GM would sit ABOVE MediaTek's ~46% corporate GM, which would BREAK the "GM-dilutive / OpM-accretive" shape MS (08-01) and JPM (08-02) had corroborated. Logged as the number to attack, with tape-out (end-2026) added to Catalysts as the adjudicating event. (3) 2028 DEMAND-SIDE ALLOCATION: "Google will probably allocate 4 million to even 5 million for MediaTek" — with a basis guardrail Fubon states himself twice: this is DEMAND, not production ("from the production side… the real production number, I think it is still too early to talk about"), so it must NOT be netted against JPM's ~3.0mn or GS's ~3mn 2028 v9 unit forecasts. He also states plainly that no 2028 allocation checks exist yet. (4) THE PACKAGING TWO-TRACK, as two SEPARATELY ATTRIBUTED claims rather than one: the official line is that MediaTek "will only use Intel" for V9, while Fubon's supply-chain check says "that doesn't mean MediaTek is giving up TSMC's at all. They still have two advanced packaging technologies in their pocket" — corroborating the MS "back-up plans (e.g. TSMC CoWoS/CoPoS) but switching the technology might take time" relay already on the page. (5) 🔴 THE RISK IS NAMED AND IT IS NOT MEDIATEK'S: "Intel's production is the biggest risk… if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some potential downside for the numbers" — against sell-side 2028 ASIC-revenue estimates now spanning US$35bn to US$52bn, i.e. elevated expectations resting on a third party's learning curve. Added to Risks and to Catalysts with Fubon's own watch window ("Intel's expansion and production learning curve improvement in the next six months") plus the Ibiden earnings call (08-04) as "another important indicator." ⚠️ The yield input behind it is logged in full on [[INTC]] and is the sharpest on this wiki: the reported ~90% "is just from the lab… I heard it is only around 75%" in production. (6) SECOND PROJECT — Fubon names it, unverified: "everyone knows that the second customer, the second project is XAI or SpaceX. But currently, I'm not sure about the detailed spec of this chip." Recorded as attribution-by-market-consensus rather than a check (two alternative names, no spec), and it is explicitly EXCLUDED from the 30-33% 2028 share number. Sits with JPM's "we believe SpaceX" and BofA's asserted xAI 4Q27 tape-out — three houses, two names, zero disclosure. (7) ODM/ASSEMBLY READ-THROUGH from Arthur's downstream checks, net-new to this page: Hon Hai currently shows a silicon controller for the majority of L11 TPU assembly, but "because his capacity extension speed, the learning curve is not so much. So Hon Hai probably will be get the V8 rack system for TPU for 2027" — [[GOOG]] needs additional ODMs for L11 rack assembly, "very in line with MediaTek, whatever Broadcom say, Google is very aggressive" for 2027-2028.** **ASR caveat flagged once on the page (in the Debate bullet): this is a machine transcript; garbled names corrected inline on first use, and the "Zebrafish" codename is quoted as the transcript renders it rather than normalised to this page's v8/v9 naming.** **Deliberately NOT asserted: no 2028 MediaTek-vs-Broadcom allocation split (Fubon says the checks do not exist), and the "2 million TPU revenue" unit basis is left ambiguous rather than guessed.** Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[TSM]], [[GOOG]], [[AVGO]], [[SPCX]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).

- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — Q2 FY26 PRINTED, and the ASIC guide was raised on both axes. This is the single most consequential update this page has taken.** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT — SUPERSEDED VALUES, retained here in full: (a) 2027 AI-accelerator SAM ~~US$70-80bn~~ → **US$80bn**; (b) MediaTek's 2027 share target ~~10-15%~~ → **15-20%**; (c) implied 2027 ASIC revenue ~~US$7-12bn~~ → **US$12-16bn**; (d) 2026 ASIC revenue ~~"exceed US$1bn in FY26, ~US$2bn in Q4 FY26 alone"~~ → **"more than US$2bn" for 2026**, production from Q4'26.** The raise landed at the **top** of the bull range: Fubon's 15-20% call (07-30) and MS's expected raise to US$12-15bn (Chan, 07-26) were both vindicated — logged as a **win for the variant view this page has been carrying since June.** ⚠️ **A SLIP also logged: TPU v9 "Humufish" high-volume production now guided to EARLY 2028, vs JPM's prior model of late 2027** — management reframed it as a positive ("the first and second ASIC will be in production at the same year, driving significant share gains"), which is a real argument but does not undo the slip. **The EMIB-T debate moved materially and in MediaTek's favour: asked directly, management said "Yes this is true, we are more committed to EMIB-T" — the strongest first-party EMIB-T endorsement anywhere on this wiki, and it resolves in the affirmative the question MS listed as item (2) into the call. Counterweight logged the same day: Unimicron says Intel EMIB-T will not reach full mass production before 2027 (@jukan05, 07-31).** ⚠️ **BASIS GUARDRAIL ADDED, and it is the most reusable thing here: the US$80bn SAM is ACCELERATOR-ONLY — it excludes HBM and network switch. Management confirmed Google buys the HBM directly, not MediaTek, and Jefferies' checks say saving that HBM margin is "one of the key reasons Google is moving more to MTK." MediaTek ASIC dollars therefore must NOT be compared gross against an AVGO figure that may embed HBM pass-through.** Genuine surprise: **400G SerDes ready 2H27** (Fubon: "a huge surprise"), timed for TPU v10 in 2029 — ⚠️ **with a sourcing inconsistency flagged rather than resolved: prepared remarks referenced 448G (BofA cites 448G) while the Q&A said 400G (Fubon/JPM); the generation label is unsettled.** Also net-new: co-packaged copper today → CPO via TSMC COUPE later, an own-IP rack design including **NVLink Fusion**, and the explicit admission that **"beyond 400G SerDes will need to move to optical."** Pricing precision worth keeping: the hikes are **defensive** — "we are not increasing price to increase GM… we are just looking to maintain GM." **PTs: GS RAISED NT$6,800 → NT$7,000; JPM NT$5,300, BofA NT$5,230, Fubon NT$6,000 all held. JPM says consensus FY27 EPS should rise 5-10%.** BofA now asserts co-development with **xAI, tape-out 4Q27**, plus Meta and Toyota/Denso optionality — logged as an assertion, **not** confirmed: management said only "we have several, we will provide details when we can," and JPM independently guesses SpaceX. Per this page's standing convention, all of the above went into `## Current state` + this record rather than an `## Intra-quarter` table (the page still has none). - **2026-07-28 (/run-inbox — the Fubon PRIMARY PDF)** — **1 Debate/thesis bullet (this page's no-Intra-quarter convention), the existing `## CoWoS allocation` section enriched in place, 1 Sources entry, this record. NO rating or PT superseded** (Fubon Buy / NT$6,000 from 06-05, reiterated 07-22, stands; Macquarie NT$10,000, GS NT$6,800, UBS NT$6,500, MS NT$5,588, JPM NT$5,300, BofA NT$5,230, DB NT$5,000 all unaffected). **Dedup note recorded first: the `/wiki-ingest` entry below already logged this note via the Jefferies desk relays (Rankie Wong / Sherman Shang and William Beavington, both 07-28). The 7.3% / ~138k / top-pick-on-the-pullback material is CONFIRMED verbatim against the primary and was NOT re-entered — only net-new content was added and the section was marked as confirmed.** **Net-new from the primary: the full series rather than the single 2027 point — MediaTek at 0.0% (2023-25) → 1.5% (2026) → 7.3% (2027) of TSMC CoWoS = 0 → 17,400 → 137,700 wafers. The 2026 base is the useful part: 17,400 wafers is effectively a standing start, so 2027 is a ~8x step, and it moves MediaTek to the fourth-largest TSMC CoWoS customer (behind NVDA 945,000, AVGO 421,800, AWS 196,500; ahead of AMD's 132,300).** Also net-new: **Fubon's causal ordering, verbatim — "MediaTek is able to get more capacity allocation in 2027, and therefore, we think it is possible that MediaTek could further raise its custom-ASIC market share in 2027." Allocation is the cause, share the consequence, which is the framing this page has been arguing (supply, not demand, is the gate on the 2027 guide).** ⚠️ **Basis guardrail added: 137,700 is TSMC-only CoWoS; Fubon's "total allocation" figures for other names in the same note are on a different basis (NVDA 1.03mn = TSMC + SPIL; AMD 300k = TSMC + ASE) and no total-allocation figure is given for MediaTek — so 137,700 must not be compared against those.** **⚠️ THESIS DRIFT LOGGED, and it is the material item of this run — Fubon has moved against its own prior position on the v9 packaging path.** **Superseded position, retained here in full: Fubon (2026-07-08) reported that Google initially planned Intel EMIB-T for TPU V9 in late-2027/2028 but asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to 95%+ before production, judged that "very challenging to achieve by mid-2027," and concluded Google would "at last rely on TSMC's [2028 CoWoS] expansion plan." Fubon (2026-06-05) had similarly held that "Intel EMIB-T is not yet MP-ready, so the V9 priority stays TSMC CoWoS-L" on a 60-70% EMIB yield vs CoWoS 95%+.** **New position (2026-07-28), verbatim: "Based on our checks, Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with four compute dies, suggesting that their capacity consumption will more than double in 2028 vs. 2027. Although we do not have the detailed allocation yet, we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and INTEL'S SUPPLY IS A MUST BY 2028." Plus: "although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement, we see Intel becoming more positive in capacity expansion, which is positive to the Google supply chain" — with EMIB capacity at 10-12k/month by end-2026 and a plan to double to 24-25k/month by end-2027, and Intel's CFO earmarking a large portion of the announced capex increase for advanced packaging such as EMIB-T.** **What changed, precisely, and it is not a yield capitulation: the 07-08 argument was that the TSMC fallback would absorb Google's demand; the 07-28 argument is that the demand is too large for TSMC to absorb, so Intel is required regardless of where the yield debate lands. The learning-curve caveat is explicitly retained — it is reclassified from "reason the ramp doesn't happen" to "risk to the ramp."** **Why it matters on this page: it removes the last major house arguing MediaTek's v9 has a clean TSMC CoWoS escape hatch — which is item (2) on MS's list of questions for the 7/31 call ("Intel EMIB-T production yield and whether there is a TSMC CoWoS backup plan"). The source of the backup-plan thesis has now answered it in the negative, on capacity rather than yield grounds. Logged WITHOUT declaring the debate resolved: UBS's ~90% EMIB-T packaging yield on MediaTek's own v9 prototype against a high-90s HPC bar, the Ibiden capacity ceiling, and Arcuri's same-day "very low yields and immaturity" all stand.** New watch item, corroborating UBS from a second house: **"Intel's major supplier Ibiden's upcoming earnings call and potential capex increase in the next two years will be another highlight."** The **12-15mn TPU / 2028** figure now also has primary-source attribution (previously on the page only via Jefferies sales, 07-23, and the Jefferies/Fubon post-TSMC call, 07-17). **Housekeeping flag for the orchestrator: this page still has NO `## Intra-quarter` section, so all datapoints were folded into Debate/thesis + the existing CoWoS-allocation section + Sources, per the page's standing convention — not scaffolded here.** Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[GOOG]], [[TSM]], [[AVGO]], [[AMD]], [[NVDA]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).
- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook + Twitter). New section added: `## CoWoS allocation — the 2027 supply unlock`. No rating/PT superseded.** **The substantive change is a materially higher 2027 CoWoS allocation: Jefferies raises MediaTek's assumed share to 7.3% (from 5.3%), or ~138k wafers** — described as “the biggest change” in its updated CoWoS model — on **increased allocation plus higher total TPU shipment targets by [[GOOG]]**. **This bears directly on the 7/31 call's #1 watch item (the 2027 TPU revenue guide).** The page already notes the sell-side sits **US$1-8bn above the company's own US$7-12bn guide** (BofA US$13.3bn, MS ~US$15bn, Citi/UBS US$18bn, GS US$20.3bn) and that **the guide, not the estimates, is the constraint** — a ~38% relative increase in assumed packaging allocation loosens precisely that constraint. **The composition of the revision is the more interesting part: [[NVDA]] held flat at 50%, [[AMD]] rose to ~300k wafers (mostly via ASE), and [[AVGO]] was TRIMMED to 22.3% from 24.3%** — so MediaTek and AMD are the only two customers taking incremental 2027 allocation, and **MediaTek's gain comes partly at AVGO's expense**, which is the direct head-to-head for the custom-ASIC share thesis. Positioning: **Jefferies keeps MediaTek as its top TPU pick and calls the pullback “another opportunity to re-enter,”** published into a session with the SOX -4.5% and Taiwan -4.6%; the same desk is **more confident in the CPU/SPE/TPU chains than in the [[NVDA]] GPU chain**, where Rubin was cut again to ~1mn units this year. ⚠️ Note this is a broker capacity-model assumption, **not a company or [[TSM]] disclosure** — treat as a Street estimate revision, and test it against the 7/31 guide. | 2026-07-27 (/run-inbox) | **The FULL UBS advanced-packaging note (primary PDF). 1 Debate/thesis bullet (this page's no-Intra-quarter convention), 1 Sources link. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — UBS Buy / PT NT$6,500 is confirmed unchanged against the note's own Figure-1 valuation table** (px NT$3,750 priced 24 Jul 2026; mkt cap US$192,527m; 2026/27E P/E 58.7x/20.7x, P/BV 15.2x/11.3x, ROE 25.7%/62.7%), i.e. the same PT Sunny Lin has carried since 07-02; no other PT, rating or estimate touched (BofA NT$5,230, MS NT$5,588, GS NT$6,800, JPM NT$5,300, Fubon NT$6,000, DB NT$5,000, Macquarie NT$10,000 all stand). **Dedup note: the 07-26 Debate bullet already on this page covers the SAME UBS note via the Outlook preview — the "we like MediaTek on its TPU opportunity and see the EMIB-T execution risks as manageable" stance, the ~90-92% validation / ~50% substrate yields and the H2'27-28 MP timing are confirmed verbatim against the PDF and were NOT re-entered. Only net-new was added.** **The single best addition is an execution fact, not an opinion: UBS's ~90% EMIB-T packaging-yield figure is measured "based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype" — so MediaTek's v9 silicon has already been through EMIB-T trial production. That is meaningfully further along than "design intent," and it is the first source on this page to attach the packaging-yield number to MediaTek's own part.** **Second, the full note reconciles the yield spread this page (and [[INTC]]) has been treating as contradictory: UBS gives FOUR distinct figures — standard-EMIB PACKAGING >90%, standard-EMIB SUBSTRATE ~60%, EMIB-T SUBSTRATE ~50% (leading Japan suppliers), EMIB-T PACKAGING ~90% (the MediaTek prototype runs). Fubon's flat "60-70%" and UBS's "~50%" are substrate marks; Google's 95%+ gate and the 98-99% marks are packaging marks. They were never measuring the same thing.** What stays genuinely open is the bar, stated by UBS as **"for HPC products… packaging yields ideally should improve to the high 90% range"** — ~90% today vs a high-90s requirement, a narrower gap than "not MP-ready" implies but not closed. **Third, a new supply dependency two steps removed from MediaTek's control and worth raising on the 7/31 call: Ibiden's ¥220.0bn Gama-plant investment (Feb-2026) puts EMIB-T MP at late 2027 with a meaningful 2028 ramp, but UBS's checks say "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity"** — so v9 volume beyond the first tranche hangs on **Ibiden's next capex decision**, not on Intel's yield. UBS also has Intel prioritizing **internal products plus Google TPU v9 "which is being developed by MediaTek and set for mass production in 2028"** — MediaTek is first in the external queue, which means priority access *and* absorbing the ramp risk. **Fourth, UBS's Google-TPU EMIB-T volume path, logged with an explicit unit-basis warning: 2.3mn packages (2028E) / 3.5mn (2029E) / 5.0mn (2030E) base case, 2.3 / 4.5 / 6.5mn upside, at US$2,175 / 2,393 / 2,632 of EMIB-T content per package. These are PACKAGES of ~4 XPU dies each and the dollars are INTEL's packaging revenue, not MediaTek ASIC content — do not net them against JPM's ~3mn Humufish units (2028) or Fubon's US$15-16k/chip.** Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[TSM]], [[GOOG]], [[AVGO]]. |
| 2026-07-27 (/wiki-ingest) | **/wiki-ingest (Outlook). 1 Debate bullet. Nothing superseded.** **The single most useful framing this run comes from MS (Charlie Chan) and it is an asymmetry, not a share call: he 'acknowledges that [[AVGO]] should remain the MAJORITY GOOGL TPU SUPPLIER over time, but 20% REV SHARE IMPLIES $20B REV FOR MEDIATEK, ABOVE CURRENT GUIDE FOR $7-12B.' MediaTek can lose the share debate outright and still beat its own guidance by a wide margin — which is the correct way to hold this page's central controversy.** Around it: Chan **'sees the revenue bottom as MediaTek transitions to Cloud AI ASIC revenue', with a STRONG TPU REVENUE RAMP STARTING IN 4Q26 REACHING >50% OF REVENUES BY END-2027**, while **smartphone weakness drives his 3Q26 estimate to -5% q/q** — so the mix shift and the cyclical trough are the same event. **The genuinely new risk, and it is technical rather than commercial: UBS (Arcuri) argues MediaTek's Google position is 'HEAVILY RELIANT ON [[INTC]]'S EMIB-T PROCESS — a process we think STILL SUFFERS FROM VERY LOW YIELDS AND IMMATURITY', and uses that to argue investors have become too negative on AVGO.** This page has carried the MediaTek share-gain thesis largely as a commercial story (HBM-in-BOM pricing, per-die fees ~50% below AVGO); a packaging-yield dependency on a third party is a different and less controllable failure mode. **Logged with the intra-UBS contradiction explicit rather than resolved: Arcuri (US) is using MediaTek's EMIB-T risk to defend AVGO at the same time UBS's own Sunny Lin (Asia, 07-02) models MediaTek TPU unit share rising 9% → 38% in 2026/27E.** Third datapoint, from the test side: **MS's Shane Brett thinks [[TER]] may get into TPU test 'via the MediaTek chip set to ramp in 4Q26' (against [[ADVANTEST]]'s AVGO-anchored position), and frames the MediaTek-vs-AVGO debate as 'really about 2028 (so late 2027 ramp)'** — with MS's own Chan and Joe Moore differing on the split. Context: **[[GOOG]] disclosed $707bn of future guaranteed commitments through 2030**, which UBS reads as including TPU purchase commitments to both suppliers. Cross-filed [[AVGO]], [[GOOG]], [[INTC]], [[TER]], [[ADVANTEST]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). |

However, AVGO should remain the majority TPU supplier over time, with ~80% revenue share, given higher ASP on higher gross margin"** — MediaTek closes the unit gap, Broadcom keeps the revenue. **⚠️ One discrepancy logged and deliberately NOT resolved: the 2Q26 gross-margin guide.** This page carries **43% ±1.5pt** from the Q1 call (2026-04-30); BofA cites a **44.5-47.5%** guide and says the quarter landed in its upper half (BofAe 46.4%); MS models **45-46%** for 2Q and 3Q. That is a ~3pt gap on a guided range — one of the two is stale or mistranscribed, and the 7/31 call settles it. Flagged in Catalysts. Also added: **UBS states "we like MediaTek on its TPU opportunity and see the EMIB-T execution risks as manageable,"** with its own checks putting **EMIB-T validation yields at ~90-92% and EMIB-T substrate yields possibly ~50% (vs >80% for HPC ABF), but Ibiden capacity expansion "could offset lower yields and support selected products like Google's TPU v9"** — a direct rebuttal to Fubon's "EMIB-T not yet MP-ready" bear, and the reason MS wants the CoWoS-backup question asked. New competitive vector logged: **Qualcomm entering ASIC design services.** Edits: 3 Debate bullets, 2 Catalysts bullets, this entry.
